Wrong basis for a dismissal, no outcome change. Minor; the [SCR] row 9a and the corrected 404 wording accepted.

### CIS-R1-11 (system; reviewer major; final minor; accepted)

- Location: docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:831 (Table 6.0a BQ25690 row), 5.3 lines 651-652 and 665-667, Table 6.0b HR4 status 'P / C'
- Claim: BQ25690: the ceiling is continuous and fails low; HR4 P / C. M7 is not listed among its conditional passes, while the BQ25798 M7 is conditional on EN_EXTILIM.
- Problem: [D690] sections 3 (M5, M7 rows) and 16 (line 736) record that EN_EXTILIM (REG0x14 bit 5) = 0 disables the ILIM_HIZ pin ceiling AND HIZ control through the pin (DS 6.3.8.1.2.1 p.26). One register write therefore removes both the only conversion-stopping inhibit (M5) and the hardware ceiling (M7/HR4), leaving /CE, which does not stop conversion; restoration needs the watchdog at 40 s minimum. REG0x14 also holds FORCE_VINDPM and EN_BAT_DETECT, which the firmware writes in the POR enable sequence and at mode changes, so a corrupted read-modify-write of REG0x14 is a plausible exposure path. The scoring grades the same EN_EXTILIM defeat as M7 'c' for the BQ25798 and the BQ25773 but as M7 'P' for the BQ25690. The target never states the combined single-write defeat of M5 and M7. Section 6.4 statement 5 then ranks the BQ25690 over the BQ25773 partly on its HR4 FC bound.
- Reviewer recommendation: Grade the BQ25690 M7 and HR4 as conditional (never-write plus readback of REG0x14, 40 s watchdog restore). State in 5.3 and in the succession rationale that one bit defeats both the HIZ inhibit and the ceiling. Re-check section 6.4 statement 5.
- Verdict: PARTLY_CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: CONFIRMED core: (1) [D690] dossier_BQ25690.md:107 (M5 row) states "EN_EXTILIM = 0 disables HIZ control through the pin (DS section 6.3.8.1.2.1 p.26)"; :109 (M7 row) grades M7 "**P pass**" while noting the same bit "can disable the pin: never-write plus readback"; :920 (review registry item 18) repeats "EN_EXTILIM = 0 also disables HIZ through the pin". (2) [D798] dossier_BQ25798.md:124 grades the BQ25798 M7 "**Conditional pass**" for the identical mechanism (REG14[1]); [D773] dossier_BQ25773:128 grades M7 "**c**" for EN_EXTILIM at two addresses. (3) [SCO] scoring.md:147-149 and target charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:830-832 carry M7 as a conditional pass for the BQ25798 and BQ25773 but omit M7 from the BQ25690 row (:831); HR4 status "P / C" at :854 and [SCO]:169; dossier HR4 "P / C" at [D690]:130. (4) The target never mentions EN_EXTILIM in connection with the BQ25690 (grep: EN_EXTILIM occurs at target :605, :830, :832, :928, :1093 only, none BQ25690); 5.3 risks (:683-689) name the HIZ entry level, the VRECHG condition and the grounded-pin fault direction but not the register defeat. (5) REG0x14 shared-byte exposure: [D690]:746-747 ("REG0x14 holds FORCE_VINDPM and EN_BAT_DETECT next to EN_EXTILIM and BATFET_CTRL ... every write to REG0x12, 0x13 and 0x14 is therefore a read-modify-write with a masked readback"); POR enable sequence writes EN_BAT_DETECT = 0 and FORCE_VINDPM = 1 ([D690]:226-227); watchdog resets FORCE_VINDPM -> 0 ([D690]:240), so REG0x14 is rewritten after every true POR and every watchdog expiry. (6) [D690]:736 lists the EN_EXTILIM corruption consequence as "Hardware ceiling disabled" only, omitting the HIZ-pin disable its own M5 row states. (7) Section 6.4 statement 5 (target :977-982) ranks the BQ25690 over the BQ25773 after T-5.HIZ on the 1.10 A FC bound without naming the single-bit combined defeat; the BQ25773 M5 is P with "no HIZ-related register can override a LOW pin" ([D773]:126), so the BQ25773 does not share the combined defeat. NOT CONFIRMED (finding premise overstated): (a) "at mode changes" — a mode change writes VINDPM and IINDPM ([D690]:726), not REG0x14; (b) "restoration needs the watchdog at 40 s minimum" — only on the host-absent path; the never-write readback the dossier prescribes rewrites the bit on the next poll pass, and with STOP_WD_CHG = 1 the 40 s expiry also sets EN_CHG = 0 ([D690]:242). Primary datasheet (SLUSFN4A) could not be fetched in this session (no fetch tool, no local copy); the DS 6.3.8.1.2.1 claim rests on [D690]'s two consistent citations.
- Quantification: Single corrupt REG0x14 bit 5 (EN_EXTILIM = 0), FC mode, IINDPM intact at the 900 mA setting: bus draw = IINDPM typ 0.855 A ([D690]:136) + 0.15 A aux = 1.005 A (<= 0.90 + 0.15 = 1.05 A at the setting) < 1.20 A arm drop, < 1.247 A P_MAX, < 1.4 A OC_FC. /CE still blocks charging (no register defeats it, [D690]:107), so HR3 is not opened by this single fault. Double fault (EN_EXTILIM = 0 AND IINDPM lost to its POR 3.3 A, [D690]:406): 3.3 + 0.15 = 3.45 A > 1.4 A OC_FC (firmware latch) and above the eFuse 2.0-2.5 A backstop; same class as the BQ25798 double fault (EN_EXTILIM REG14[1] = 0 plus IINDPM loss). Like-for-like HR4 FC row (register value lost, pin ceiling intact): BQ25690 0.950 + 0.15 = 1.10 A ([D690]:404) vs BQ25773 1.24 + 0.15 = 1.39 A ([D773]:553) — unchanged by this finding, so statement 5 reason 1 stands. Restoration latency: one readback poll pass (host present); 40 s minimum watchdog (host absent, [D690]:239-248). Score impact: S3 bands carry no defeatability term ([D798]:586, score kept 2 for the same defeat), so 48.3 is unchanged; condition 1 for the BQ25690 is already Open on M5, and the BQ25798 M7 is itself c, so an M7 c on the BQ25690 adds no condition-1 trigger (the symmetric reading already applied to the BQ25773 at target :832).
- Source: Working documents only (authority tier: [D690] section 3 M5/M7, section 5.3-5.4, section 14 table and shared-byte note; [D798] M5/M7 and section 16 S3 note; [D773] M5/M7 and section 11.4; [SCO] Tables 4-5; [CRIT] 3.5). Primary SLUSFN4A section 6.3.8.1.2.1 p.26 not re-read this session (unfetchable here); the DS claim is taken from [D690]'s two consistent citations and is marked as such.
- Severity rationale: Grading inconsistency and an unstated hazard, not a bound breach: a single corrupt EN_EXTILIM write leaves the bus draw at about 1.0-1.05 A (inside the 1.20 A arm drop) through the IINDPM register and leaves /CE blocking charge, so no kill path, back-feed or circular loop is reachable from one fault; the double fault is the same class the BQ25798 carries. The recommendation (keep BQ25798) and the shortlist order are unchanged: condition 1 for the BQ25690 is already Open on M5, the BQ25798 M7 is itself conditional, S3 carries no defeatability penalty, and the 1.10 A vs 1.39 A succession comparison is like-for-like. The real defect is that the target grades the same mechanism c for two parts and P for the third, and omits a documented single-bit defeat of both hardware layers from 5.3 and from the succession rationale — doc drift / superseded-as-current class.
- Minimal fix: (1) Table 6.0a (target :831), BQ25690 "Conditional passes" cell: insert "M7 (EN_EXTILIM REG0x14 bit 5; the same bit disables HIZ entry through ILIM_HIZ, [D690] section 3 M5)" after "M5 (...)". Condition-1 cell unchanged ("Open: M5 is B ..."). (2) Table 6.0b (target :853-854): change the BQ25690 HR4 FC cell "(P/C)" to "(C)" and the HR4 status cell "P / C" to "C (continuous pin ceiling; register-defeatable through EN_EXTILIM REG0x14 bit 5, never-write plus readback, as the baseline REG14[1])". (3) Section 5.3 "Principal risks" (after :688): add the bullet "- **Single-bit defeat.** EN_EXTILIM (REG0x14 bit 5) = 0 disables both the ILIM_HIZ ceiling and HIZ entry through the pin (DS section 6.3.8.1.2.1 p.26, [D690] section 3). The remaining limits are /CE (charge disable, converter running) and the IINDPM register (0.90 A in FC mode, about 1.05 A bus draw with the 0.15 A aux term, inside the 1.20 A arm drop). REG0x14 also holds FORCE_VINDPM and EN_BAT_DETECT, which the POR enable sequence writes, so every REG0x14 write is a masked read-modify-write with readback; a watchdog expiry (40 s minimum) restores the bit only if the host does not." (4) Section 6.4 statement 5 (target :981-982), extend the "However" sentence: "..., its watchdog is 80 times slower than the baseline setting, one register bit (EN_EXTILIM) removes both its HIZ inhibit and its ceiling, where the BQ25773 HIZ entry has no register override ([D773] section 3 M5), and it delivers 2.62 A ...". (5) Propagate to the working documents: [D690]:109 M7 "P pass" -> "Conditional pass (P mechanism; EN_EXTILIM defeat, never-write plus readback)"; [D690]:130 HR4 "P / C" -> "C"; [D690]:736 consequence -> "Hardware ceiling AND HIZ entry through the pin disabled"; [SCO]:148 and :169 as in (1)-(2). Proof: the corrected cells reproduce the grading already applied to the BQ25798 ([D798]:124) and BQ25773 ([D773]:128) for the same mechanism; the numbers (1.10 A, 48.3, condition-1 Open) are unchanged because the fix alters evidence class only, not any computed bound (S3 bands have no defeatability term, [D798]:586). Follow-on skill: none (document-consistency fix; no firmware, test, pinmap, protocol, coefficient or bench change).
- Adjacent findings: [D690]:736 (section 14 corruption table) records the EN_EXTILIM consequence as 'Hardware ceiling disabled' only, contradicting [D690]:107 and :920 which state the same bit also disables HIZ through the pin — internal dossier inconsistency.; Target 5.3 M5 bullet (:683-684) omits the register path to M5 (EN_EXTILIM = 0) that [D690]:107 lists as part of the M5 grade; the M5 condition is stated as the HIZ entry level only.; Finding premise 'REG0x14 written at mode changes' is unsupported: [D690]:726 records mode changes as VINDPM and IINDPM writes; REG0x14 is written in the POR enable sequence and after a watchdog expiry (FORCE_VINDPM reset, [D690]:240).; Primary SLUSFN4A section 6.3.8.1.2.1 p.26 is cited only through [D690]; no local copy exists in the repository and it was not fetchable here, so the HIZ-disable reading remains a working-document claim until a datasheet re-read.; BQ25798 M5 carries its own B on the EN_EXTILIM interaction with the < 0.75 V stop ([D798]:122, S1-B3); the target's Table 6.0a lists the BQ25798 M7 c but not this M5 B, so a strict reader of condition 1 ('where the BQ25798 has P') could argue the BQ25798 M5 is not clean P either — worth one sentence in 6.1.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): The single-fault bound is decisive: EN_EXTILIM = 0 alone leaves IINDPM at 0.90 A (bus draw about 1.00-1.05 A, inside the 1.20 A arm drop) and /CE still blocks charging, so no HR3 or kill path opens from one fault; the double fault (plus IINDPM lost to 3.3 A POR) is the same class the BQ25798 carries. The like-for-like HR4 FC comparison 1.10 A vs 1.39 A is unchanged, so succession reason 1 stands. The retained defect is grading the same mechanism c for two parts and P for the third and omitting the single-bit defeat of both hardware layers from 5.3. Minor; the five edits accepted, including the REG0x14 masked read-modify-write note.

### CIS-R1-12 (system,decision; reviewer major; final minor; accepted)

- Location: docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:771-801 (section 5.6, items 1-3); also :18-20 (evidence-base claim), 5.3 lines 676-679, Table 6.6 line 1017 (finding 8, BQ25690); scoring.md:243
- Claim: Under the always-powered design D-BC-RG (REGEN) is held closed through Run, so VCHG-IN stays at bus level through a purge and no part takes a POR or cold start per purge in Run; a cold re-entry occurs only outside Run. Table 6.6: BQ25690 finding 8 "Kept; 6-7 transactions after every true POR (a purge in Run causes none, section 5.6)". 0.0: "Every candidate fact in this document is taken from a working document."
- Problem: Item 2 of the same section cites the IO CSV row-31 guard, which reads 'BT_BUS_ENABLE and REGEN_ENABLE *must* be turned off before [FC_CHARGE_ENABLE] is turned on'. The firmware enforces it: FAULT_SWITCH_CONFLICT 0x0008 = 'FC_CHARGE_ENABLE asserted while BT_BUS or REGEN high' (teensy_controller.ino:2041), and assertFcChargeEnable() drives REGEN LOW (about line 12772). Under the authoritative CSV, REGEN is LOW for the whole of every FC window; at a pre-purge FC_CHARGE drop D-BC-FC is the only feed, VCHG-IN decays and every part pays the Table 5.2 cold re-entry in Run on every purge. REGEN can stay closed concurrently only under [EVAL] 0.1 ruling 7 ('IO CSV row-31 rule ... to be amended (pending documentation change, not yet made)'), which section 5.6 never mentions; the authority order puts the CSV above [EVAL]. Section 5.6 is a system assumption introduced by the target, replacing the correct [D690] 11.5 assumption, and no working document owns it: [SCO] Table 8 cites "the system assumption of the main document, section 5.6" for the same cell, so the two documents cite each other. It is load-bearing for the BQ25690 condition 3: under the dossier assumption the 6-7-transaction enable sequence recurs per purge, reopening finding 8 (major) under the [SCO] 2.4 first-order rule and failing condition 3. The baseline's own per-purge cost stays hidden: about 150 ms to REGN, the VAC_OVP re-write (POR 7 V) and the risk of the 7-minute poor-source lockout (C6) at every purge, which threatens HR2. The Table 6.6 BQ25690 entry ('a purge in Run causes none') is wrong under the CSV.
- Reviewer recommendation: State that section 5.6 depends on the pending ruling-7 amendment of CSV row 31 (and on [EVAL] ruling 3) and on a firmware change to FAULT_SWITCH_CONFLICT and assertFcChargeEnable(). Until both land, apply Table 5.2 per purge in Run to every part, and add the per-purge C6 lockout risk to the BQ25798 HR2 row and to reason 2 of section 0.1. Move the assumption into [SCO] section 2 as a normalisation rule with its [EVAL] citation, and add "condition 3 conditional on the ruling" to the BQ25690 cells in Tables 6.4 and 6.6.
- Verdict: PARTLY_CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: CONFIRMED premise. (a) IO CSV row 31 (`C:/Life Ops/School/Thesis/DC-Balancer-charger-search/references/Scale Car Teensy IO - IO.csv`:24): "BT_BUS_ENABLE and REGEN_ENABLE *must* be turned off before this is turned on". (b) Firmware enforces it: `teensy_controller/teensy_controller.ino`:2041 `#define FAULT_SWITCH_CONFLICT 0x0008 // FC_CHARGE_ENABLE asserted while BT_BUS or REGEN high`; the check at :7415-7417 (compiled under BENCH_TEST 0); `assertFcChargeEnable()` at :12743-12778 drives BT_BUS LOW (:12762) and REGEN LOW (:12772) 100 us before FC_CHARGE HIGH (:12774). (c) [EVAL] `docs/charger_replacement_evaluation_2026-11-05.md`:59-62, ruling 7: concurrent regen + FC charging, "IO CSV row-31 rule ... is to be amended (pending documentation change, not yet made)"; :618 "Under CSV row 31 regen and FC charging are mutually exclusive in time"; :151 "forbidden today by IO CSV row 31". (d) Target `docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md`:777-781: item 1 "REGEN (D-BC-RG) is held closed through Run" sits beside item 2 citing "IO CSV row-31 guard, `FAULT_SWITCH_CONFLICT`"; ruling 7 appears nowhere in the target (grep for "ruling 7|row-31|row 31" returns only :779-780). Under the CSV, REGEN is LOW for the whole of every FC window, so item 1 as worded is false inside FC windows and the mechanism that re-feeds VCHG-IN (REGEN re-close after FC_CHARGE drops) is never stated. (e) Circular evidence: `dossier_BQ25690.md`:621-623 now opens 11.5 with "(Superseded in part 2026-11-10 by the main document ... section 5.6 ...)" while `scoring.md`:243 cites "the system assumption of the main document, section 5.6"; the target :18-20 claims every fact comes from a working document. Partly refuting "no working document owns it": `dossier_LTC4015.md`:401-402 and `dossier_RAA489110...md`:356 both carry the always-powered assumption citing [EVAL] ruling 3 / 0.2, and [EVAL]:731-736 (section 4.4 transition table) is the owner of the handover sequence.

REFUTED conclusion. [EVAL]:735 "FC -> regen (brake edge)": "FC_CHARGE LOW; BT_BUS HIGH ... and REGEN HIGH in parallel ... ≈ 10-12 ms" and :734 "Regen -> FC": "REGEN LOW (no unplug: VCHG-IN 70 µF falls at ~40-70 V/s, far above VBUS_PRESENTZ ~3.2 V) ... FC_CHARGE HIGH (warm, <= 0.6 V droop over the typical-only tD_ON)"; :620-622 "FC windows open warm from the regen path (VCHG-IN droops <= 0.6 V over the 8 ms tD_ON)". So the [EVAL] design is already CSV-compliant (sequential) and treats both handovers as warm. `teensy_controller.ino`:4875 `const uint32_t RT1987_T_D_ON_MS = 8u;` (RT1987 DS §17.4/§17.6). Per-part draw and capacitance: BQ25798 IQ_VBUS 3-5 mA at 15 V, `dossier_BQ25798.md`:125, VBUS_PRESENTZ 3.1-3.3 V :297, 70.2 uF :463 / 25.8 uF eFuse baseline :465; BQ25690 IQ_VIN 250-700 uA from about 11 uF, VIN_OKZ 2.35 V max, `dossier_BQ25690.md`:631, :299, :424; LTC4015 5-20 mA, 40 uF, BATSENS + 100 mV, `dossier_LTC4015.md`:401-405; BQ25773 IQ_VAC 2.5 mA typ, VVBUS_CONVEN 3.1-3.7 V, `dossier_BQ25773...md`:129, :83-84. Current firmware: REGEN HIGH in Run only in the regen branch; :8324 is the State 99 teardown and :11457 the State-98 `J` session; the always-powered Run sequence is a design item, not implemented.
- Quantification: Pre-purge handover gap under the CSV: RT1987 turn-off (100 us firmware allowance, S0-M3 measured) + tD_ON 8 ms typ = about 8.1 ms; [EVAL] 4.4 budgets 10-12 ms for the whole FC -> regen transition. Charger inhibited before FC_CHARGE drops (5.6 and [D690] 11.5 both place the inhibit first), so only quiescent draw discharges VCHG-IN. Droop dV = I*t/C over 12 ms:
- BQ25798: 5 mA x 12 ms / 70.2 uF = 0.85 V -> VCHG-IN about 15.1 V (eFuse baseline 25.8 uF: 2.3 V -> 13.7 V); VBUS_PRESENTZ 3.1-3.3 V not reached, so no REGN 150 ms, no 30 ms poor-source check, no VAC_OVP re-write, no C6 7-minute lockout. Time to VBUS_PRESENTZ at 5 mA / 70 uF: (16 - 3.2) V x 70 uF / 5 mA = 179 ms.
- BQ25690: 0.25-0.7 mA x 12 ms / 11 uF = 0.27-0.76 V -> about 15.2-15.7 V; time to VIN_OKZ 2.35 V: (16 - 2.35) x 11 uF / 0.7 mA = 214 ms (600 ms at 0.25 mA). A true POR therefore needs the feed open longer than the whole 100-200 ms purge, i.e. REGEN never re-closed, which contradicts ruling 3. The 6-7-transaction enable sequence does not recur per purge under the CSV with the handover.
- BQ25773: 2.5 mA x 12 ms / 20 uF (motherboard C-D8/C-D10 alone) = 1.5 V -> 14.5 V, above VVBUS_CONVEN 3.1-3.7 V.
- LTC4015: 5-20 mA x 12 ms / 40 uF = 1.5-6.0 V -> 10.0-14.5 V, above BATSENS + 0.1 V = 8.5 V (the dossier's 8 ms figure gives 1.0-4.0 V). At the [EVAL]:148 upper bound of 40 ms: 5-20 V -> cold at 20 mA (see adjacent findings).
- RAA489110: IQ 2.25-2.35 mA; CCB capacitance not fixed in [DRAA]; at 20 uF, 1.4 V -> 14.6 V, above the pack, so no ACOK fall.
Score impact if the finding's conclusion had held: S14 weight 5 (`calc/score.py`:1), BQ25690 S14 2 -> 1 = -1.7 points (Table 6.5 D1 row: 48.3 -> 46.7, margin +6.3), condition 3 fails; recommendation (keep BQ25798) unchanged in either case because the margin is already below 10 and the BQ25690 is already "Alternate, pending T-5.HIZ". The recomputation shows the reopening does not occur.
- Source: IO CSV row 31 (authority 1) for the exclusion rule; firmware `teensy_controller.ino` for enforcement and `RT1987_T_D_ON_MS`; [EVAL] section 0.1 rulings 3, 6, 7 and section 4.4 transition table (lines 731-736) for the sequential, CSV-compliant handover and its warm result; working dossiers [D798] (IQ_VBUS, VBUS_PRESENTZ, capacitance), [D690] (IQ_VIN, VIN_OKZ, 11 uF), [D4015] 8.4 (5-20 mA, 40 uF), [D773] (IQ_VAC, VVBUS_CONVEN) for the droop recomputation; `calc/score.py` for the S14 weight. No primary datasheet was re-fetched; every figure used is a working-document figure with its page citation.
- Severity rationale: The confirmed part is documentation drift: 5.6 item 1 ("held closed through Run") contradicts the row-31 guard it cites in item 2, the handover mechanism and its dependency on an unimplemented firmware sequence ([EVAL] 4.4) are unstated, and the evidence chain for the assumption runs main document -> [D690] 11.5 superseded note -> [SCO] Table 8 -> main document. No exposure path follows: a literal implementation of item 1 trips `FAULT_SWITCH_CONFLICT` into State 99 (a latch, not a kill path); the recomputed per-purge event is a 0.3-2.3 V dip for the TI parts, so no published number is wrong, the BQ25690 finding-8 cell and condition 3 stand, the BQ25798 HR2 row gains no per-purge C6 risk, and the operator would not decide differently. Caps at minor.
- Minimal fix: Section 5.6, replace items 1-3 with: "1. Under the always-powered design ([EVAL] section 0.1, ruling 3), REGEN (D-BC-RG) is closed through Run except inside an FC window, where IO CSV row 31 requires REGEN and BT_BUS LOW (`FAULT_SWITCH_CONFLICT`, `assertFcChargeEnable()`). V-MOT is energized at bus level through Idle and Run. 2. Before a purge the firmware runs the [EVAL] section 4.4 'FC -> regen' transition: the charger is inhibited, FC_CHARGE goes LOW, then BT_BUS and REGEN go HIGH ([EVAL] section 0.1, ruling 6). VCHG-IN has no feed for the RT1987 turn-off plus tD_ON, about 10-12 ms (8 ms typical, bench S0-M3). This transition is a design item of [EVAL]; the fw v37 rev 2 Run path does not implement it. 3. Over that gap only the quiescent draw discharges VCHG-IN: BQ25798 about 0.9 V (5 mA, 70 uF; 2.3 V at the 25.8 uF eFuse baseline), BQ25690 about 0.3-0.8 V (0.25-0.7 mA, 11 uF), BQ25773 about 1.5 V (2.5 mA, 20 uF), LTC4015 about 1.5-6 V (5-20 mA, 40 uF), each above its re-qualification threshold (VBUS_PRESENTZ 3.1-3.3 V, VIN_OKZ 2.35 V, VVBUS_CONVEN 3.7 V, BATSENS + 100 mV). The next FC window opens warm. Concurrent REGEN and FC_CHARGE ([EVAL] ruling 7, CSV row-31 amendment pending) is not required for this result." Proof: the arithmetic above; a true BQ25690 POR needs the feed open 214 ms or more. Table 5.2 title: "Per-purge cost of a cold re-entry (FC window closed with D-BC-RG left open for longer than the hold-up, or opened with D-BC-RG open)". Table 6.6 BQ25690 cell and `scoring.md`:243: "(a purge in Run causes none under the [EVAL] section 4.4 handover; section 5.6)". Section 0.0, after "Every candidate fact ... working document": add "[D690] section 11.5 was amended in this round to defer to section 5.6; the handover figures of section 5.6 are those of [EVAL] section 4.4 and the dossier hold-up rows." Fix class: documentation only; no follow-on skill (no test, pin map, protocol, coefficient or bench change).
- Adjacent findings: [D4015] 8.4 (dossier_LTC4015.md:403-405) sizes the FC-to-regen handover at the 8 ms tD_ON only; [EVAL]:148 gives 11-40 ms for the same transition, and at 40 ms the LTC4015 droop is 20 mA x 40 ms / 40 uF = 20 V, i.e. a cold 2-4 s battery-detection cycle per purge at the upper bound; the dossier's S1 score 2 (B) should name the 12 ms [EVAL] 4.4 budget it depends on.; [EVAL] is internally inconsistent on the FC -> regen transition time: 11-40 ms at :148 (ruling 3 text) versus about 10-12 ms in the section 4.4 table at :735; the target inherits the 4.4 figure implicitly.; dossier_BQ25690.md:621-623 was edited in the target round to defer to the main document ('Superseded in part 2026-11-10'), while the target :12-13 states no existing charger document was edited and :18-20 that every fact comes from a working document; the dependency direction should be recorded in the target.; The always-powered Run sequence (REGEN held closed, FC -> regen handover) exists only as the [EVAL] 4.4 design; `teensy_controller.ino` as committed (fw v37 rev 2) closes REGEN in Run only in the regen branch and holds it LOW under `AG105_VBATN_ISOLATED` 0, so every 'warm through a purge' statement in the target and the dossiers is conditional on that firmware change.
- Counter-review: REFINE (minor): I accept minor and the verifier's droop arithmetic: over the [EVAL] 4.4 handover gap (RT1987 turn-off plus tD_ON, about 10-12 ms) only quiescent draw discharges VCHG-IN, so the TI parts dip 0.3-2.3 V and none reaches its re-qualification threshold; a true BQ25690 POR needs the feed open >= 214 ms, longer than the purge, so finding 8 does not reopen and condition 3 stands. Two points survive and should appear in the fix text. First, the handover is a design item of [EVAL] 4.4 that the fw v37 rev 2 Run path does not implement, so the 'warm per purge' result is conditional on firmware not yet written; the verifier's own fix wording says this and it must stay. Second, the authority-order point stands: 5.6 item 1 ('held closed through Run') contradicts the IO CSV row-31 guard it cites and would trip FAULT_SWITCH_CONFLICT if implemented literally; the circular citation chain (main -> [D690] 11.5 -> [SCO] Table 8 -> main) should be broken by the 0.0 provenance note. With those two retained, the per-purge cost remains equal for every part and the recommendation is unaffected.

### CIS-R1-13 (system; reviewer major; final minor; accepted)

- Location: docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:779-785 (section 5.6, items 2-3)
- Claim: Before a purge, the preemptive share shift closes BT_BUS_ENABLE. FC_CHARGE_ENABLE must then be LOW, so D-BC-FC opens; the bus stays at about 16 V on the battery boost and D-BC-RG keeps VCHG-IN at bus level; the H2 permit falls, the charger is inhibited and its threshold returns above the bus.
- Problem: The order is inverted. Closing BT_BUS while FC_CHARGE is HIGH is the illegal combination, and present firmware latches it as FAULT_SWITCH_CONFLICT, so the order as written would cause a State-99 latch at every predicted purge. With REGEN closed, opening D-BC-FC removes nothing: VCHG-IN is still fed from V-MOT, which equals the bus, now battery-fed. The FC-mode threshold (13.14-13.5 V) sits below the ~16 V bus, so the charger runs the bus->charger->pack->BT boost->bus loop that HR3 forbids. Only the hard inhibit, the threshold write and the H2/BT_ACT hardware trip stop it; the BT_ACT trip leaves an accepted residual of up to 0.29 A ([EVAL] ruling 8, P10). The section lists the stop actions without any order relative to the BT_BUS close. The 2026-11-08 ruling note requires the share shift after the window closes.
- Reviewer recommendation: State the order explicitly: (1) inhibit the charger and move the threshold above the bus (or wait for the H2 permit to fall), (2) drop FC_CHARGE, (3) close BT_BUS. State that with REGEN closed, D-BC-FC is not a stop element. Quote the 0.29 A residual as the bound if the order is violated.
- Verdict: PARTLY_CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: Target docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:779-785 orders BT_BUS close -> FC_CHARGE 'must then be LOW' -> D-BC-FC opens -> bus on battery boost -> (next paragraph) H2 falls / inhibit / threshold above bus, with no order between the stop actions and the BT_BUS close. Sourced order: docs/charger_bq25798_wiring_design_2026-11-05.md:114 (r1-1: P5 LOW, then FC_CHARGE and FCCEIL LOW, then VINDPM 19.0 V read back, then BT_BUS HIGH; invariant I-9 'FC_CHARGE and BT_BUS EN nets never HIGH together'; timing lines 1672-1680), which the target itself defers to at line 583; docs/reviews/battery_isolation_2026-11-07/detailed_design_IC1.md:2029-2036 (9.11.1: 'The share shift ... cannot start inside a window. The order is: window close, bond, BT_BUS re-close, share shift, purge') and 9.11.3 steps 1-5 (lines 2120-2133: FC_CHARGE LOW first, BT_BUS re-close step 4, share shift step 5); [EVAL] ruling 6 (charger_replacement_evaluation_2026-11-05.md:57-58), cited by the target for item 2, says the window is interrupted so the battery is back on the bus before the purge - the target reversed it. Illegal combination: references/Scale Car Teensy IO - IO.csv:24 (row 31); teensy_controller/teensy_controller.ino:7413-7419 (FAULT_SWITCH_CONFLICT, compiled only under #if !BENCH_TEST; the check also includes REGEN); guard order ino:12761-12774. VCHG-IN at bus level through D-BC-RG: target items 1 and 3; D-MT-EN conducts when V-MOT <= VBUS ([EVAL] line 387). Threshold floor: target line 277 and Table 2.2a lines 291-295. Hardware stop terms: wiring design line 1226 (H2c = FC_CHARGE AND BTB_OFF AND BT_OK AND FCI_OK AND ((UVB AND CHG_FCCEIL) OR (H1 AND REGEN_ENABLE))), line 1728 (FCC net LOW -> H2c = 0 within 2-4 us). The 0.29 A residual: [EVAL] lines 393-394, 409-411, 63, 219 - the case is D-BT-EN SHORTED with BT_BUS commanded LOW, not a commanded BT_BUS close. Operator ruling verbatim (battery_isolation_feasibility_2026-11-07.md:76-78) states no order; the order is [DD] 9.11.1's derivation, not the 'ruling note'.
- Quantification: FC-mode floor 13.5 V x (1 - 0.026) = 13.149 V (target 13.14 V) < 16.0 V bus, so an un-inhibited charger with the FC image would draw from the battery-fed bus at up to IINDPM 0.90 A (FC ILIM ceiling 0.74-0.96 A) - loop premise confirmed. If BT_BUS closed while the charger were still permitted, the H2c BTB_OFF term withdraws the permit in the 2-4 us comparator/gate class: overlap energy ~ 16 V x 0.9 A x 4 us ~ 58 uJ, not a 'sustained' loop under HR3 (target line 112); a sustained 0.9 A loop needs a second fault (BTB_OFF stuck HIGH, latent under I-9, wiring design r3-5 line 167). The 0.29 A figure = BT_ACT trip ceiling (19.8 mV + 9 mV Vos) / 100 mV/A = 0.288 A for D-BT-EN shorted with BT_BUS commanded LOW; it does not bound an order violation, where BTB_OFF, not BT_ACT, is the stop. 'State 99 at every purge' is reachable only in hypothetical firmware writing BT_BUS HIGH with FC_CHARGE HIGH across a detectFaults() sample in a BENCH_TEST 0 build; present chargingControl() never does this, and target item 1 (REGEN closed with FC_CHARGE HIGH) trips the same check (ino:7416) regardless, so the firmware must change for the always-powered design in any case.
- Source: Repo design files (IO CSV row 31; .ino guard ino:12741-12778 and fault check ino:7413-7419) for the illegal combination; [EVAL] ruling 6 and the 2026-11-05 wiring design r1-1 / I-9 / H2c (lines 114, 1226, 1672-1680, 1728) for the sourced order and the stop mechanism; [DD] 9.11.1 and 9.11.3 for the share-shift position; [EVAL] 2.3 (lines 393-411) for what the 0.29 A residual bounds. No datasheet fetch required: the finding is about sequencing, not a part parameter.
- Severity rationale: The order in 5.6 items 2-3 is inverted relative to every source the target cites, and D-BC-FC is not the stop element with D-BC-RG closed - real defects in a safety-relevant section. However the section is a cost-equalising system assumption (every part 'costs the same'); Table 5.2 and the scores are untouched; the target defers the window-close path to the 2026-11-05 design (line 583), which carries the correct order and I-9. Worst reachable consequence of following the inverted order: a FAULT_SWITCH_CONFLICT safe latch (production build) or a microsecond-class permit withdrawal by H2c's BTB_OFF term (~58 uJ); a sustained HR3 loop requires a second fault (BTB_OFF stuck HIGH). The finding's severity vehicles - a State-99 at every purge and a 0.29 A loop - do not hold as bounds for this defect, so it caps at minor (doc drift / inverted sequencing prose).
- Minimal fix: Replace docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md section 5.6 items 2-3 (lines 779-781) with: "2. Before a predicted purge the FC window closes first, in the order of the 2026-11-05 design (r1-1, invariant I-9) and of [DD] section 9.11.1: the charger is inhibited (P5 LOW, or the part's hard-inhibit equivalent) and its threshold is written back above the bus, then `FC_CHARGE_ENABLE` goes LOW, then `BT_BUS_ENABLE` closes (IO CSV row 31; `FAULT_SWITCH_CONFLICT` is the backstop). The share shift toward the battery follows the BT_BUS close ([EVAL] section 0.1, ruling 6; [DD] section 9.11.1). 3. With D-BC-RG closed, the opening of D-BC-FC does not remove the charger input: VCHG-IN stays at about V-MOT, that is, at bus level, now battery-fed, and the FC-mode threshold floor (13.10-13.14 V, Table 2.2a) is below the 16 V bus. The stop is the hard inhibit, the threshold write and the H2 permit, whose FCC_OFF term falls with `FC_CHARGE_ENABLE` and whose BTB_OFF term falls with `BT_BUS_ENABLE`. A BT_BUS close while the charger is still permitted would run the bus -> charger -> pack -> BT boost -> bus loop of HR3 at up to the input limit (0.90 A IINDPM; FC ILIM ceiling 0.74-0.96 A) until the permit falls; I-9 forbids that order." Do NOT quote 0.29 A as the order-violation bound (it is the D-BT-EN-shorted residual, [EVAL] 409-411). Each clause cites wiring design lines 114, 1226, 1728; [DD] 2029-2036; Table 2.2a 291-295; [EVAL] 604. Fix class: documentation only, no follow-on skill; when carrier firmware is written, the r1-1 host test `preempt-fcc-btb-overlap` pins I-9. Apply the same correction to dossier_BQ25690.md section 11.5 lines 629-630.
- Adjacent findings: dossier_BQ25690.md:629-630 (section 11.5) repeats the inverted order ('The preemptive share shift closes BT_BUS_ENABLE, and FC_CHARGE_ENABLE may not be HIGH ... The FC feed ... is therefore opened'); its 2026-11-10 supersession note does not correct it.; Target 5.6 item 1 (REGEN held closed through Run while FC windows run) is itself the FAULT_SWITCH_CONFLICT combination in present firmware (ino:7416 tests REGEN), and the row-31 amendment is 'pending documentation change, not yet made' ([EVAL] ruling 7, lines 59-60); the target cites 'IO CSV row-31 guard' as current in item 2 while relying on its amended form in item 1.; FAULT_SWITCH_CONFLICT is compiled out under BENCH_TEST 1 (ino:7413), the build currently flashed on the bench (fw v37 rev 2); 'present firmware latches it' holds for the production build only.; The finding attributes 'share shift after the window closes' to the 2026-11-08 ruling note; the verbatim ruling (battery_isolation_feasibility_2026-11-07.md:76-78) states no order - the order is [DD] 9.11.1's derivation.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): My severity vehicles do not hold as bounds. The 0.29 A figure is the BT_ACT trip ceiling for the D-BT-EN-shorted residual, not an order-violation bound; with BT_BUS closed while the charger is permitted, the H2c BTB_OFF term withdraws the permit in the 2-4 us class (about 58 uJ overlap), and a sustained HR3 loop needs a second fault (BTB_OFF stuck HIGH). 'State 99 at every purge' requires hypothetical firmware writing BT_BUS HIGH with FC_CHARGE HIGH across a detectFaults() sample, which present chargingControl() never does; and item 1 (REGEN closed with FC_CHARGE HIGH) trips the same check regardless, so the firmware changes for the always-powered design in any case. The inverted order in 5.6 items 2-3 and the misidentification of D-BC-FC as the stop element are real defects in a safety-relevant section but the window-close path is deferred to the 2026-11-05 design with I-9. Minor; the replacement text accepted, with the dossier_BQ25690 11.5 mirror.

### CIS-R1-14 (coverage; reviewer major; final minor; accepted)

- Location: Table 6.0b row 'HR1 input at the threshold' (l.847); Table 5.1 column 'HR1 input' (l.534)
- Claim: BQ25798 '28.9 W (1.55 A x 18.62 V), +25 % (C)' against the challengers' '23.7 W (charge-limited, 7.4 V pack), +3.0 %' and similar entries
- Problem: The BQ25798 entry is the input-ceiling product. The challenger entries are the charge-limited input draw at a 7.4 V pack, with the BQ25773 at its ICHG low corner (3.04 x 7.4 / 0.95 = 23.7 W). On the same basis the BQ25798 draws 24.8 W at nominal ICHG (dossier_BQ25798.md 11.3, 7.4 V row), a margin of +7.8 %. At the ICHG -6.5 % corner it draws about 3.1 x 0.935 x 7.4 / 0.925 = 23.2 W (+0.9 %). The table shows a +25 % versus +3-6 % gap that does not exist on a like-for-like basis. Exposure: silently wrong published margins.
- Reviewer recommendation: Report every part on one basis: charge-limited input at a 7.4 V pack with every tolerance at its low corner. Report the ceiling capability in a separate column.
- Verdict: CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: The target, at charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:847 (Table 6.0b) and :534 (Table 5.1), gives the BQ25798 "28.9 W (1.55 A x 18.62 V), +25 % (C)". That figure copies scoring.md:162 verbatim. It is the input-ceiling product: the reference-biased ceiling minimum of 1.55 A (dossier_BQ25798.md section 7.2) times the 18.62 V threshold floor. The challenger cells in the same row use a different method. The BQ25773 dossier states that rule at dossier_BQ25773_SMBus_twin_BQ25770G.md:528: "Input power is the lower of the input-ceiling limit and the charge-current limit." Its line 532 then takes the charge limit at a 7.4 V pack with ICHG at the low corner: 3.04 x 7.4 / 0.95 = 23.7 W. The LTC4015 and RAA489110 cells are also labelled "charge-limited, 7.4 V pack". The BQ25690 cell is a ceiling product, but there the ceiling really is the lower bound, because its charge demand at 7.4 V is 3.1 x 7.4 / 0.92 = 24.9 W, above the 23.9 W ceiling. The BQ25798 dossier's own table at dossier_BQ25798.md:395-397 gives the 7.4 V row as "ICHG 3.1 A demand (input) 24.8 W", delivered 3.10 A, ICHG-limited. So on the lower-of rule the BQ25798's governing figure is 24.8 W, not 28.9 W. dossier_BQ25798.md:374 gives the ICHG accuracy only at 0.5-2 A (2 A: -6.5/+3.5 %, VBAT 8 V, DS EC p.11), so a low corner at 3.1 A is an extrapolation. calc/score.py does not use the HR1 input margin at all. A grep of the calc/*.py files for W / 23 W / 28.9 found no hits, so the scores and the recommendation do not depend on this row. The finding's premise holds: the BQ25798 is the only part reported as a ceiling product where the charge limit is lower.
- Quantification: BQ25798 as published: 1.55 x 18.62 = 28.86 W, and 28.86/23 = 1.255, so +25.5 %.
Efficiency implied by the BQ25798 dossier: 3.1 x 7.4 / 24.8 = 0.925.
Charge-limited at nominal ICHG and a 7.4 V pack: 3.1 x 7.4 / 0.925 = 24.80 W, which is +7.8 % against 23 W.
ICHG -6.5 % corner (the 2 A spec, extrapolated): 3.1 x 0.935 = 2.8985 A; 2.8985 x 7.4 = 21.45 W; 21.45 / 0.925 = 23.19 W, which is +0.8 %.
Challengers as published: BQ25773 23.7 W (+3.0 %), BQ25690 23.9 W (+3.9 %), LTC4015 24.0 W (+4.3 %), RAA489110 24.4 W (+6.1 %).
On the like-for-like low-corner basis the BQ25798 has the smallest HR1 input margin, not the largest. Every part still passes 23 W.
The efficiency assumptions also differ between parts (0.925 for the BQ25798, 0.95 for the BQ25773, 0.94 assumed for the RAA489110). This changes the margins by a few percent but does not change any pass/fail result.
- Source: Working documents, because the claim is a basis mismatch inside the document set and needs no new datasheet value: dossier_BQ25798.md:374 (ICHG accuracy quoted from DS EC p.11) and :395-397 (section 11.3, 24.8 W ICHG-limited at 7.4 V); dossier_BQ25773_SMBus_twin_BQ25770G.md:528-533 (the lower-of rule and 23.7 W); scoring.md:162; calc/score.py (HR1 input not scored). The target's prose is the lowest authority and is the place where the inconsistency appears.
- Severity rationale: The published margin ordering is inverted. The BQ25798 shows +25 % where the like-for-like figure is +7.8 % at nominal ICHG, or +0.8 % at the extrapolated low corner, against +3 % to +6 % for the challengers. However, the exposure is bounded. (1) Every part passes the 23 W condition on either basis, so no pass/fail status changes. (2) calc/score.py does not consume the HR1 input margin, so the scores (40.3 / 49.0 / 48.3 / 48.7 / 35.7) and the keep-BQ25798 recommendation are unchanged. (3) Each cell states its own basis: "1.55 A x 18.62 V" against "charge-limited, 7.4 V pack". The basis is disclosed in the cell, so the number is inconsistently compared rather than silently wrong. There is no kill path or decision reversal, so the finding is published-margin drift and caps at minor.
- Minimal fix: Apply the same edit in three places: target Table 6.0b row "HR1 input at the threshold" (l.847), target Table 5.1 BQ25798 cell (l.534), and scoring.md:162 (Table 5), so that the source and the target stay identical.

Replace "28.9 W (1.55 A x 18.62 V), +25 % (C)" with "24.8 W (charge-limited, 7.4 V pack, ICHG 3.1 A, eta 0.925), +7.8 % (C); 23.2 W at the ICHG -6.5 % corner (accuracy given only at 2 A, DS EC p.11; extrapolation U), +0.8 %; input-ceiling capability 28.9 W (1.55 A x 18.62 V)".

In Table 5.1 change "28.9 W" to "24.8 W (+7.8 %, charge-limited at 7.4 V; ceiling 28.9 W)".

Add one sentence under Table 6.0b: "The input at the threshold is the lower of the input-ceiling limit and the charge-current limit ([D773] section 11.3), at a 7.4 V pack and the low tolerance corners."

Proof: 3.1 x 7.4 / 0.925 = 24.80 W, from dossier_BQ25798.md:397; 3.1 x 0.935 x 7.4 / 0.925 = 23.19 W; 24.8/23 - 1 = 7.8 %; 23.19/23 - 1 = 0.8 %. score.py is unaffected, so the totals are unchanged.

No firmware skill is triggered. This is a document-only correction.
- Adjacent findings: Efficiency basis is inconsistent across the HR1 input cells: BQ25798 eta 0.925 (implied by dossier_BQ25798.md:397), BQ25773 eta 0.95 (dossier_BQ25773 l.532), RAA489110 eta 0.94 assumed. The charge-limited margins are not on one efficiency basis.; The BQ25798 ICHG low corner at 3.1 A is an extrapolation of the 2 A spec (dossier_BQ25798.md:374). The 'Met at the floor' / '3.10 A' entries in Table 5.1 and Table 6.0b take ICHG at nominal, while the BQ25773 is graded at its -5 % ICHG corner (3.04 A, 'Not met'). At -6.5 % the BQ25798 would deliver about 2.90 A, so the 3.1 A delivered-current comparison also uses mixed bases. This should be checked as a separate finding.; dossier_BQ25773 l.538 and l.786 assert that HR1 input power 'is not a differentiator'. That statement is consistent with the corrected figures but contradicts the +25 % vs +3 % presentation now in the target.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): The like-for-like figures stand (BQ25798 24.8 W / +7.8 % at nominal ICHG, 23.2 W / +0.8 % at the extrapolated -6.5 % corner against +3-6 % for the challengers), so the published margin ordering is inverted. But each cell states its own basis ('1.55 A x 18.62 V' against 'charge-limited, 7.4 V pack'), every part passes 23 W on either basis, and score.py does not consume the HR1 margin, so this is an inconsistently compared number rather than a silently wrong one. Minor; the three-place edit and the basis sentence under Table 6.0b accepted. This edit should land together with R1-04/R1-07/S01 so Table 5.1 is re-based once.

### CIS-R1-16 (decision; reviewer minor; final minor; accepted)

- Location: charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:58 (Table 0.2), :930 (Table 6.4), :1044 (section 7), :1082 (R-S14)
- Claim: BQ25773: "Alternate: decision-rule conditions 1, 3 and 4 met; margin below 10"; section 7: "it meets conditions 1, 3 and 4 on present evidence".

- Verdict: PARTLY_CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: Condition 3 (CONFIRMED as stated-settled-while-open): target Table 0.2 line 58 reads "Alternate: decision-rule conditions 1, 3 and 4 met; margin below 10" with no qualifier; section 7 line 1044 reads "it meets conditions 1, 3 and 4 on present evidence". The same document grades condition 3 for the BQ25773 as "Met (provisional)" in Table 6.4 line 930; section 6.4 statement 4 lines 971-972 states "Under the strict S14 reading (D1), the BQ25690 and the BQ25773 each lose 1.7 points and fail condition 3, because finding 8 is major"; Table 8.1 row R-S14 line 1082 says the ruling "Decides condition 3 for the BQ25690 and the BQ25773"; Table 8.1 item 15 line 1123 (the word-by-word never-write count that resolves R-S14) is "Not done". The unqualified "conditions 1, 3 and 4 met" wording is inherited verbatim from [SCO] Table 14 line 486, so the target does not diverge from [SCO]; the defect is the summary/recommendation text contradicting the body's own grading.

Condition 1 (NOT confirmed as "open to a ruling"): [CRIT] 3.5 item 1 (screening_criteria.md line 158) bars a conditional pass on M5-M7 only "where the BQ25798 has P evidence". dossier_BQ25798.md line 124 grades the BQ25798 M7 as "Conditional pass (P mechanism, C nominal bounds, B re-sampling)", not Pass. Applying the rule as written, the BQ25798 does not "have P" on M7, so the BQ25773's conditional M7 does not trigger condition 1. [D773] 16.3 line 769 records the alternative reading ("If the main document reads the BQ25798's 'P mechanism' as P evidence for M7, condition 1 bars the BQ25773"), and [D773] review item 14 line 828 records that the symmetric reading was adopted deliberately. The target hedges condition 1 in Table 6.0a line 832 and Table 6.4 line 928 ("Met under a symmetric M7 grading"). Condition 1 is a rule application with a recorded alternative reading, not an open ruling.

Strict S14 (D1): S14 = 1 -> 1.667; drop = 1.667; total 49.0 - 1.667 = 47.3, margin 47.3 - 40.3 = +7.0 (matches Table 6.5 line 953). BQ25690 likewise 48.3 - 1.667 = 46.7 (+6.3). Under D1, R6 finding 8 counts as reopened; [CRIT] 3.5 item 3 names finding 8 major, so condition 3 fails and the BQ25773 is no longer "the alternate" under [CRIT] 3.5 line 166. Order check under D1: BQ25773 47.3 (condition 1 met, 3 fails), BQ25690 46.7 (1 open, 3 fails), LTC4015 B 48.7 (1 fails), RAA489110 35.7. No hardware exposure, no wrong number (the D1 arithmetic 49.0 - 1.667 = 47.3 is correct where it appears), no protocol effect. Exposure path examined: (a) wrong-part / board-spin exposure - none, the recommendation (keep BQ25798) and the rank order are invariant to the basis choice, because the LTC4015 fails condition 1 on M5 c regardless, M3 carries no score weight, and the BQ25798 and BQ25773 pass on every published basis; (b) pack over-voltage - none, the failing side on every part and basis is the lower bound (undercharge <= 13 mV for the BQ25690, <= 46 mV for LTC4015 code 30), while the upper bounds stay inside 8.484 V (BQ25690) or are already flagged (LTC4015 8.505 V); (c) operator-decision exposure - bounded to the rank-3 status label: if the operator selects the full operating-junction basis (the only basis on which the LTC4015 is graded), the BQ25690 reads M3 F by 13 mV and Table 0.2 rank 3 moves from "Alternate only if T-5.HIZ passes" to "Not eligible", which is a status change but not an order or recommendation change. The junction rise of 48-93 C at 3.1 A makes TJ > 85 C reachable, so the inconsistency is not purely academic, but the consequence remains a lower-side undercharge. Caps at minor.
- Minimal fix: Three text edits in docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md (doc-only; no test, pinmap, protocol, coefficient or bench follow-on):

1. Section 5.3 key facts, line 650, replace the bullet with: "- 2S CV 8.358-8.442 V at TJ 0 to 85 C (section 5.5 p.8): M3 is P on that basis. Over TJ -40 to 125 C (-1.15/+0.9 %, same page) the band is 8.303-8.476 V, 13 mV below the 8.316 V lower limit and 8 mV inside the 8.484 V upper limit."

2. Section 5.4 key facts, lines 701-702, replace with: "- VCHARGE tolerance +-1.25 % over the full operating junction range (p.4; no narrower-range row is published): code 31 (4.200 V/cell) gives 8.295-8.505 V, 21 mV outside the M3 band on both sides. DAC code 30 (4.1875 V/cell) gives 8.270-8.480 V: the upper limit holds, the lower bound is 46 mV below 8.316 V, so no code meets +-1.0 % on both sides; code 30 also reverts to 4.20 V/cell after every charger POR."

3. Section 6.0, one sentence after Table 6.0a (line 835): "M3 is graded on the TJ 0 to 85 C specification where the datasheet publishes one (BQ25690 and BQ25773 at 0 to 85 C; BQ25798 at -40 to 85 C; the RAA489110 row states no basis). The LTC4015 publishes one tolerance over its full operating junction range only; on that same -40 to 125 C range the BQ25690 reads 8.303-8.476 V, 13 mV below the lower limit, so the BQ25690 pass and the LTC4015 fail rest on different bases, and an operator ruling for the full range would move the BQ25690 to M3 F and Table 0.2 rank 3 to Not eligible."

Proof: all numbers above recomputed from SLUSFN4A p.8 (-1.15/+0.9 %: 8.4 x 0.9885 = 8.3034, 8.4 x 1.009 = 8.4756) and the LTC4015 DAC law (3.8125 + N/80) V/cell with +-1.25 % (code 30: 8.375 x 0.9875 = 8.2703, 8.375 x 1.0125 = 8.4797); the two 0-85 C TI rows are datasheet-tabulated. The same basis clause should be added to [CRIT] screening_criteria.md:67 and the 8.316 V lower limit to its :186 table (working documents, outside the target but the same class of edit); [D690] M3 row :105 should state the full-TJ lower bound against 8.316 V rather than only the upper bound.

The failing side on every part and basis is the lower bound (undercharge), the LTC4015 fails condition 1 on M5 c regardless, and M3 carries no score weight, so the exposure is bounded to the rank-3 status label under an operator basis ruling. ### CIS-R1-18 (decision; reviewer minor; final minor; accepted)

- Location: charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:66-67 ("2.3-4.0 points"), :952 (Table 6.5 row C1); calc/score.py scenario D
- Claim: "C1: stock and price caps lifted (best case)": BQ25798 43.3, BQ25690 52.3 (+9.0), BQ25773 51.3 (+8.0), LTC4015 52.7 (+9.3), RAA489110 38.0 (-5.3).
- Problem: score.py lifts the BQ25798 to S12 = 3, S13 = 3 but the BQ25773 and RAA489110 to S13 = 2 only, although their stage-cost allowances (USD 13-20 and USD 11-15, Table 6.3) reach the S13 = 3 anchor (< USD 15) just as the BQ25798's USD 10-12 does; it also lifts the BQ25690 to S12 = 3 (>= 1000 units at two sources) against snippets of 176 and 460 units with an 18-week lead time. The scenario is thus the point estimate for the challengers and the best case for the baseline. Recomputed with BQ25773 S13 = 3: 52.0 (+8.7), not 51.3 (+8.0); RAA489110 38.7 (-4.7). No outcome changes, but the label "best case" and the derived "2.3-4.0 points" band understate the BQ25773 movement (3.0, not 2.3).
- Reviewer recommendation: Either apply one rule in C1 (lift every part to the top of its allowance range, or none) or relabel the row "caps lifted at the dossier point estimates" and recompute the quoted 2.3-4.0 band.
- Verdict: PARTLY_CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: The score.py scenario D, at calc/score.py:41-42, lifts the BQ25798 to S12 = 3 and S13 = 3. It lifts the BQ25773 to S12 = 3 and S13 = 2, the BQ25690 to S12 = 3 and S13 = 2, and the RAA489110 to S12 = 2 and S13 = 2. The S13 anchors are defined in screening_criteria.md:109: 3 is < USD 15, 2 is USD 15-30, 1 is USD 30-60. The stage-cost allowances in target Table 6.3 are at charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:911-915: BQ25798 USD 10-12, BQ25690 USD 15-19 with the monitor, BQ25773 USD 13-20, LTC4015 USD 19-30 and RAA489110 USD 11-15. The dossiers give the same figures: dossier_BQ25798.md:596, dossier_BQ25773_SMBus_twin_BQ25770G.md:751, dossier_RAA489110_alternates_RAA489118_RAA489108_in_the_same_dossier.md:714 and dossier_LTC4015.md:614.

Under a best-case rule the BQ25773 (USD 13 low end) and the RAA489110 (USD 11 low end) both reach S13 = 3. The RAA489110 range has a midpoint of USD 13, so it scores S13 = 3 even at its point estimate. Its C1 value of 2 therefore understates it under either rule.

The BQ25690 S13 = 2 and the LTC4015 S13 = 2 follow from their allowances, because neither range goes below USD 15. That part of the scenario is consistent.

The BQ25690 S12 = 3 lift is not a defect. C1 is defined as the case in which the blocked fetches return favourable stock (target :1111), and the BQ25798 receives the same lift from an UNKNOWN second source (dossier_BQ25798.md:595). The regional snippets (target :912) are UNVERIFIED partial views and do not refute ">= 1000 at two sources". That sub-claim of the finding is therefore not confirmed.

The C1 row appears in two places, target :952 and scoring.md:371. The band sentence appears at target :66, target :999 and scoring.md:401-403. The E2 row at target :957 inherits the C1 values for the BQ25773 and the RAA489110.
- Quantification: The weighted total is sum(w*s/3). An S13 step from 2 to 3 adds 2/3 = 0.667 points. An S12 step adds 5/3 = 1.667 points.

The C1 rows as published:
- BQ25798: +1.667 + 1.333 = +3.00, giving 43.33.
- BQ25773: +1.667 + 0.667 = +2.33, giving 51.33 (+8.00).
- RAA489110: +2.33, giving 38.00 (-5.33).

With BQ25773 S13 = 3 and RAA489110 S13 = 3, the same arithmetic gives:
- BQ25773: +3.00, giving 52.00 (+8.67).
- RAA489110: +3.00, giving 38.67 (-4.67).
- BQ25690 and LTC4015-B are unchanged at 52.33 (+9.00) and 52.67 (+9.33).

I confirmed these figures by running a scratch copy of score.py with only the two C1 lines changed (scratchpad/advrev/score_c1.py, python -I). The E2 row also changes: BQ25773 52.0 (+8.7) and RAA489110 38.7 (-4.7).

The challenger movement band becomes 3.0-4.0 points. It is not 2.3-4.0, because the 2.3 lower end came from the BQ25773 and the RAA489110. Every margin stays below 10, with a maximum of +9.33 in C1. The BQ25773 margin is +8.67, 1.33 points short of the switching margin, so no outcome changes.
- Source: The cost anchor is screening_criteria.md:109 (working document), and the allowances are Table 6.3 at target :911-915, which matches the dossiers. The scoring was recomputed with calc/score.py. No primary datasheet is involved, because this is a scoring-consistency finding.
- Severity rationale: The published C1 numbers for two parts are inconsistent with the scenario's own "best case" label: BQ25773 is understated by 0.67 points and RAA489110 by 0.67 points. The 2.3-4.0 band is understated at its lower end by 0.7 points. No exposure path follows from this. With the correction, the largest C1 margin is still +9.33 (LTC4015-B), the BQ25773 margin is +8.67 and the RAA489110 margin is -4.67. None crosses the 10-point switching margin, so the recommendation to keep the BQ25798 and the shortlist order are unchanged. The BQ25773 rises from below the BQ25690 to 52.0 against 52.3, which is still below it, so the order is preserved. The defect is a scenario-label drift, not a wrong part recommendation.
- Minimal fix: 1. In calc/score.py line 41, change D['BQ25773'].update(S12=3,S13=2) to D['BQ25773'].update(S12=3,S13=3).
2. In calc/score.py line 42, change D['RAA489110'].update(S12=2,S13=2) to D['RAA489110'].update(S12=2,S13=3). Each S13 now takes the top anchor its allowance range reaches: BQ25773 USD 13 and RAA489110 USD 11, both < USD 15. BQ25690 (USD 15) and LTC4015 (USD 19) stay at 2.
3. In target :952 and scoring.md:371, replace the C1 row with: | C1: stock and price caps lifted (best case) | 43.3 | 52.3 (+9.0) | 52.0 (+8.7) | 52.7 (+9.3) | 38.7 (-4.7) |
4. In target :957, replace the E2 row with: | E2: challengers optimistic (C1 with D3) | 43.3 | 55.7 (+12.3) | 52.0 (+8.7) | 52.7 (+9.3) | 38.7 (-4.7) |
5. In target :66, target :999 and scoring.md:402, replace "2.3-4.0 points" with "3.0-4.0 points".

Step 3 is proven by the scratch run, which output 52.00 / +8.67 and 38.67 / -4.67. Rounding gives 52.0 (+8.7) and 38.7 (-4.7).

As an alternative, keep the numbers and relabel the row "caps lifted at the dossier point estimates". This alternative is not self-consistent, because the RAA489110 point estimate (USD 13) is already S13 = 3. For that reason the first fix is preferred.

Follow-on skill: none. This is a document and scoring-script fix that needs a re-run of score.py.
- Adjacent findings: scoring.md:401-402 says C1 raises the baseline by 3.0 points through 'verified passive prices'. The BQ25798 S13 1-to-3 lift is +1.33 points. Its USD 10-12 range is below USD 15 throughout, so the BQ25798 lift is its point estimate and not a best case. The 'best case' asymmetry therefore applies to the challengers only.; The RAA489110 USD 11-15 allowance has its midpoint (USD 13) inside the S13 = 3 anchor. Its uncapped dossier score (dossier_RAA489110...md:714) would be 3, not the band-2 reading that C1 implies.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): The scratch re-run confirms BQ25773 52.0 (+8.7) and RAA489110 38.7 (-4.7) with S13 = 3, and the band becomes 3.0-4.0. Every margin stays below 10 (maximum +9.33), so no outcome changes. The first fix (edit score.py lines 41-42, re-run, update the C1 and E2 rows and the '2.3-4.0' text in three places) is preferred over relabelling because the RAA489110 point estimate is already S13 = 3. Minor; this is the one finding that requires a score.py re-run.

### CIS-R1-19 (system; reviewer minor; final minor; accepted)

- Location: docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:86 (0.1), :1050-1053 (section 7, condition R-M2)
- Claim: R-M2 holds with the TPS26630 OVP response read from SLVSE94G and a VCHG-IN transient measurement on a REGEN close at 21.5 V showing less than 30 V at the BQ25798 pins.
- Problem: 30 V is the absolute maximum. The functional limits are 24 V operating and VBUS_OVP 25.2 V minimum (5.1 line 566). A transient between 25.2 V and 30 V passes this criterion yet trips VBUS_OVP, and recovery is a re-qualification. [D798] line 227 does not exclude a VINDPM overwrite on that path (B, S1-B2), which would be a cold path (M9) and would move the HR3 threshold layer below the bus. A single REGEN close at 21.5 V stays below the eFuse OVP band (22.61-23.63 V), so it never exercises the OVP response and overshoot that R-M2 depends on. Section 0.1 states R-M2 without the U mark on the eFuse response; sections 1.3 and 7 carry it.
- Reviewer recommendation: Set the pass limit at the BQ25798 VBUS/VAC OVP minimum, not at 30 V. Add a ramp through 22.6-23.6 V at the measured worst V-MOT dV/dt to test the OVP response. Carry the U mark into the 0.1 condition.
- Verdict: PARTLY_CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: CONFIRMED parts. (1) Pass-limit inconsistency: C:/Life Ops/School/Thesis/DC-Balancer-charger-search/docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:1050-1052 sets the R-M2 bench pass at "less than 30 V at the BQ25798 pins"; the same document's M2 criterion (:171 "operating range covers 22.28 V; no OVP drop-out") and protected-standoff reading (:186-187) and its dossier premise (docs/reviews/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10/dossier_BQ25798.md:119 "keeps the IC pins at or below the 24 V operating maximum. VBUS_OVP 25.2 V min") rest on 24 V / 25.2 V, not 30 V. [WD] docs/charger_bq25798_wiring_design_2026-11-05.md:879 and :2145 size the eFuse OVP to sit "0.37 V below the BQ25798 recommended VBUS maximum 24 V". Primary: SLUSDV2C p.7 §6.1 VBUS abs max 30 V, §6.3 V_VBUS max 24 V; p.9 §6.5 V_VBUS_OVP rising 25.2/25.7/26.2 V, falling 24.0/24.4/24.8 V (scratch copy ds_bq25798/bq25798.pdf). (2) Stimulus does not exercise the OVP: eFuse OVP cutoff 22.61-23.63 V ([WD]:879, recomputed below from SLVSE94G p.6 V(OVPR) 1.176-1.224 V and R102/R103/R104 845k/66.5k/49.9k); a REGEN close at 21.5 V ([WD]:1172 "VCHG-IN reaches 21.2-21.5 V measured") is 1.11 V below the band minimum, so the eFuse FET never opens in that test and the OUT ramp is the dVdT-controlled 2.19 V/ms turn-on ([WD]:880), which cannot overshoot IN. (3) U-mark omission: target :86-87 states R-M2 with no TODO; :189 carries "TODO(verify: SLVSE94G)", :1050 carries "read from SLVSE94G", :1109 lists it as "Not fetched in this round"; dossier :134-135 carries the TODO. NOT CONFIRMED parts. (4) "recovery is a re-qualification": SLUSDV2C §7.3.13.1.2 (scratch bq25798.txt lines 3298-3304): "the converter stops switching immediately ... The device automatically starts switching again when the VBUS voltage drops below the threshold"; a re-run of input-source qualification is stated only when VBUS_OVP is present during the qualification step (§7.3.4.2, lines 1742-1745). The dossier already carries the VINDPM-overwrite question on this path as B (dossier :227, :548), which is the correct status; the finding states it as fact. (5) Reachability of a 25.2-30 V transient at the BQ25798 pins with a functioning eFuse: SLVSE94G p.8 §6.6 OVP_tOFF(dly) = 8.5/11/14 µs (scratch tps2663.txt line 647); the window is unreachable at any credible slew (see quantification). (6) The "unread eFuse property" is in fact published in SLVSE94G §6.6 p.8, the same timing table [WD]:906 already cites for the 1 µs hot-short response; the symlink URL at target :1109 resolves (5.7 MB, SLVSE94G Sept 2018 rev. June 2024).
- Quantification: eFuse OVP band (SLVSE94G p.6, [WD]:879 divider): k = 49.9/(845+66.5+49.9) = 49.9/961.4 = 0.05190; trip = V(OVPR)/k: 1.176/0.05190 = 22.66 V, 1.2/0.05190 = 23.12 V, 1.224/0.05190 = 23.58 V; with 0.1 % resistors (ratio +-0.2 %) 22.61-23.63 V. Matches [WD]. Overshoot bound after the OVP trip: OUT holds at V_trip + dV/dt x OVP_tOFF(dly); OVP_tOFF(dly) max 14 µs (SLVSE94G p.8). Design regen slope 4.30 V/ms ([EVAL] docs/charger_replacement_evaluation_2026-11-05.md:460): 4.30 V/ms x 14 µs = 0.060 V -> pins <= 23.63 + 0.06 = 23.69 V. VESC Battery Current Regen Max 1.5 A (docs/VESC_MOTOR_INTEGRATION.md:231) into board-only V-MOT 0.48 mF ([EVAL]:538): 3.1 V/ms -> 0.044 V. Pathological bound MOTOR_I_CMD_MAX 12 A fully regenerative into 0.48 mF: 25 V/ms -> 0.35 V -> 23.98 V. Conservative read of the 20 mV OVP-pin overdrive in the delay spec (0.020/0.05190 = 0.385 V input-referred): add 0.39 V -> 24.07 V (design slope) / 24.37 V (12 A). To reach the VBUS_OVP minimum 25.2 V: (25.2 - 23.63)/14 µs = 112 V/ms = 54 A into 0.48 mF (448 A at the 4 mF estimate) with no overdrive term, or 84 V/ms = 40 A with it; to reach 30 V: 455 V/ms = 218 A. Margin of the design-slope hold level to VBUS_OVP min: 25.2 - 23.69 = 1.51 V. Stimulus gap: 22.61 - 21.5 = 1.11 V below the eFuse band minimum; the OVP never trips in the specified test. Hysteresis note: VBUS_OVP falling 24.0-24.8 V, so a pin voltage between 24.0 and 25.2 V is outside the recommended range but does not trip VBUS_OVP.
- Source: Primary datasheets fetched to the scratchpad: TPS2663 SLVSE94G (Sept 2018 rev. June 2024) §6.5 p.6 V(OVPR), §6.6 p.8 OVP_tOFF(dly), §8.3.4; BQ25798 SLUSDV2C (May 2020 rev. June 2026) §6.1 p.7, §6.3 p.7, §6.5 p.9, §7.3.4.2 p.26-27, §7.3.13.1.2. Repo design files: [WD] docs/charger_bq25798_wiring_design_2026-11-05.md:879-880, :1172, :2145; [EVAL]:460, :538; docs/VESC_MOTOR_INTEGRATION.md:231. Working documents: dossier_BQ25798.md:119, :134-135, :184, :227, :548; scoring.md:510-513. Target :86-87, :171, :186-190, :1050-1053, :1109.
- Severity rationale: The 30 V pass limit contradicts the document's own protected-standoff premise (<= 24 V operating, dossier :119, [WD]:879) and the 21.5 V stimulus cannot exercise the eFuse OVP, so section 7 condition 1 as written would be satisfied by a measurement that proves nothing about the eFuse standoff. However, no exposure path reaches the 25.2-30 V window with a functioning eFuse: the recomputed hold level is 23.69 V at the design regen slope and <= 24.0 V at the 12 A motor-command bound (<= 24.4 V with the overdrive term), and the window needs >= 112 V/ms (54 A into 0.48 mF) against a 1.5 A VESC regen cap. The published OVP_tOFF(dly) <= 14 µs closes the U rather than opening a hazard. No charger kill path, no back-feed, no part-choice change, no shortlist reorder: doc/criterion drift plus a missing U mark in 0.1, capped at minor.
- Minimal fix: Section 7, condition 1 (target :1050-1053), replace with: "**R-M2.** The operator accepts the protected-standoff reading of M2. Its basis is the TPS26630 OVP cutoff 22.61-23.63 V and OVP switch turn-off delay OVP_tOFF(dly) 8.5-14 µs (SLVSE94G §6.5 p.6, §6.6 p.8): at the 4.30 V/ms regen slope ([EVAL] section 2.5) the eFuse output holds at most 0.06 V above the cutoff, so the BQ25798 pins stay at or below 23.7 V, under the 24 V recommended VBUS maximum and the 25.2 V VBUS_OVP minimum (SLUSDV2C §6.3 p.7, §6.5 p.9). Bench confirmation at the CCB, both with VCHG-IN, U101 OUT and the BQ25798 VBUS pin scoped: (a) a REGEN close at 21.5 V, pass < 24.0 V at the VBUS pin; (b) a bench-supply ramp into VCHG-IN at >= 4.3 V/ms through 22.6-23.6 V, pass: U101 OUT trips inside the band and holds < 24.0 V, VBUS_OVP_STAT stays 0. A hold level between 24.0 V and 25.2 V is outside the recommended range without a VBUS_OVP trip and needs an operator ruling. If the operator requires 35 V at the IC pins, the BQ25798 is not eligible, and the rule of succession below applies (section 6.4, statement 5)." Proof of the 24.0 V limit: 23.63 V (band max) + 4.30 V/ms x 14 µs = 23.69 V < 24.0 V (SLUSDV2C §6.3) < 25.2 V (SLUSDV2C §6.5); test (b) reaches the band because 22.6-23.6 V > the 21.5 V the REGEN close provides. Section 0.1 (target :86-87): append after "scored separately": "(the eFuse OVP cutoff 22.61-23.63 V and turn-off delay <= 14 µs, SLVSE94G p.6 and p.8, hold the IC pins under the 24 V operating maximum; bench confirmation in section 7)"; if the document keeps SLVSE94G as unread, instead append "(eFuse OVP response TODO(verify: SLVSE94G))" so 0.1 matches 1.3 and 7. Section 1.3 (:188-189) and Table 8.3 item 1 (:1109): close the TODO with "OVP_tOFF(dly) 8.5/11/14 µs, SLVSE94G §6.6 p.8; URL confirmed". Apply the same wording to scoring.md:510-513 and dossier_BQ25798.md:134-135. Follow-on skill class: test (bench procedure addition to the T-C-04 class); no pinmap, protocol, coefficient or firmware change.
- Adjacent findings: The target's open item Table 8.3 row 1 (:1109) and dossier_BQ25798.md:565 say SLVSE94G was 'not fetched', but [WD]:906 already cites SLVSE94G §6.6 for the 1 µs hot-short response, the same timing table that carries OVP_tOFF(dly) 8.5/11/14 µs; the U could have been closed from the repo's own citation.; SLVSE94G specifies OVP_tOFF(dly) from 20 mV above V(OVPR) at the OVP pin, which is 0.385 V input-referred through the 845k/66.5k/49.9k divider; a conservative bound that adds this term gives 24.07 V at the 4.30 V/ms slope, 0.07 V above the 24 V recommended maximum, so the R-M2 basis should state which reading it uses.; The finding's premise that VBUS_OVP recovery 'is a re-qualification' is not supported by SLUSDV2C §7.3.13.1.2 (auto-restart of switching); re-qualification is stated only for an OVP present during the §7.3.4.2 qualification step. dossier_BQ25798.md:184 ('Auto-restart below 24.0-24.8 V') and :227 (B, S1-B2) already state this correctly.; With a failed TL431/BSP170P chopper the V-MOT node rises until the RT1987 D-BC-RG OVP (33 V min) or D107 (VBR 36.7 V); the eFuse OVP removes the charger at 22.6-23.6 V, so the exposed elements are the MB-side C-D8/C-D10 (35 V) and the pin 38/39 dividers, not the BQ25798 ([WD]:902-903, J.2). The target's M2 discussion does not mention that the IC is the protected element while the motherboard end is not.; BQ25798 VAC_OVP register POR is 7 V (6.7-7.3 V rising, SLUSDV2C p.9) and the register image writes 0h = 26 V ([WD]:1973); with VAC1/VAC2 tied to VBUS the effective OVP floor after the image is min(VAC_OVP 25.2 V, VBUS_OVP 25.2 V) = 25.2 V, consistent with the 25.2 V tier used above; the pre-image 7 V tier is panel item P5, unchanged.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): The verifier's OVP_tOFF(dly) bound closes the U rather than opening a hazard: at the 4.30 V/ms design slope the eFuse output holds at most 23.63 + 0.06 = 23.69 V, <= 24.0 V at the 12 A motor-command bound, and reaching the 25.2 V VBUS_OVP minimum needs >= 112 V/ms (54 A into 0.48 mF) against a 1.5 A VESC regen cap. The two real defects stand: the 30 V pass limit contradicts the <= 24 V operating premise, and a 21.5 V REGEN close sits 1.11 V below the eFuse band so it never exercises the OVP the ruling depends on. Minor; the rewritten condition 1 with test (b) (a >= 4.3 V/ms ramp through 22.6-23.6 V) and the 0.1/1.3/8.3 TODO closure accepted.

### CIS-R1-20 (decision,coverage; reviewer minor; final minor; accepted)

- Location: charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:88-89 (S1-B4 condition in 0.1) vs :1054-1056 (section 7 condition 2)
- Claim: 0.1: "If the BQ25798 warm-response bench gate fails, the BQ25798 fails M9, and the margin of every finalist except the RAA489110 exceeds 10 points (scenario A2)." Section 7 adds "while a challenger passes its equivalent gate".
- Problem: The two statements of the same condition differ in the clause that matters. Scenario A2 sets S1 = 0 for the BQ25798 only and keeps S1 = 1 for untested challengers on U/B evidence, so it presumes they pass. [SCO] section 4 states that "The BQ25798 and the BQ25773 stop the converter with a REGN-biased hold-down on ILIM_HIZ", the same mechanism; a BQ25798 S1-B4 failure is therefore at least as consistent with scenario A1 (all TI parts fail, LTC4015 the only part above 10 and still ineligible) as with A2. The BQ25773, first in the rule of succession, also carries a documented HR1 item (tTRANS_CHG 5 ms > 4 ms), so the 0.1 wording reads as an automatic switch to a part with a worse known warm-path fact. Citing the A2 margin is also moot by the document's own statement 1: once the baseline fails M9 the margin no longer decides anything.
- Reviewer recommendation: Use the section 7 wording (with its qualifier) in 0.1, note that A2 assumes the challenger's gate passes, drop the A2 margin figure from the condition, and name A1 as the alternative outcome of the same gate failure.
- Verdict: PARTLY_CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: Target line 88-89 (0.1, S1-B4) states the condition without the qualifier: "If the BQ25798 warm-response bench gate fails, the BQ25798 fails M9, and the margin of every finalist except the RAA489110 exceeds 10 points (scenario A2)". Section 7 condition 2 (lines 1054-1056) adds "while a challenger passes its equivalent gate". Scenario table lines 945-946 matches [SCO] lines 364-365. A2 keeps S1 for the challengers unchanged, so it does assume they pass. A1 (all three TI parts S1 = 0) gives BQ25773 44.0 (+8.7), BQ25690 43.3 (+8.0), LTC4015 48.7 (+13.3), so the LTC4015 is the only part above 10 points, and it fails M3 and M5 and needs R-M3, R-BR and R-INH. [SCO] line 199 states the same REGN-biased hold-down for BQ25798 and BQ25773, so a BQ25798 S1-B4 failure is plausibly shared with the BQ25773. The wording drift between 0.1 and section 7 is real. The overstated parts are these. First, the document does name the A1 outcome: lines 961-966 (statement 2), 1064-1065 and [SCO] 383-385 say the LTC4015 leads only if the TI warm response fails for all three TI parts. Second, the document already says A2 is "no longer a scoring question" (lines 962-963, [SCO] 388-390). Third, the 0.1 text points on to the rule of succession (lines 91-93), whose BQ25773-first order rests on condition 1 and T-5.HIZ and is not driven by the A2 margin. Fourth, line 1070 already keeps the BQ25773 as the second inhibit mechanism, so there is no automatic switch.
- Quantification: Margins recomputed from the cited tables: A2 gives +13.0 (BQ25690), +13.7 (BQ25773), +13.3 (LTC4015) and +0.3 (RAA489110) against a 10-point threshold. A1 gives +8.0, +8.7, +13.3 and +0.3. The 10-point margin is therefore not met under A1 for any eligible part. The claim that the A2 figure is moot once the baseline fails M9 matches the document's own statement 1.
- Source: Target lines 84-96, 945-946, 959-966, 1048-1071. [SCO] lines 199, 364-365, 380-390. Tier 'working documents' and the target's own prose. No datasheet is needed because the claim is about internal consistency.
- Severity rationale: It is a wording inconsistency between the summary and section 7. The decision, the succession rule and the A1 branch are all stated elsewhere in the document. No exposure path leads to a wrong part recommendation: the recommendation (keep the BQ25798) is unchanged, and any switch is gated on bench results.
- Minimal fix: Replace lines 88-89 with: "S1-B4. If the BQ25798 warm-response bench gate fails while a challenger passes its equivalent gate, the BQ25798 fails M9 and the decision is no longer a scoring question (scenario A2 assumes the challenger passes). If the gate fails for all three TI parts (scenario A1), only the LTC4015 exceeds 10 points, and it stays ineligible without R-M3, R-BR and R-INH." Make the same edit to the S1-B4 row at line 540 of [SCO] only if that file is kept in step. Drop the A2 margin figure from line 1055 if desired. No follow-on skill is triggered (document-only change).
- Adjacent findings: The BQ25773-first succession under a BQ25798 S1-B4 failure rests on a hold-down mechanism that [SCO] line 199 says is shared with the BQ25798, and on a condition 1 pass that sits beside the BQ25773's documented tTRANS_CHG 5 ms > 4 ms HR1 item. The document gives its rationale at lines 1058-1061, but the shared-mechanism correlation is not stated there.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): The margins reproduce (A2 +13.0/+13.7/+13.3/+0.3; A1 +8.0/+8.7/+13.3/+0.3) and the A2 figure is moot once the baseline fails M9 by the document's own statement 1. The defect is a wording inconsistency between 0.1 and section 7 on the 'while a challenger passes its equivalent gate' clause, with the A1 branch stated elsewhere. Minor; the replacement for lines 88-89 accepted.

### CIS-R1-21 (coverage; reviewer minor; final minor; accepted)

- Location: Section 0.1 (l.94-95) versus section 3.3 (l.408-416) and Table 3.1 'Not scorable' row
- Claim: 'The search is closed except for one reserve part: the TI BQ25820'
- Problem: Section 3.3 holds four reserve groups. One of them, the MP2769 (60 V, I2C/PMBus buck-boost controller, 'only MPS part above 35 V with I2C', every must-have U), is as unresolved as the BQ25820, and the SY20776/SY20779 are 'not scorable'. Only the BQ25820 is called reserve in 0.1, which understates the open coverage.
- Reviewer recommendation: Name the MP2769 (and the not-scorable Silergy parts) in the 0.1 closure statement, or give a reason why they cannot change the outcome.
- Verdict: PARTLY_CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: Target l.94-95 states 'The search is closed except for one reserve part: the TI BQ25820'. Target l.408-416 (section 3.3) says 'Four parts or part groups are held in reserve with a named promotion condition ([SCR] section 4.4)'. The four are the BQ25820, the RT9492, the LT3651-8.4 and SC8802, and the MP2769. Table 3.1 l.406 lists 'Not scorable | MP2769 ..., SY20776/SY20779 ...'. screening.md l.85 shows the MP2769 as 'All U' ('Not scorable; manual fetch requested'). screening.md l.201 gives its promotion condition. screening.md l.86 shows SY20776/SY20779 as 'M2 likely F (3.5-25 V operation per snippet, UNVERIFIED)'. So 0.1 is inconsistent with 3.3 on the count of reserve parts. The inconsistency is real for the MP2769. For the Silergy parts the 'open coverage' premise is weak, because the only evidence points to an M2 fail (25 V class). Section 0.1 itself reads 'closed except for one reserve part' as the BQ25820 only. It also limits its justification to the BQ25820. That is narrower than 3.3, which lists the MP2769 and, with a different trigger, the RT9492 and the analog-only parts. The RT9492 and the analog-only parts are conditional on a changed requirement or a second-source role, not unresolved evidence.
- Quantification: Not a quantitative finding. Count: 0.1 names 1 reserve item; 3.3 names 4 groups. Of those 4, 2 are evidence-unresolved (BQ25820, MP2769). The other 2 are conditional on an operator requirement relaxation or a second-source role. The Silergy parts are not in the reserve list; they are 'not scorable' with a likely M2 fail.
- Source: Target l.94-95, l.406, l.408-416, l.1118-1120. Working document screening.md l.85-86, l.201, l.251-265. Authority is the working documents; no datasheet was fetched. The MP2769 primary is behind an MPS login, per screening.md.
- Severity rationale: This is an internal inconsistency in a closure statement. I found no exposure path: the MP2769 is 'pre-release' and all items are U. The recommendation does not rest on the MP2769. The finding offers no computed bound on how much an MP2769 pass could change the outcome. Each of the other three reserve groups needs a requirement change before it could displace the recommendation. The MP2769 could only displace it if its datasheet unexpectedly shows a pin-set ceiling and a non-charging POR, so reachability is low but nonzero. That caps this at minor (a statement that overstates closure).
- Minimal fix: Section 0.1, l.94-95. Replace 'The search is closed except for one reserve part: the TI BQ25820 (85 V buck controller with I2C) is held at M9 U, because the figure that would reject it is illegible (section 3.3, Table 8.1 item 13).' with: 'The search is closed except for two evidence-unresolved reserve parts: the TI BQ25820 (85 V buck controller with I2C), held at M9 U because the figure that would reject it is illegible (section 3.3, Table 8.1 item 13), and the MPS MP2769 (60 V I2C buck-boost controller), whose primary datasheet is behind an MPS login so every must-have is U (section 3.3). Two further reserve groups (the RT9492 second source; the LT3651-8.4 and SC8802 analog-only option) apply only if the operator changes a requirement. The SY20776/SY20779 are not scorable but are likely M2 fails (25 V class, UNVERIFIED) and are not held in reserve.' Alternatively, use 'one reserve part' only if 3.3 is changed to match.
- Adjacent findings: Section 3.3 l.408 says four reserve groups, but 3.3 l.406 and 0.1 do not use the same reserve vocabulary: SY20776/SY20779 are 'Not scorable' but never given a promotion condition.; Target l.1118 says the MP2769 is the 'Only MPS part above 35 V with I2C (reserve)'. Neither 0.1 nor Table 3.1 mentions that it is in reserve.; Not checked: whether Table 8.1 gives the MP2769 an item number matching 'item 13' for the BQ25820 or only lists it in the manual-fetch table at l.1118.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): Closure statement understates open coverage (1 reserve named in 0.1 against 4 groups in 3.3, of which 2 are evidence-unresolved). The MP2769 could displace the recommendation only if its login-gated datasheet showed a pin-set ceiling and a non-charging POR, so reachability is low but nonzero. Minor; the replacement sentence for l.94-95 accepted.

### CIS-R1-22 (system; reviewer minor; final minor; accepted)

- Location: docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:135-136 (section 1.1) and section 5.6
- Claim: The FC purge ruling of 2026-11-08 applies: no FC charging during or just before a purge, a preemptive share shift, EMS-requested charging between purges.
- Problem: The same-day second ruling (predictor assumed ideal; an unpredicted purge that collapses the bus is acceptable only if no component is damaged) is not carried, and no candidate is checked against it. Under the CSV-correct topology an FC window has an FC-only bus that collapses at 2.57 V/ms ([CRIT] 4.5). The safe-collapse case for each part (charger POR at bus level, VAC_OVP or poor-source state, eFuse behaviour) is absent. This is a coverage gap; no damage path has been shown.
- Reviewer recommendation: Add the safe-collapse obligation to section 1.1 and give one line per finalist in section 5.6 for an unpredicted collapse during an FC window.
- Verdict: PARTLY_CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: VERDICT: PARTIAL. The omission is real; the "no candidate is checked" half overstates, because the recovery-side obligations of the second ruling are already scored per part under HR4/HR5 and Table 5.2.

EVIDENCE:
- Target C:/Life Ops/School/Thesis/DC-Balancer-charger-search/docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:135-136 carries ruling 1 only ("no FC charging during or just before an H-20 purge, a preemptive share shift ... EMS-requested charging between purges"). A grep of the whole target for "unpredicted|OR-SC|safe collapse|no component" returns no line. The second ruling is absent. CONFIRMED.
- The second ruling exists verbatim: docs/battery_isolation_feasibility_2026-11-07.md:79-81 ("An unpredicted purge causing the bus to collapse and the system to shut down without any damage to components is acceptable ... assume [the predictor] works perfectly"), applied as OR-SC at :90-92 (no damage on the collapse, on the FC recovery or at the next power-up; pack not left in a hazardous configuration; no ride-through designed) and :325-326.
- Target section 5.6 (:771-801) covers only the PREDICTED case: item 2 (:779-780) has the preemptive share shift re-closing BT_BUS before the purge, and the conclusion (:783-785) "No part takes a POR or a cold start per purge in Run" follows only from that.
