Table 5.2 (:790-798) gives the per-part cold re-entry cost but captions it "FC window with D-BC-RG open (outside Run)". For an UNPREDICTED purge BT_BUS is LOW, the FC-only bus collapses, and VCHG-IN follows it whether fed by D-BC-FC (CSV topology) or by D-BC-RG via V-MOT at bus level (target 5.6 item 1). So the unpredicted case is uncovered in 5.6, and Table 5.2 applies to it but does not say so. CONFIRMED.
- Per-part post-collapse behaviour IS already in the target: Table 1.1 HR4 "the ceiling holds after a charger POR" (:113) and HR5 "no autonomous charge out of POR or from stored settings" (:114); scored per part at :853 (HR4 FC bus draw after a charger POR or register loss: 1.15 / 1.10 / 1.39 / about 1.15 / 0.20 A) and :855 (HR5), reproduced from docs/reviews/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10/scoring.md:168-170. The "no candidate is checked against it" half is therefore overstated: two of the three OR-SC obligations (recovery, next power-up) are checked; the collapse-phase (during) behaviour and the "no damage" statement are not. PARTIAL.
- Topology: references/Scale Car Teensy IO - IO.csv:24 (row 31) "BT_BUS_ENABLE and REGEN_ENABLE *must* be turned off before this is turned on". docs/charger_replacement_evaluation_2026-11-05.md:59-62 (ruling 7) says the row-31 REGEN half "is to be amended (pending documentation change, not yet made)". Either way BT_BUS is LOW in an FC window, so the bus is FC-only and collapses on an unpredicted purge; the finding's premise holds under both topologies.
- Collapse rate source: docs/charger_replacement_evaluation_2026-11-05.md:611-612 ("I_AUX/C_VBUS (2.57 V/ms, campaign G) and UV_BUS (20 ms dwell) latches inside a 100-200 ms purge"); constants tools/hil_plant_sim.py:516 (I_AUX_A = 0.09) and tools/hil_electrical.py:456 (C_VBUS = 35e-6).
- Damage path: none found by me and none shown by the finding. Pack-to-charger reverse current is blocked by the RT1987 D-BT-SQ (target :122); BIF :192-197 examined eleven damage mechanisms on the collapse grid (Death-5 hot-plug on FC recovery, TPS61288 body-diode back-feed, RT1987 stress, pack left in charge mode, next power-up) and found no path, with "no damage" UNCONFIRMED until bench gate W2c. The charger-specific residue is the post-POR behaviour, already in HR4/HR5.
- Quantification: Collapse rate recomputed from the HIL plant constants: I_AUX_A / C_VBUS = 0.09 A / 35e-6 F = 2571 V/s = 2.57 V/ms, matching [EVAL] 3.4 item 1 and [CRIT] 4.5. This is the aux-only rate (charger stopped). While the charger still draws its FC input ceiling (IINDPM 0.90 A, [EVAL] 3.4), the initial slope is (0.09 + 0.90) / 35e-6 = 28.3 V/ms, so 16.0 V reaches the BQ25798 FC VINDPM 13.5 V in (16.0 - 13.5)/28.3 = 0.09 ms, not the 0.97 ms the aux-only rate gives (and the LTC4015 14.04 V threshold in 0.07 ms, not the 0.76 ms at target :721). After the charger stops, 13.5 V to the 12.0 V UV_BUS threshold takes 1.5/2.57 = 0.58 ms; with the 20 ms dwell the State-99 latch lands about 21 ms after onset, inside a 100-140 ms purge (BIF :87). Pack-side exposure on the collapse: zero reverse current (D-BT-SQ blocks). Post-POR bus draw per part is already bounded in the target (:853): 1.15 / 1.10 / 1.39 / about 1.15 / 0.20 A against the 1.20 A arm drop and 1.4 A OC_FC. No damage bound is exceeded by any finalist; no number in the recommendation (40.3 vs 49.0 / 48.3 / 48.7 / 35.7) moves.
- Source: Settled by: (3) IO CSV row 31 (references/Scale Car Teensy IO - IO.csv:24) for the FC-window topology; (6/7-class design documents) docs/battery_isolation_feasibility_2026-11-07.md:79-92 for the verbatim second ruling and its OR-SC application; docs/charger_replacement_evaluation_2026-11-05.md:59-62 (ruling 7, CSV amendment pending) and :611-612 (2.57 V/ms); tools/hil_plant_sim.py:516 and tools/hil_electrical.py:456 for the recompute; working documents scoring.md:168-170 and the target's own Table 5.2 / :853-855 for the per-part post-POR coverage. No datasheet fetch was needed: the finding is a coverage claim, and every per-part number cited already carries a datasheet reference in the dossiers.
- Severity rationale: Severity requires a computed bound, reachability or an exposure path. The exposure class that could apply (charger/converter kill path or back-feed during an unpredicted collapse) has no path: pack-to-charger reverse current is blocked by D-BT-SQ, the input draw stops at each part's threshold or buck dropout, the bus latches UV_BUS about 21 ms after onset, and the BIF's eleven-mechanism check found no damage path (UNCONFIRMED until gate W2c, but on the Ag105 design, and the charger-specific residue is post-POR behaviour already scored per part under HR4/HR5). The finding itself concedes no damage path. What remains is a document gap: one of two same-day operator rulings is not carried, and section 5.6 states "No part takes a POR or a cold start per purge in Run" without the predicted-purge qualifier, so a reader could take the unpredicted case as covered. That is a superseded/incomplete ruling statement and a missing hedge: minor. It does not reach major because no published number is wrong and the operator would make the same part decision.
- Minimal fix: Section 1.1, replace the bullet at :135-136 with: "The two FC purge rulings of 2026-11-08 apply ([BIF] section 0.1). Ruling 1: no FC charging during or just before an H-20 purge, a preemptive share shift to the battery, and EMS-requested charging between purges. Ruling 2 (OR-SC): the purge predictor is taken as ideal; an unpredicted purge may collapse the bus and latch State 99, provided that no component is damaged on the collapse, on the fuel-cell recovery or at the next power-up, and the pack is not left in a hazardous configuration. The FC-mode input budget is about 1.0 A bus."

Section 5.6, change :783-785 to begin "Under this assumption a PREDICTED purge in Run costs every part the same thing: ...", and add after :785: "An unpredicted purge in an FC window (ruling 2) falls outside the assumption: BT_BUS_ENABLE is LOW, the FC-only bus falls at about 2.57 V/ms once the charger has stopped (I_AUX 0.09 A / C_VBUS 35 uF; about 28 V/ms while the charger still draws its 0.90 A FC ceiling), UV_BUS (12.0 V, 20 ms dwell) latches State 99 about 21 ms after the onset, and VCHG-IN follows the bus through D-BC-RG and MOT_PWR. The collapse is bounded identically for every part: D-BT-SQ blocks pack-to-charger reverse current, the input threshold or the buck dropout stops the input draw, and the eFuse sees a falling input only. Each part then pays the Table 5.2 cold re-entry cost, and its behaviour on the fuel-cell recovery is the HR4 'after a charger POR' row and the HR5 row of Table 6.x: BQ25798 VAC_OVP returns to its 7 V POR default and P5 stays LOW until the image is re-verified (1.15 A bus draw bound); BQ25690 ICHG strap fault clears EN_CHG (1.10 A); BQ25773 CHARGE_CURRENT 0 and VINDPM 3.2 V, but IIN_HOST 5 A leaves 1.39 A to the eFuse (HR4 C); LTC4015 2-4 s battery detection, design A 1.80 A hardware bound; RAA489110 ACLIM reload to 53 mA, 0.20 A. No damage path is identified for any finalist; 'no damage' stays UNCONFIRMED until a CCB equivalent of [BIF] gate W2c is run." Re-caption Table 5.2: "Per-purge cost of a cold re-entry (FC window with D-BC-RG open, or any unpredicted collapse in Run)."

Proof: every per-part figure in the added text is lifted unchanged from the target's own :794-798 and :853 (sourced to the dossiers); the rates are recomputed above from tools/hil_plant_sim.py:516 and tools/hil_electrical.py:456; the ruling text is the verbatim BIF :79-81 / :90-92. No score, margin or shortlist entry changes. Fix class: documentation edit; no follow-on skill (no test, pin map, protocol or coefficient change); the W2c-equivalent bench gate is already in the BIF's gate order.
- Adjacent findings: Section 5.6 item 1 (:777-778, REGEN held closed through Run) rests on [EVAL] ruling 7 (:59-62), whose IO CSV row-31 amendment is 'pending documentation change, not yet made'; the CSV (authority 1, references/Scale Car Teensy IO - IO.csv:24) still requires REGEN_ENABLE off before FC_CHARGE_ENABLE on. The target cites the row-31 guard for its BT_BUS half (:779) while silently dropping its REGEN half; it should carry the 'pending' hedge. Minor.; The 2.57 V/ms figure (target :721, [CRIT] 4.5 :229) is the aux-only rate (I_AUX_A 0.09 A era); during an FC window the charger's own 0.90 A input ceiling makes the initial slope about 28 V/ms until its threshold, so the LTC4015 '0.76 ms to 14.04 V' at :721 is about 10x too long while the charger is drawing. Minor; direction of the conclusion (threshold is a bus-collapse guard only) is unchanged and strengthened.; Table 5.2 caption (:790) 'FC window with D-BC-RG open' understates its scope: with REGEN closed and MOT_PWR closed, VCHG-IN follows a collapsing bus in Run too, so the table is also the per-part cost of any unpredicted collapse. Minor caption fix, included in the minimal fix above.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): The recomputed collapse (28.3 V/ms while the charger still draws its 0.90 A FC ceiling, 2.57 V/ms aux-only after it stops, UV_BUS latch about 21 ms after onset inside a 100-140 ms purge) shows no damage bound exceeded by any finalist and no pack-side reverse path (D-BT-SQ blocks). The gap is that ruling 2 (OR-SC) is not carried and 5.6 lacks the 'predicted' qualifier. Minor; the section 1.1 and 5.6 additions accepted, including the useful correction that the LTC4015 reaches its 14.04 V threshold in 0.07 ms under charger load, not the 0.76 ms at :721.

### CIS-R1-23 (system; reviewer minor; final minor; accepted)

- Location: docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:184-190 (section 1.3 protected-standoff reading); 1.1 line 122
- Claim: M2 is met at the CCB input when the TPS26630 eFuse (OVP cut-off 22.61-23.63 V) is the 35 V element and the IC operating range covers 22.28 V without an OVP drop-out.
- Problem: The document does not state two consequences of the reading. (1) The eFuse OVP minimum of 22.61 V sits only 0.33 V (1.5 %) above the 22.28 V clamp maximum. An eFuse trip during regen collapses VCHG-IN and forces the charger through a cold start, which M9 forbids: for the BQ25798, REGN about 150 ms plus the VAC_OVP re-write. (2) The lever [EVAL] gives for a failed tau (line 575: raise the VESC cut-off to about 23-24 V) is no longer available for the BQ25798, because V-MOT would then cross the eFuse OVP band and the 24 V operating limit of the IC. That lever is one of the remedies for the S1-B4 failure that is the document's other recommendation condition. Neither coupling appears in R-M2's 'Effect' column (Table 8.1).
- Reviewer recommendation: Add both consequences to the R-M2 row and to section 1.3: the 0.33 V OVP margin as a trigger for a cold restart, and the VESC cut-off held below 22.6 V for the BQ25798 under the protected reading.
- Verdict: CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: Target omission (load-bearing): C:/Life Ops/School/Thesis/DC-Balancer-charger-search/docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:184-190 states the protected-standoff reading and hedges only the IC-protection side ("TPS26630 OVP response time and output overshoot ... An overshoot above 30 V"); :1050-1053 (condition 1) and :1081 (Table 8.1 R-M2 Effect) carry the same IC-protection framing only. A grep of the target for "lever", "VESC cut", "l_max_vin", "0.33 V", "cold restart", "nuisance" returns only line 186 (eFuse introduction); neither coupling is stated anywhere in the target.

Both couplings already exist in the prior/working documents and were not carried over: charger_bq25798_wiring_design_2026-11-05.md:879 ("OVP 23.12 V (22.61-23.63 V): 0.33 V above the 22.28 V design clamp, 0.37 V below the BQ25798 recommended VBUS maximum 24 V"), :2145 (H.5 row, same figures), :2185 (monotone stack "VESC cut 21.35 V < clamp 21.98 V < eFuse OVP 22.61 V"), :884 ("peak unmeasured; the U101 OVP removes the charger load at 22.6-23.6 V"), :2306 (T-E-03 fail branch: "Raise l_max_vin (<= 23 V with 35 V caps; the eFuse OVP band 22.6-23.6 V then needs re-selection)"), :163/:225 (any V_chg collapse-and-return is handled as a charger POR, FR-30). The lever itself: charger_replacement_evaluation_2026-11-05.md:575 ("raise the VESC cut-off to about 23-24 V ... BQ25798 VBUS recommended max 24 V, so <= 23 V preferred"); the target inherits that section by reference at :110 (HR1 "tau budget of [EVAL] section 3.2").

Cold-start cost stated by the target itself: :570 (REGN about 150 ms after VBUS_PRESENT) and :794 (cold-start row: 150 ms + about 30 ms poor-source check); :131 ("A cold start (65-170 ms) never captures a burst"); :178 (M9).

Primary datasheet (SLVSE94G, revised June 2024, fetched to scratch, pages 6, 8, 9): V(OVPR) 1.176/1.200/1.224 V, V(OVPF) 1.09/1.122/1.15 V; OVP_tOFF(dly) 8.5-14 us; OVP_ton(dly) 150 + 49.5 x C(dVdT) us; t(dVdT) 8.35 ms at 22 nF / 24 V; OVP cut-off is non-latching (recovers when the OVP pin falls below V(OVPF)); MODE affects only the overload response (p.8). Divider inputs: [WD]:879 (845k / 66.5k / 49.9k, 0.1 %); PGTH tied to GND: [WD]:1063.

Clamp and measured peaks: charger_replacement_evaluation_2026-11-05.md:697 (V-MOT 21.2-21.5 V measured; clamp 21.98-22.28 V), :535 (peaks below the 22.13 V design clamp), :108 (VESC stops at about 21.35 V within 19-47 ms).
- Quantification: OVP band recomputed from the [WD] divider and SLVSE94G p.6: tap ratio 49.9 / (845 + 66.5 + 49.9) = 0.05190; nominal 1.200 / 0.05190 = 23.12 V; with V(OVPR) 1.176-1.224 V and 0.1 % resistors the band is 22.615-23.627 V, which reproduces the stated 22.61-23.63 V (UVLO check: 1.200 x 961.4 / 116.4 = 9.91 V, also reproduces).

Margins: OVP min 22.61 - clamp max 22.28 = 0.33 V = 1.48 % (the finding's 1.5 % holds); OVP min - measured peak 21.5 V = 1.11 V (5.2 %); OVP min - VESC cut 21.35 V = 1.26 V. Recovery threshold at the input: V(OVPF) 1.09-1.15 V / 0.05190 = 21.0-22.16 V (typ 21.62 V), a band that overlaps the VESC cut and the measured peaks, so an opened eFuse stays open for as long as the chopper holds V-MOT above about 21.6 V.

Trip-to-charging timeline after a V-MOT excursion above 22.61 V lasting more than 8.5-14 us: eFuse FET off within 14 us; the IC-side capacitance (about 10 uF of charger ceramics) at a 1.5 A input draw collapses at I/C = 0.15 V/us, i.e. VBUS is below the BQ25798 VBUS_PRESENT level within about 0.15 ms; re-close only after V-MOT falls below 21.0-22.2 V, then OVP_ton(dly) = 150 + 49.5 x 22 = 1239 us, then the dVdT output ramp about 8.35 x (21.6 / 24) = 7.5 ms, then the charger input qualification about 150 ms + 30 ms (target :794). Total about 190 ms, against the 4 ms tau budget and the 19-47 ms VESC window: one eFuse trip forfeits the burst and any burst within the next about 0.2 s (M9 class, by the target's own M9 definition at :178).

Lever bound: with the fitted divider the VESC cut-off must stay below 22.61 V (the finding's "below 22.6 V" holds). With any re-selected divider whose OVP maximum stays at or below the BQ25798 24 V operating limit: nominal <= 24.0 / 1.0219 = 23.49 V, hence OVP minimum <= 23.49 x 0.9781 = 22.97 V, so the cut-off can rise at most to about 22.9 V (+1.5 V over today's 21.35 V). The [EVAL] 23-24 V lever is therefore unreachable for the BQ25798 under the protected reading; a reduced lever (<= about 22.9 V) survives, which the finding does not mention. A 23 V cut-off exceeds the fitted OVP minimum by 0.39 V.

Reachability of the trip: requires V-MOT >= 22.61 V, 0.33 V above the design clamp maximum; the V-MOT peak above the VESC cut is recorded as unmeasured ([WD]:884), and in normal regen the charger holds V-MOT at VINDPM 18.6-19.7 V (2.9 V of margin), so the trip is reachable only when the charger is not absorbing (inhibit, ceiling, H1 drop) and the 0.46 A chopper saturates; the regen bus current at the defining 18.9 W burst is about 0.9 A at 21 V, above the chopper's 0.46 A, so the clamp can be exceeded in that state. Not excluded, not demonstrated.
- Source: Primary datasheet TI SLVSE94G (TPS2663, rev. June 2024) p.6 EC (V(OVPR), V(OVPF)), p.8 timing (OVP_tOFF(dly), OVP_ton(dly)), p.9 (t(dVdT)); repo design documents [WD] charger_bq25798_wiring_design_2026-11-05.md B.4 row 879, H.5 row 2145, stack row 2185, T-E-03 row 2306; [EVAL] charger_replacement_evaluation_2026-11-05.md lines 108, 535, 575, 697; the target's own lines 110, 131, 178, 184-190, 566, 570, 794, 1050-1053, 1081. [SCO] section 2.1 and [D798] lines 119, 133-134 confirm the working documents carry the same IC-protection-only framing as the target.
- Severity rationale: No published number in the target is wrong: "no OVP drop-out at or below 22.28 V" holds (22.61 V > 22.28 V), and the 22.61-23.63 V band recomputes from the datasheet. No hardware-kill or back-feed path is created: an eFuse OVP trip is protective, and the V-MOT side above the trip is already covered by the [WD] r4/r5 RT1987-OVP and D107 analysis. The exposure is (a) a harvest loss of one burst plus about 0.2 s (M9 class) on an event that needs V-MOT to exceed the design clamp maximum by 0.33 V with the peak unmeasured, and (b) an undocumented cost of ruling R-M2: the eFuse band caps the VESC cut-off below 22.61 V as fitted and below about 22.9 V for the BQ25798 in any case, so the [EVAL] 23-24 V lever is unavailable. Both are documented in [WD] (B.4, H.5, T-E-03) and absent from the target, which is doc drift. The recommendation does not invoke the lever (S1-B4 failure leads directly to the rule of succession), and the eFuse is system core shared by every candidate, so the ordering is unchanged. A decision-relevant omission for R-M2, but without a wrong number, a kill path, or an order change it caps at minor.
- Minimal fix: Section 1.3, append after "...covers 22.28 V without an OVP drop-out." (line 187): "The eFuse OVP minimum, 22.61 V, is 0.33 V (1.5 %) above the 22.28 V clamp maximum ([WD] B.4, H.5). A V-MOT excursion above 22.61 V for more than 8.5-14 us opens the eFuse (SLVSE94G p.8). VCHG-IN then collapses, and the charger re-starts cold once V-MOT falls below 21.0-22.2 V: eFuse turn-on about 1.2 ms, dVdT ramp about 7.5 ms (SLVSE94G p.8-9, C(dVdT) 22 nF), then the charger cold start of Table 5.4 (BQ25798 about 150 ms). M9 is therefore met only while V-MOT stays below 22.61 V; the peak above the VESC cut is unmeasured ([WD] T-E-03, [EVAL] M-R0). In addition, the [EVAL] section 3.2 lever 'raise the VESC cut-off to about 23-24 V' is not available under this reading: the cut-off must stay below 22.61 V with the fitted divider, and below about 22.9 V with any divider whose OVP maximum stays under the BQ25798 24 V operating limit ([WD] T-E-03)."

Table 8.1, R-M2 Effect column, append: "Under the protected reading the eFuse OVP band (22.61-23.63 V) bounds the VESC cut-off below 22.61 V as fitted and below about 22.9 V for the BQ25798 in any case, so the [EVAL] section 3.2 cut-off lever (23-24 V) is not available; a V-MOT excursion above 22.61 V costs a charger cold start (section 1.3)."

Proof of the inserted numbers: 22.61 - 22.28 = 0.33 V = 1.48 %; 8.5-14 us = OVP_tOFF(dly) p.8; 21.0-22.2 V = V(OVPF) 1.09-1.15 V / 0.05190; 1.2 ms = 150 + 49.5 x 22 us; 7.5 ms = 8.35 ms x 21.6 / 24; 22.9 V = floor of 24.0 / 1.0219 x 0.9781 = 22.97 V; 150 ms = target line 570/794. Fix class: documentation only; no follow-on skill (no test, pin map, protocol, coefficient or bench-incident change). The reachability measurement is the existing [WD] T-E-03 / [EVAL] M-R0 gate and needs no new gate.
- Adjacent findings: Target line 189, [SCO] 2.1 and [D798] 133-134 keep 'TODO(verify: SLVSE94G) OVP response time' open; SLVSE94G rev. June 2024 p.8 gives OVP_tOFF(dly) 8.5-14 us and OVP_ton(dly) 150 + 49.5 x C(dVdT) us, so the response-time half of the TODO can be closed from the datasheet; only the output overshoot during the 8.5-14 us turn-off remains a measurement item.; Target line 794 lists 're-write of VAC_OVP (POR 7 V)' as part of the BQ25798 cold start after a VCHG-IN collapse; with the pack holding VBAT a VBUS-only loss is not a register POR, so the re-write is the [WD] FR-30 firmware policy (any V_chg collapse-and-return handled as a POR), not an IC requirement. The row should attribute it to FR-30. TODO(verify: SLUSDV2C register-reset conditions); the BQ25798 datasheet was not fetched in this verification.; Prior-document inconsistency inherited by the target: [EVAL] line 575 recommends a VESC cut-off of 23-24 V ('<= 23 V preferred'), while [WD] B.4 sets the eFuse OVP minimum at 22.61 V; [WD] T-E-03 notes the band 'needs re-selection' but not that the BQ25798 24 V operating limit caps the re-selection at an OVP minimum of about 22.97 V.; After an OVP trip the eFuse recovers only below V(OVPF) at the input, 21.0-22.16 V (SLVSE94G p.6), a band overlapping the VESC cut (21.35 V) and the measured peaks (21.2-21.5 V); if the chopper holds V-MOT near 22 V the charger path can stay open for the remainder of the brake. Not stated in [WD] B.4 either.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): Confirmed with the band recomputed (22.615-23.627 V) and the timeline bounded (about 190 ms from an eFuse trip to charging, M9 class). The verifier adds a point I missed: a reduced cut-off lever to about 22.9 V survives for any divider whose OVP maximum stays under the BQ25798 24 V limit, so the [EVAL] 23-24 V lever is unavailable but not every lever is. The trip needs V-MOT 0.33 V above the design clamp maximum with the peak unmeasured ([WD] T-E-03), the recommendation does not invoke the lever, and the eFuse is shared by every candidate. Minor; the 1.3 insertion and the R-M2 Effect column text accepted.

### CIS-R1-24 (system; reviewer minor; final minor; accepted)

- Location: docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:278 (Table 2.2 regen row) vs 535, 547, 847 (Table 5.1, 6.0b)
- Claim: Section 2.4: regen lowest input is 18.13 V (regen VINDPM 18.5 V at -2 %). Section 5/6: HR1 input at the threshold floor uses 18.62 V.
- Problem: Two different regen setpoints are used without reconciliation. 18.62 V is the floor of the 19.0 V setting ([D798] line 224; [EVAL] line 714), while section 2 uses 18.5 V. Section 1.1 allows 18.5-19.5 V. At the 18.5 V setting the BQ25690 HR1 input becomes 1.285 x 18.13 = 23.3 W (+1.3 %, not +3.9 %), with 23.3 x 0.92 / 8.4 = 2.55 A delivered (not 2.62 A). The BQ25798 figure becomes 28.1 W. The buck headroom conclusion is unaffected because 18.13 V is the conservative case.
- Reviewer recommendation: State that the HR1 margins assume the 19.0 V regen setting. Either fix the regen setting at 19.0 V in section 1.1, or carry the 18.5 V figures in Table 5.1.
- Verdict: PARTLY_CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: The underlying observation holds. The target uses two regen thresholds and never reconciles them, and it never states the regen VINDPM value that its HR1 figures assume. Table 2.2 (charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:278) and section 2.6 (:323) use 18.13 V, which is 18.5 V at -2 %. Table 5.1 (:535), the BQ25690 bullet (:547) and Table 6.0b (:847) use 18.62 V, which is 19.0 V at -2 %. Section 1.1 (:132) admits 18.5-19.5 V. A grep of the target for "19.0" finds no statement of the regen setting.

The finding is wrong to call this an inconsistency in substance. Each value is the right one for its own purpose:
- The 18.13 V figure is the H4 range bottom, deliberately chosen as the conservative case for buck headroom (screening_criteria.md:216, "18.5 V at -2 % (H4 range bottom)").
- The 19.0 V figure is the design baseline: [EVAL] register image 0x05 = 0xBE, 19.0 V (charger_replacement_evaluation_2026-11-05.md:747; also :714 and :735).
- dossier_BQ25798.md:224 gives the 19.0 V band as 18.62-19.38 V.
- dossier_BQ25690.md:291, :446-447 and :921 (review finding 19, major, applied) moved the BQ25690 regen VINDPM from 18.5 V to 19.0 V because 18.5 V gives only 0.94 ms of head start, 0.46 ms short. 18.5 V is therefore not an admissible BQ25690 regen setting.

The finding's proposed alternative, carrying 18.5 V figures in Table 5.1, would publish numbers for a configuration that fails the HR1 timing. It should be rejected.

The other finalists use their own regen settings:
- LTC4015 code 0xFF: 18.92 V nominal, 18.69-19.15 V (dossier_LTC4015.md:282).
- RAA489110 code 149: 19.072 V, worst case 18.53 V (dossier_RAA:241).

So the "threshold floor" in Table 5.1 is per part, and the table does not say so.
- Quantification: Values at the stated 19.0 V basis:
- BQ25690: 1.285 x 18.62 = 23.93 W, and 23.93 / 23 = 1.040, so the margin is +4.0 % (the dossier says 4.0 %; the target rounds to +3.9 %). Delivered current: 23.93 x 0.92 / 8.4 = 2.62 A. These match the target.
- BQ25798: 1.55 x 18.62 = 28.86 W, which matches the target's 28.9 W.

The finding's 18.5 V counterfactual is arithmetically right:
- BQ25690: 1.285 x 18.13 = 23.30 W, which is +1.3 %. Delivered current: 23.30 x 0.92 / 8.4 = 2.55 A.
- BQ25798: 1.55 x 18.13 = 28.10 W.

Even at 18.5 V, every part still clears the 23 W pass line, so no HR1 pass/fail changes. The BQ25690 delivered-current shortfall grows from -0.48 A to -0.55 A, but that part already fails the 3.1 A figure. At 18.5 V the BQ25690 head start would be (18.13 - 17.86) / 287 = 0.94 ms, against 2.65 ms at 19.0 V, which is the reason that setting was rejected.

Buck headroom is unaffected: 18.13 - 10.88 = 7.25 V.
- Source: [EVAL] section 4.5 register image (line 747, VINDPM 0xBE = 19.0 V) and line 714; dossier_BQ25690.md sections 6.1 and 8.3 (lines 291, 446-447) and finding 19 (line 921); dossier_BQ25798.md:224; screening_criteria.md:216; dossier_LTC4015.md:282; dossier_RAA:241. No datasheet fetch was needed, because the claim concerns internal consistency and all arithmetic was recomputed above.
- Severity rationale: This is doc drift: the document does not state an assumption it relies on. It is not a wrong number. At the 19.0 V design baseline, every Table 5.1 and Table 6.0b figure recomputes correctly. Even under the finding's counterfactual 18.5 V setting, every part still passes HR1's 23 W input condition (the BQ25690 is the lowest, at 23.30 W). The BQ25690 is already marked "Not met" on delivered current, and nothing scores delivered current. Reachability: 18.5 V cannot occur on the TI parts, because the dossiers fix 19.0 V on head-start grounds. No recommendation, score or shortlist order changes.
- Minimal fix: Section 5.0, after the Table 5.1 lead-in at line 527, add this sentence: "The regen threshold floor is the low tolerance corner of each part's regen setting: 18.62 V for the BQ25798, BQ25690 and BQ25773 (VINDPM 19.0 V at -2 %, [EVAL] section 4.5), 18.69 V for the LTC4015 (code 0xFF) and 18.53 V for the RAA489110 (code 149). The 18.13 V of Table 2.2 is the bottom of the H4 range and is used only for buck headroom."

Table 2.2 regen row (line 278): change "Regen VINDPM 18.5 V at -2 %" to "Regen VINDPM 18.5 V at -2 % (bottom of the H4 range, conservative for headroom; the design setting is 19.0 V, section 5.0)".

Optionally, change "+3.9 %" to "+4.0 %" at lines 535 and 847 (23.93 / 23 = 1.040).

Do not carry 18.5 V figures in Table 5.1: [D690] section 8.3 shows that setting fails the head start by 0.46 ms. This is a documentation fix only; it triggers no follow-on skill.
- Adjacent findings: Target lines 535 and 847 state the BQ25690 HR1 margin as +3.9 %. The recomputed value is 1.285 x 18.62 / 23 = 1.0403, so +4.0 %, and dossier_BQ25690.md:402 already says 4.0 %.; Table 5.1 applies the label 'the threshold floor' to parts whose floors differ: 18.62 V (TI parts), 18.69 V (LTC4015) and 18.53 V (RAA489110). The RAA489110's 18.53 V floor sits below the 18.62 V the other parts use, and the table does not flag this.; The finding's proposed remedy, carrying 18.5 V figures in Table 5.1, conflicts with [D690] finding 19: the BQ25690 head start at 18.5 V is 0.94 ms, which does not fit the timing.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): At the 19.0 V design baseline every Table 5.1 and 6.0b figure recomputes (BQ25690 23.93 W / +4.0 % / 2.62 A; BQ25798 28.86 W); my 18.5 V counterfactual is arithmetically right (23.30 W / +1.3 % / 2.55 A) but that setting is rejected by [D690] 8.3 on head-start grounds (0.94 ms vs 2.65 ms). Every part still clears 23 W. The defect is an unstated assumption, not a wrong number. Minor; the 5.0 basis sentence and the Table 2.2 qualifier accepted, 18.5 V figures not to be carried into Table 5.1.

### CIS-R1-25 (system; reviewer minor; final minor; accepted)

- Location: docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:303-307 (section 2.5) and 328-329 (2.6)
- Claim: A buck converter has a hardware no-charge floor of about 9-11 V in these states that no register write or charger POR can lower; this makes the passthrough-bus circulation physically impossible.
- Problem: The floor tracks the pack voltage; it is not fixed at 9-11 V. With the [CRIT] 4.3 D_max model at a 7.4 V pack it is (7.4+0.32)/0.984 = 7.85 V, and for the LTC4015 (the only buck finalist) the documented floor is VBAT + 0.14-0.25 V (Table 2.2 source, 4015fb p.4). Passthrough is blocked because VIN < VBAT, not because of a 9-11 V figure. In bring-up P0 the bus is max(V_fc, V_batt) minus up to 1.5 V. An FC-led precharge near stack open-circuit voltage (about 12-13 V for 13 cells; UNVERIFIED) exceeds VBAT plus headroom, so in P0 'no charge' rests on FC_CHARGE and REGEN being LOW, not on buck physics. The S16 credit statement is correct only for the passthrough case.
- Reviewer recommendation: Replace '9-11 V' with 'VBAT plus the part's headroom (7.5-10.9 V over the 7.4-8.4 V pack)'. State that P0 safety relies on the path switches.
- Verdict: CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: Target :306-307 says "A buck converter has a hardware no-charge floor of about 9-11 V in these states that no register write or charger POR can lower". The source is [CRIT] screening_criteria.md:233, and that figure comes from [CRIT] 4.3 :197-208. Every model in 4.3 is evaluated at one operating point, VOUT = 8.484 V (full CV) at 3.21 A, and each model has the form VIN >= f(VBAT, I). The floor therefore follows the pack voltage and the current; it is not a fixed 9-11 V. The LTC4015, the only buck finalist, documents a 140-250 mV VIN-BATSENS differential UVLO and stops charging within 100 mV (dossier_LTC4015.md:145 and :718, citing 4015fb p.4 and p.41). That is a floor of VBAT + 0.14-0.25 V. The passthrough circulation is blocked because VIN < VBAT ([CRIT] :228, "Cannot charge: VIN < VBAT"), not because of a 9-11 V figure. So the S16 credit at target :328-329 holds for the passthrough case. P0 is different. [CRIT] :227 gives the bus as max(V_fc, V_batt) minus up to 1.5 V and says FC_CHARGE and REGEN are LOW in Init. The target at :303-304 already says VCHG-IN is "normally unpowered". If the stack sits above about VBAT + 1.65 V during precharge, the precharged bus is above the buck floor. In that case "no charge in P0" depends on the RT1987 path switches, not on buck physics. The same overstatement appears in [CRIT] :229-230 ("stops by physics below about 10.9 V") and [CRIT] :255. For the LTC4015 the physical stop is about 7.5-8.7 V, so between that point and 10.9 V only UVCL, the Teensy and the path switches stop charging.
- Quantification: The buck no-charge floor depends on the pack voltage and the current. LTC4015 differential UVLO: 7.4 + 0.14 = 7.54 V to 7.4 + 0.25 = 7.65 V at a 7.4 V pack, and 8.484 + 0.25 = 8.73 V at CV. [CRIT] D_max model at a 7.4 V pack: (7.4 + 0.32) / 0.984 = 7.85 V; at 8.484 V it is 8.95 V. MP2759 VHDRM model: 7.4 + 2.4 = 9.8 V, up to 8.484 + 2.4 = 10.88 V. Across the 7.4-8.484 V pack range and all the models, the floor spans about 7.5-10.9 V; for the LTC4015 alone it spans 7.54-8.73 V. The quoted "9-11 V" (8.95-10.88 V) holds only at full CV and 3.21 A. Passthrough bus: about V_batt minus one diode drop, below VBAT under every model, so the buck block holds by about 0.3-0.7 V plus the headroom. P0 bus: V_fc minus up to 1.5 V. The bus exceeds the LTC4015 floor whenever V_fc > VBAT + 1.65-1.75 V, that is above about 9.05-10.2 V. The H-20 open-circuit voltage of about 12-13 V for 13 cells is UNVERIFIED (no datasheet value in the repo), so whether P0 reaches that region is not settled.
- Source: [CRIT] screening_criteria.md sections 4.3-4.5 (lines 197-234); dossier_LTC4015.md:145, :718, which cite the LTC4015 datasheet 4015fb p.4 and p.41 (differential UVLO 140-250 mV, falling within 100 mV); target section 2.2 lines 252-262. I recomputed the bounds by hand. I did not re-fetch the primary datasheet; the dossier's citation of 4015fb p.4 is consistent with the finding.
- Severity rationale: Severity is minor: the claim overstates the physics, but nothing in the decision changes. The S16 credit rests on the passthrough case, where VIN < VBAT under every model, so the credit and the scores are unchanged. The LTC4015 (48.7) is already inside the 10-point switching margin and is not recommended, so the ranking does not move. The exposure is a reader treating buck physics as a barrier in P0 or in a purge collapse between about 7.5 V and 10.9 V. In those states the protection is actually the RT1987 path switches (FC_CHARGE and REGEN LOW in Init), UVCL and the Teensy purge inhibit. All of these are already required by HR3, HR5 and the purge ruling, so I cannot show a reachable kill path or a wrong part choice. That caps the finding at doc-accuracy (minor).
- Minimal fix: Target section 2.5, lines 306-307. Replace "A buck converter has a hardware no-charge floor of about 9-11 V in these states that no register write or charger POR can lower." with: "A buck converter cannot charge unless VIN exceeds VBAT plus its headroom, which is about 7.5-10.9 V over the 7.4-8.4 V pack (LTC4015: VBAT + 0.14-0.25 V, 4015fb p.4). No register write or charger POR can lower this floor. The floor blocks the passthrough-bus circulation, because that bus lies below VBAT. In bring-up P0 the precharged bus can exceed this floor, so no charge in P0 relies on FC_CHARGE_ENABLE and REGEN_ENABLE being LOW." Target section 2.6, lines 328-329. Change "for its hardware no-charge floor, which makes the passthrough-bus circulation physically impossible" to "for its hardware no-charge floor (VIN > VBAT plus headroom), which makes the passthrough-bus circulation, where VIN < VBAT, physically impossible". Apply the same edit to [CRIT] :233 and :255, and change "about 10.9 V" in [CRIT] :229-230 to "VBAT plus headroom (LTC4015: about 7.5-8.7 V)". Proof: 7.4 + 0.14 = 7.54 V; 8.484 + 2.4 = 10.88 V. The passthrough bus is about V_batt - 0.4 V, which is below VBAT. This is a documentation-only change and triggers no follow-on skill.
- Adjacent findings: [CRIT] screening_criteria.md:229-230 says a buck stops a purge collapse or a cruise decay 'by physics below about 10.9 V'. For the LTC4015 the physical stop is VBAT + 0.14-0.25 V, about 7.5-8.7 V, so the 8.7-10.9 V band is covered only by UVCL, the Teensy and the RT1987 UVLO.; [CRIT] :227 says a buck in P0 'Cannot charge: VIN <= VBAT + headroom'. This holds only if V_fc stays below VBAT + 1.5 V + headroom. The H-20 open-circuit voltage is UNVERIFIED, so the claim is unsupported as stated.; Target Table 2.3 :318 gives the bring-up and low-bus input range as '5-10 V'. That range assumes the precharged bus stays below about 10 V, which in turn depends on the UNVERIFIED H-20 open-circuit voltage minus 1.5 V.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): Confirmed: the buck no-charge floor tracks VBAT plus headroom (7.5-10.9 V across models and the 7.4-8.484 V pack; LTC4015 alone 7.54-8.73 V) and the '9-11 V' figure holds only at full CV and 3.21 A. Passthrough is blocked because VIN < VBAT under every model, so the S16 credit and scores are unchanged; in P0 and a purge collapse the protection is the RT1987 switches, UVCL and the purge inhibit, all already required by HR3/HR5. Minor; the 2.5/2.6 edits and the [CRIT] mirrors accepted.

### CIS-R1-26 (coverage; reviewer minor; final minor; accepted)

- Location: Section 3.2 (l.376-382); heading structure between l.800 and l.803; section 4 l.487-488
- Claim: 'The screen covered three groups of parts:' followed by four bullets; '### 6.1' follows '### 5.6' with no '## 6.' heading; 'Neither 2026-11-05 rejection rested purely on form-factor reasons at round 2'
- Problem: Writing defects: the count disagrees with the list; the level-2 section 6 heading is missing, so sections 6.1-6.6 hang under 'Finalists'; and 'Neither ... rejection' has no clear referent, since the BQ25773 and LTC4015 were downgrades, not rejections. In addition, the term 'arm drop' (1.20 A) is used repeatedly but never defined.
- Reviewer recommendation: Write 'four groups', add '## 6. Scoring and decision', reword the section 4 sentence as in [RES] section 6 item 4, and define 'arm drop' at first use.
- Verdict: CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: All four sub-claims confirmed against the target. (1) Section 3.2 (l.376) says "three groups", followed by four bullets (l.378, 379, 380-381, 382). (2) The heading structure runs ## 5. Finalists (l.520), ### 5.6 (l.771), then ### 6.1 (l.803). The next level-2 heading is ## 7 (l.1037). No "## 6." heading exists, so 6.1-6.6 sit under 5. (3) Section 4 l.487-488 reads "Neither 2026-11-05 rejection rested purely on form-factor reasons at round 2". The sentence just before it says the BQ25773 and LTC4015 were "reopened because their downgrade rested on the EVM". The table rows give the BQ25773 as "Unchanged reject"-adjacent and the LTC4015 as a "Plausible; round 1 rank 1" downgrade. So "rejection" has no clean referent. [RES] l.324-325 (section 6 item 4) says "No part was rejected in round 2 for a reason that was purely form-factor. The round-1 list holds most of the form-factor rejections." That wording is the proposed fix. (4) "arm drop" first appears at l.71 as "1.20 A arm drop". It recurs at l.579, 629-630, 853, 859, 978, 1084. Section 1.1 (l.102-139) and HR4 (l.113) never define "arm"; the grep shows no definition anywhere in the target.
- Quantification: Not a quantitative finding. Counts: 3 stated versus 4 listed items. Number of level-2 headings between ## 5 (l.520) and ## 7 (l.1037): 0 where ## 6 is expected. "arm drop" occurs 8 times with 0 definitions.
- Source: Target document, read directly. [RES] section 6 item 4 (rescreen_2026-11-05_candidates.md l.324-325) for the replacement wording. The authority order is not engaged, because the finding is about structure and prose only.
- Severity rationale: These are editorial defects: a count mismatch, a missing heading, an ambiguous referent and an undefined term. There is no wrong number, no change to the recommendation and no hardware exposure path. The one reader-facing risk is that the undefined "arm drop" (the 1.20 A FC bus-draw limit that drives the BQ25773 and BQ25798 comparison) may be misread. That is documentation drift, not a computed bound, so the finding caps at minor.
- Minimal fix: (a) l.376: change "three groups" to "four groups". (b) Insert "## 6. Scoring and decision" before l.803, so that 6.1-6.6 sit under it; check that cross-references to "section 6" and "Table 6.x" still resolve. (c) l.487-488: replace the second sentence with "No part was rejected in round 2 for a reason that was purely form-factor; the round-1 list holds most of the form-factor rejections ([RES] section 6)." (d) At first use (l.71), define: "the 1.20 A arm drop (the FC-window bus-draw limit at which the arm drops, HR4 [EVAL] section 1.1)". Confirm the exact definition from [EVAL] or [D773] section 11.4 before inserting it. Follow-on skill: none (documentation only).
- Adjacent findings: The section 4 table l.481 and the l.487 sentence say the LTC4015 was 'reopened as finalist' while the 'Unchanged reject' label is applied elsewhere; the BQ25773 row was not checked, so verify the labels are consistent.; l.800 refers to 'The R6 finding 8 entries of Table 6.6' from inside section 5.6, which is a forward reference that the missing ## 6 heading obscures.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): Editorial defects confirmed by count (3 stated vs 4 listed; 0 level-2 headings between ## 5 and ## 7; 'arm drop' 8 uses, 0 definitions). Minor; the four edits accepted, with the arm-drop definition confirmed against [EVAL] 1.1 / [D773] 11.4 before insertion.

### CIS-R1-27 (coverage; reviewer minor; final minor; accepted)

- Location: Section 4 Table 4.1 row BQ25756 (l.482); [RES] section 3.7
- Claim: BQ25756: 'Screened again; M9 F (section 3.3)'
- Problem: [RES] section 3.7 marks the BQ25756 reopen-possible precisely because the CCB makes the 2026-11-05 R2 options buildable. W2 is an analog warm inhibit holding ILIM_HIZ at about 3.0 V inside the 2.0-3.7 V regulation band; W3 is an ACUV-trip detector. RES asks for a dossier 'only if W2 has a primary basis'. The re-screen rejects the part under reading 2, which concerns a pin-level stop restart, without addressing W2, which is not a stop. The CCB-enabled question that the re-screen existed to answer is left unanswered. The same family now includes the BQ25856-Q1.
- Reviewer recommendation: State whether W2 has a primary basis in SLUSEN5A. If it does not, record W2 as rejected for that reason in Table 4.1.
- Verdict: CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: VERDICT: CONFIRMED. The three premises of the finding hold and the gap is real.

(1) [RES] holds the BQ25756 open on a named condition. rescreen_2026-11-05_candidates.md l.200-212 (section 3.7 "reopen-possible"): "The custom board does make the R2 options W2 (an analog warm inhibit holding ILIM_HIZ at about 3.0 V inside the 2.0-3.7 V input-current-regulation band) and W3 ... buildable ... Re-screen in the wide search; a full dossier only if W2 has a primary basis." [RES] l.319 lists the BQ25756 among parts that "reopen for screening only". W2/W3 are defined in docs/reviews/charger_eval_2026-11-05/round2/dossier_A1.md l.70-73 and l.480-484 (table "Where H1 can act on this IC"), with the bench gate B10 at l.572 ("ILIM_HIZ forced to 3.0 V while warm at VAC 21 V; I_IN < 10 mA; recovery to I_LIM <= 4 ms on release; REGN stays up").

(2) The re-screen rejects under M9 reading 2 and never mentions W2. screening.md l.31-34 (section 1.2 item 2): "A part whose only pin-level inhibit has a documented restart of 10 ms or longer therefore fails M9 ... This reading is the one that rejected the BQ25756 on 2026-11-05". screening.md l.70 (row 9): "M9 F (about 40 ms compensation toggle on every start, SLUSEN5A p.21 ...; /CE to current 145-175 ms typical, R2 dossier A1) | Reject (rejection was IC-intrinsic, RESCREEN section 3.7) | ... the cold restarts remain". Target charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md l.397 (Table 3.1), l.482 (Table 4.1: "Screened again; M9 F (section 3.3)"), l.512 (Table 4.2, evidence class P). A grep of the target, screening.md and scoring.md for "W2", "warm analog", "off-label" in connection with the BQ25756 returns nothing; scoring.md has no BQ25756 row; the target's open-items table (Table 8.1) has no BQ25756 entry. By contrast the analogous condition IS applied to the BQ25820: target l.410 and screening.md l.198 hold the BQ25820 in reserve pending "a primary-backed warm analog inhibit".

(3) W2 is not a pin-level stop. W2 holds ILIM_HIZ between VREF_ILIM_HIZ and VIH_ILIM_HIZ, which per the primary keeps the device out of HIZ and out of the /CE-disabled state; reading 2 therefore does not dispose of it.

(4) The primary basis question, answered from SLUSEN5A (fetched 2026-11-10 from ti.com/lit/ds/symlink/bq25756.pdf, 3,898,676 bytes, "SLUSEN5A - AUGUST 2023 - REVISED AUGUST 2026"; text via pdftotext, page map from the footer markers):
- p.10 EC: VREF_ILIM_HIZ "ILIM_HIZ pin voltage when ILIM_HIZ pin is in regulation" 2.0 V typ; VIH_ILIM_HIZ "ILIM_HIZ input high threshold to enter HIZ mode" 3.7 V MIN. The 2.0-3.7 V band exists in the primary.
- p.29 section 6.3.5.1.1.1: "The device regulates the pin at VREF_ILIM_HIZ. If pin voltage exceeds VREF_ILIM_HIZ, the device enters input current regulation. Entering input current regulation through the pin sets the IAC_DPM_STAT and FLAG bits, and produces an interrupt" and "If ILIM_HIZ pin is pulled above VIH_ILIM_HIZ, the device enters HIZ mode".
- p.28 section 6.3.5.1: "The device then reduces the charge current until the input current falls below the input current limit ... When the charge current is reduced to zero, but the input source is still overloaded ...": the DPM loop range reaches zero charge current.
- p.21 section 6.3.3.2: REGN LDO enabled when "VAC voltage above VVAC_OK and charge is enabled in forward mode"; p.22 section 6.3.3.6: HIZ "disables switching and disables the REGN LDO". W2 keeps charge enabled and stays below VIH, so REGN stays up by the primary's own conditions.
- section 5.1 abs max: ILIM_HIZ -0.3 to 6 V (3.0 V external drive within rating). Pin type "I" (p.3 pin table); the pin is described only as resistor-programmed; no spec covers an externally sourced voltage, the regulation-loop response time, the residual input current at saturation, or whether the converter keeps switching at zero charge current (dossier_A1 l.579-585 "missing_primaries" lists exactly these).
So W2 has a primary basis for its PREMISE (direction of action, no HIZ entry, REGN retained) and none for its PERFORMANCE claim (tau <= 4 ms warm recovery, I_IN < 10 mA). It is UNVERIFIED, not refuted, and the target states neither.

(5) The BQ25856-Q1 claim in the finding is correct and exposes a second error: target l.430 lists "BQ25856" among "TI part numbers that return HTTP 404 ... assumed non-existent, UNVERIFIED". ti.com/lit/ds/symlink/bq25856-q1.pdf returned HTTP 200 (4,348,068 bytes), SLUSFH5A (May 2024, rev Oct 2024), "Automotive ... 1- to 14-Cell Bidirectional Buck-Boost Battery Charge Controller", 85 V abs max (l.371 of its text), VREF_ILIM_HIZ 2.0 V / VIH_ILIM_HIZ 3.7 V (l.606, l.622), the same "toggles the SW node for about 40 ms" (l.1409) and the same ILIM_HIZ regulation sentence (l.1914-1915). SLUSFF4A (BQ25756E, rev Oct 2026) carries identical text (l.1429, l.1923-1924). The bare "bq25856" slug 404s; the -Q1 slug does not.
- Quantification: W2 operating point against the primary: V_hold 3.0 V - VREF_ILIM_HIZ 2.0 V (typ) = +1.0 V above the regulation reference (forces the IAC loop into regulation per p.29); VIH_ILIM_HIZ(min) 3.7 V - 3.0 V = 0.7 V of margin below the HIZ threshold (no maximum is specified for VIH, so the margin can only be larger); abs max 6 V - 3.0 V = 3.0 V. Cold-restart figures recomputed from SLUSEN5A Fig 7-7 (p.66, 40 ms/div, VAC 20 V, VBAT 24 V, ICHG 5 A): /CE falls at about division 2.0, ICHG rises at about division 5.6, delta about 3.6 div x 40 ms = about 144 ms (the documents' "about 145 ms" holds; it is a figure reading of a typical curve, consistent with dossier_A1 l.67). The 40 ms compensation toggle is stated for "At startup" (p.21); the primary does not say it recurs on every /CE enable, which dossier_A1 l.582 lists as a missing primary. No quantitative W2 performance figure (recovery time, residual input current) exists in SLUSEN5A, SLUSFF4A or SLUSFH5A; the HR1 tau <= 4 ms criterion therefore cannot be settled from the primary either way.
- Source: Primary datasheet SLUSEN5A (BQ25756, rev Aug 2026) p.10, p.21, p.22, p.28, p.29, p.66, section 5.1; corroborated by SLUSFF4A (BQ25756E) and SLUSFH5A (BQ25856-Q1), all fetched from ti.com on 2026-11-10 into the scratchpad. Working documents: [RES] section 3.7 l.200-212 and l.319; [SCR] section 1.2 item 2 l.31-34, row 9 l.70, section 4.4 l.198; round-2 dossier_A1.md l.62-74, l.480-484, l.572, l.579-585; [EVAL] l.136, l.352-353. Target l.397, l.410, l.430, l.482, l.512.
- Severity rationale: The defect is documentary: a screening condition the working document explicitly left open ("a full dossier only if W2 has a primary basis") is closed by omission, Table 4.2 attaches evidence class P to an M9 verdict whose load-bearing half ("on every start") the round-2 dossier itself lists as a missing primary, and the identical condition earns the BQ25820 a reserve row while the BQ25756 gets none. The exposure path is "a decision the operator would make differently": whether to commission a BQ25756/BQ25856-Q1 dossier or a B10 bench gate. No computed bound shows the BQ25756 displacing a finalist: even with W2 granted, its M9 would be conditional (as the BQ25690's /CE "typical curve only" is), the >= 80 uF C_IN / H8 eFuse conflict stands, and the part is unscored, so the recommendation (keep BQ25798 under a 10-point margin) is not shown wrong. Under the contract that caps the severity at minor; it escalates only if a dossier later places the part inside the switching margin.
- Minimal fix: Three text changes to charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md; no firmware, pin-map, protocol or coefficient follow-on (document fix only; the item it opens is a bench gate on a BQ25756EVM, dossier_A1 gate B10, not a repo skill).

(a) Table 4.1 row BQ25756 (l.482), replace the "New status" cell with:
"Screened again; M9 F on every pin stop (section 3.3). The [RES] section 3.7 option W2 (ILIM_HIZ held at about 3.0 V, between VREF_ILIM_HIZ 2.0 V typ and VIH_ILIM_HIZ 3.7 V min, SLUSEN5A p.10) has a primary basis for its premise only: SLUSEN5A section 6.3.5.1.1.1 p.29 states that a pin voltage above VREF_ILIM_HIZ enters input-current regulation, section 6.3.5.1 p.28 that DPM reduces the charge current to zero, and sections 6.3.3.2 and 6.3.3.6 (p.21-22) keep REGN enabled while charge is enabled and HIZ is not entered. The recovery time, the residual input current and the pin's tolerance of an external drive are not specified. W2 is UNVERIFIED (dossier A1 bench gate B10: I_IN < 10 mA, recovery <= 4 ms, REGN up). Held in reserve on the same condition as the BQ25820, not rejected on W2."

(b) Section 3.3 reserve list (after l.413), add the bullet:
"- the BQ25756 (with the BQ25756E and the automotive BQ25856-Q1, SLUSFH5A), if the W2 analog warm inhibit passes bench gate B10 on an EVM and an input-capacitance scheme meets its >= 80 uF minimum; until then M9 stands at F on the pin stops and U on W2."

(c) Table 4.2 row l.512, evidence class cell: replace "P" with "P (40 ms toggle at startup, p.21; /CE to current about 144 ms, Fig 7-7 p.66, figure reading); U for recurrence on every /CE start (dossier A1 section 11) and for W2".

Proof: every number in (a)-(c) is read from the cited SLUSEN5A page in the QUANTIFICATION section above (2.0 V, 3.7 V min, 6 V abs max, about 144 ms from Fig 7-7 at 40 ms/div, zero-charge-current DPM statement p.28), and the B10 gate wording is dossier_A1 l.572 verbatim. The wording makes no performance claim the primary does not support.
- Adjacent findings: Target l.430 and [SCR] section 3: 'BQ25856 ... HTTP 404 ... assumed non-existent, UNVERIFIED' is wrong. The part is the BQ25856-Q1 (SLUSFH5A, May 2024 rev Oct 2024); ti.com/lit/ds/symlink/bq25856-q1.pdf returns HTTP 200 (4,348,068 bytes). It is an AEC-Q100 BQ25756 sibling: 85 V abs max, VREF_ILIM_HIZ 2.0 V / VIH_ILIM_HIZ 3.7 V, the same 40 ms compensation toggle and the same ILIM_HIZ regulation sentence. The 404 was a URL-slug miss, not non-existence; the same may apply to other '-Q1' names in that list.; Table 4.2 l.512 labels 'about 40 ms compensation toggle on every start' as evidence class P. SLUSEN5A p.21 says only 'At startup'; whether the toggle recurs on each /CE enable is listed as a missing primary in dossier_A1.md l.582. The 'every start' half is U. The /CE-to-current figure (about 144 ms, Fig 7-7 p.66) is a typical-curve figure reading, correctly flagged as such in [RES] l.308.; Asymmetric treatment: target l.410 / [SCR] l.198 hold the BQ25820 in reserve pending 'a primary-backed warm analog inhibit', the exact condition [RES] section 3.7 posed for the BQ25756, which receives no reserve row. The BQ25756's W2 premise has more primary support (p.29 regulation sentence) than the BQ25820 reserve condition cites.; Citation check passed: the target's 'SLUSEN5A p.21' for the compensation toggle and [RES]'s 'p.10' thresholds are correct against the fetched Aug 2026 revision; the W2-relevant text is unchanged across SLUSEN5A, SLUSFF4A (Oct 2026) and SLUSFH5A, so no revision drift affects this finding.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): Confirmed: the [RES] 3.7 condition ('dossier only if W2 has a primary basis') is closed by omission, W2's premise has a primary basis (pin above VREF_ILIM_HIZ 2.0 V enters input-current regulation, p.29; DPM to zero charge current, p.28; 0.7 V margin below VIH 3.7 V) but no recovery-time or residual-current figure exists in any primary, and Table 4.2 attaches P to an M9 verdict whose 'on every start' half is a listed missing primary. No bound shows the BQ25756 displacing a finalist (M9 at best conditional, >= 80 uF C_IN / H8 conflict, unscored). Minor; the three edits accepted, placing the BQ25756/BQ25856-Q1 in reserve on the same condition as the BQ25820 with gate B10.

### CIS-R1-28 (facts; reviewer minor; final minor; accepted)

- Location: docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:535 and :543-544 (section 5.0)
- Claim: Table 5.1 BQ25690 loss '2.1-2.45 W typical at 3.1 A'; 'M4 still passes for every part, because ... every part can be set to 3.1 A or more within the 2 W class.'
- Problem: [D690] section 11.2 gives 2.48 W at a 20 V input and 2.14 W at 15 V, and states 'H10 (<= 2 W) is met at 3.1 A only below about 13 V input'. That is below every VCHG-IN in Table 2.2 (FC floor 13.23 V for this part). The 2.45 W in Table 5.1 copies an internally inconsistent dossier M4 row. The BQ25798 also exceeds 2 W (2.11 W at 20 V). The sentence that every part is 'within the 2 W class' is therefore false for the BQ25690 by 0.14-0.48 W at every operating input.
- Reviewer recommendation: Quote 2.14-2.48 W. Replace the sentence with: M4 is conditional (loss) for the BQ25798 (+0.11 W) and the BQ25690 (+0.14 to +0.48 W at 15-20 V); the 2 W limit is met only by the three external-FET parts.
- Verdict: CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: The target, docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:535, gives the BQ25690 loss as "2.1-2.45 W typical at 3.1 A (B)". At :543-544 it says "M4 still passes for every part, because M4 is a capability test and every part can be set to 3.1 A or more within the 2 W class." The same table grades both the BQ25798 (:534) and the BQ25690 (:535) M4 as "c (B, loss)". So the table already treats loss as the conditioning item that the sentence denies.

The source analysis is dossier_BQ25690.md:574-576 (section 11.2): "20 V input, 91.3 % -> 2.48 W; 15 V input, 92.4 % -> 2.14 W. H10 (<= 2 W) is met at 3.1 A only below about 13 V input. At ICHG 2.8 A and 20 V ... 2.10 W; at 2.6 A ... 1.87 W."

The dossier summary rows at dossier_BQ25690.md:106 (M4) and :141 (H10) say "2.1-2.45 W". That disagrees with its own section 11.2 value of 2.48 W, and the target copied the summary row.

The criterion is defined at screening_criteria.md:68 (M4: "dissipates <= 2 W at that point") and :41 (H10: "Loss <= 2 W is kept"). The target restates it at :173 ("<= 2 W loss on the CCB").

For the BQ25798, dossier_BQ25798.md:121 and :384 give 2.11 W at 20 V and 750 kHz, which "exceeds by 0.11 W". The target's own :830 lists "M4 (B, loss 2.11 W at 20 V)".

The BQ25690 FC-mode input floor is 13.23 V at target :294, and the FC bus nominal is 16.0 V. The regen input is 18.62-21.5 V. Every operating input is therefore above the roughly 13 V point where H10 holds at 3.1 A.

The external-FET parts are under 2 W: BQ25773 about 1.4 W, LTC4015 B 1.2 W (estimate), RAA489110 1.41 W (C) (target :536-538).
- Quantification: P_out = 3.1 A x 8.4 V = 26.04 W.

BQ25690 loss is P_out x (1/eta - 1):
- At 20 V input, eta 0.913: 26.04 x 0.09529 = 2.481 W.
- At 15 V input, eta 0.924: 26.04 x 0.08225 = 2.142 W.

The correct range is 2.14-2.48 W. The target's 2.45 W upper end is 0.03 W low, and the dossier's own arithmetic gives 2.48 W. The excess over 2 W is +0.14 to +0.48 W, which is 7-24 % over the limit. At 16 V (FC nominal) the loss interpolates to about 2.2 W. H10 needs loss <= 2 W. At eta of about 0.92-0.93 that holds only below about 13 V input, and the BQ25690 never sees an input that low: the FC floor is 13.23 V and the regen input is 18.62-21.5 V.

BQ25798: 2.11 W at 20 V, +0.11 W over the limit. At 15 V it is 1.66 W, which complies.

Net result: the "every part ... within the 2 W class" sentence is false for 2 of the 5 finalists. The table's c grades already encode this.
- Source: I settled this from the working documents, which are the target's own cited sources:
- dossier_BQ25690.md section 11.2 (:574-576), against its summary rows :106 and :141.
- dossier_BQ25798.md :121 and :384.
- screening_criteria.md :41 and :68, which define the 2 W limit as part of M4 and H10.

I recomputed the loss arithmetic from the dossier's efficiency readings. I did not re-read the BQ25690 datasheet efficiency figure (Figure 5-12, SLUSEO-family). The efficiency values are B, figure reading, and the dossier states that inductor DCR changes them.
- Severity rationale: This is prose that contradicts the document's own table and dossiers. The scored grades are already correct: both parts are graded M4 "c (B, loss)" in Table 5.1, [SCO] Table 5 and :830-831. So score.py and the 40.3 / 49.0 / 48.3 / 48.7 / 35.7 totals are not affected, and the recommendation and shortlist order do not change.

The published loss number is off by 0.03 W (2.45 W against 2.48 W). That error is within the B-grade figure-reading uncertainty.

There is no kill path. Overheating at 2.48 W is a thermal-design input that bench gate T-11 (dossier :776) already tests for. The sentence could mislead a reader into thinking the integrated-FET parts meet H10 at 3.1 A. However, every place that drives a decision carries the conditional grade. This caps at minor.
- Minimal fix: Section 5.0, Table 5.1, BQ25690 row, column 2: replace "2.1-2.45 W typical at 3.1 A (B)" with "2.14-2.48 W typical at 3.1 A, 15-20 V input (B)". This is recomputed above: 26.04 W x (1/0.924 - 1) = 2.14 W and 26.04 W x (1/0.913 - 1) = 2.48 W, per [D690] section 11.2.

Replace :543-544 with: "M4 is a capability test. Every part can be set to 3.1 A or more. The 2 W loss limit at 3.1 A is met by the three external-FET parts (BQ25773 about 1.4 W, LTC4015 B 1.2 W estimate, RAA489110 1.41 W). M4 is conditional on loss for the BQ25798 (2.11 W at 20 V, +0.11 W) and the BQ25690 (2.14-2.48 W at 15-20 V, +0.14 to +0.48 W). The shortfall in delivered current is a separate item, and it is graded as an HR1 margin note:"

Follow-on, in the working document (not the target): correct dossier_BQ25690.md:106 and :141 from "2.1-2.45 W" to "2.14-2.48 W". Correct :141's "<= 2 W at ICHG <= 2.8 A" to "<= 2 W at ICHG <= about 2.6 A (20 V)", per :575-576, which gives 2.8 A -> 2.10 W and 2.6 A -> 1.87 W.

This is a doc-only fix. It triggers no follow-on skill, because the scores are unchanged: the M4 grade stays c.
- Adjacent findings: dossier_BQ25690.md:141 says H10 is '<= 2 W at ICHG <= 2.8 A', but its own section 11.2 (:575-576) gives 2.10 W at 2.8 A and 20 V. The compliant cap is about 2.6 A (1.87 W). Any target text that cites a 2.8 A H10-compliant cap inherits this error.; dossier_BQ25798.md:152 says 'ICHG 3.1 A is now available within the 2 W class at 750 kHz', yet :121 and :388 of the same dossier say the limit is exceeded by 0.11 W at 20 V. 'Within the 2 W class' is a loose phrase that conflicts with the <= 2 W criterion at screening_criteria.md:41 and :68. The same wording appears again at target :557 / :1095 ('Proves M4 in the 2 W class').
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): Confirmed: 26.04 W x (1/0.913 - 1) = 2.48 W and x (1/0.924 - 1) = 2.14 W, so the loss range is 2.14-2.48 W (+0.14 to +0.48 W over 2 W, 7-24 %), the BQ25690 never sees an input below 13 V where H10 would hold, and the BQ25798 is also over at 20 V (2.11 W). The 'within the 2 W class' sentence is false for 2 of 5 finalists but the table's c grades already encode it, so no score moves. Minor; the Table 5.1 cell and the :543-544 replacement accepted, with the dossier_BQ25690 :106/:141 mirror.

### CIS-R1-29 (facts; reviewer minor; final minor; accepted)

- Location: docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:546-549 (section 5.0 BQ25690 bullet), :673-674
- Claim: A 1.52 A ceiling minimum scales to about 2.03 A, above the 1.72 A row, so the BQ25690 'cannot meet the delivered 3.1 A ... and the HR4 ceiling at the same time'.
- Problem: The arithmetic is correct: 3.1 x 8.4 / (0.92 x 18.62) = 1.520 A, and 1.520 x 1.715 / 1.285 = 2.029 A, which gives an allowance of about 90 us. However, the conclusion holds only while the ILIM_HIZ pin is the HR4 element. Table 1.2 (line 152) explicitly admits a CCB current-regulator ceiling, which could be narrower than the +-13 % KILIM band at an S3 penalty. In addition, both 1.715 A and 2.03 A lie above the 1.6 A KILIM test point (extrapolated, B), so 'cannot' rests on an extrapolation.
- Reviewer recommendation: Qualify the claim as 'cannot with the IC pin ceiling (KILIM +-13 %, extrapolated above 1.6 A)'. Note the CCB-regulator route and its S3 cost.
- Verdict: PARTLY_CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: Arithmetic reproduced. Target lines 546-549 and 673-674 state the 1.52 A to 2.03 A scaling and call it structural. Dossier_BQ25690.md lines 387-391 and 403 derive the regen ceiling band as 1.285 A (min) / 1.497 A (typ) / 1.715 A (max), and label the maximum as extrapolated above the 1.6 A KILIM test point (B). Dossier line 109 and line 369-371 confirm KILIM is specified only at IIN_MAX = 0.5, 1.0 and 1.6 A. Target Table 1.2 line 152 admits that the HR4 ceiling may come from a CCB current regulator, with the IC route scoring higher. Target line 852 labels the 1.715 A figure (B) in the HR4 table, while line 548 tags the 2.03 A / 1.72 A comparison (C). The reviewer's point about the unqualified word "cannot" is valid but narrow. The "cannot" refers to the BQ25690's IC-pin ceiling, which is the only HR4 route the dossier evaluates (S3 = 3, dossier line 410). A CCB regulator would be a different design with extra parts, not the BQ25690 pin ceiling that was scored.
- Quantification: 3.1 x 8.4 = 26.04 W. 0.92 x 18.62 = 17.130 W. 26.04 / 17.130 = 1.5202 A, so the claimed 1.520 A is correct. Band ratio 1.715 / 1.285 = 1.3346; 1.5202 x 1.3346 = 2.029 A, correct. 2.029 A is above the 1.72 A row by 18 %. Delivered current at the regen ceiling minimum is 1.285 x 18.62 x 0.92 / 8.4 = 2.620 A, a 0.48 A shortfall, consistent with Table 5.1 line 535. The ratio 1.3346 is wider than the KILIM-only ratio 3780 / 2890 = 1.308 because it includes resistor tolerance. Both 1.715 A and 2.029 A exceed the 1.6 A KILIM test point, so the upper-band width is extrapolated (B). The lower end of the band, 1.285 A, lies inside the test range. The 1.52 A requirement lies inside the test range, so the structural shortfall at the minimum is not extrapolated. Only the maximum-side comparison is.
- Source: Dossier_BQ25690.md (datasheet-derived KILIM rows, DS section 5.5 p.9), the target's Table 1.2 line 152, and independent arithmetic. Primary datasheet not re-fetched, because the dossier statement matches the target's own B label.
- Severity rationale: The arithmetic is correct, and the claim is already grounded in the IC-pin HR4 route that the dossier scores. No wrong part recommendation follows. BQ25690 scores 48.3 against 40.3 for the recommended BQ25798 (the recommendation keeps the BQ25798 on other grounds, and the section states this disadvantage is not scored). The only defect is a missing qualifier and a (C) tag where the underlying 1.715 A figure is (B). This is a labeling and hedging issue; it has no computed bound that changes a decision or an exposure path to a board kill, loop or silent wrong number.
- Minimal fix: Replace line 548 sentence: "The BQ25690 therefore cannot meet the delivered 3.1 A at an 8.4 V pack and the HR4 ceiling at the same time." with: "The BQ25690 therefore cannot meet the delivered 3.1 A at an 8.4 V pack and the HR4 ceiling at the same time with the IC pin ceiling (KILIM +-13 %; the 1.715 A and 2.03 A values lie above the 1.6 A KILIM test point and are extrapolated, B). A CCB current regulator narrower than the KILIM band (Table 1.2) could close the gap at an S3 penalty that this document does not score." Change the (C) tag at line 548 to (B), and make the same hedge at lines 673-674 ("the HR4 ceiling row prevents a fix by a higher ceiling with the IC pin"). Pure wording change; no follow-on skill beyond a doc consistency check.
- Adjacent findings: Line 548 tags the 2.03 A vs 1.72 A comparison (C), while line 852 and dossier line 391 label the underlying 1.715 A maximum (B); the tag is internally inconsistent.; Line 80 states the BQ25690 gap 'cannot close without a regen ceiling above the HR4 hard-stop row', which repeats the same unqualified claim and should receive the same hedge.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): The arithmetic I questioned is correct (1.5202 A minimum; x 1.3346 = 2.029 A, 18 % above the 1.72 A row) and the 1.52 A requirement lies inside the KILIM test range, so the structural shortfall at the minimum is not extrapolated; only the maximum-side comparison (1.715 A and 2.03 A above the 1.6 A test point) is B. The missing qualifier is the CCB current-regulator route Table 1.2 admits at an S3 penalty. Minor; the hedged sentence and the (C) -> (B) tag change accepted.

### CIS-R1-30 (facts,system; reviewer minor; final minor; accepted)

- Location: docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:567 (section 5.1 key facts) and :586-589 (section 5.1 principal risks)
- Claim: BQ25798 key facts: 'VINDPM 3.6-22 V ... not reset by REG_RST or the watchdog'; principal risks list no register-defeat path for the ceiling.
- Problem: [D798] section 6 / line 227 records that every plug-in, FORCE_VINDPM_DET and EN_MPPT overwrite the host VINDPM, and that every re-qualification is not excluded (automatic VBUS_OVP retry, 7-minute poor-source retry, EN_HIZ retry), where the write is VBUS0 - 1.4 V, which can fall below the pack in low-input states (B: S1-B2). In the always-powered regen-armed cruise, the 19 V VINDPM above the bus is the HR3 threshold layer; a re-qualification at a ~16 V bus that rewrites VINDPM below the bus removes that layer (H1 and the hard inhibit still bound the loop). [SCO] Table 4 also grades the BQ25798 M7 as conditional on EN_EXTILIM REG14[1], and [D798] M5 marks the EN_EXTILIM interaction B. Section 5.2 lists the register-defeat surface of the BQ25773 and section 5.5 that of the RAA489110, but section 5.1 omits the baseline's and drops the B mark from its source, presenting the baseline more favourably than its dossier does.
- Reviewer recommendation: Add to section 5.1 and to the BQ25798 HR3 condition list: 'VINDPM overwritten at each plug-in; survival across re-qualification B (S1-B2); ILIM ceiling defeatable by REG14[1] EN_EXTILIM (M7 c, read-back required)'.
- Verdict: PARTLY_CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: Primary datasheet (SLUSDV2C, local copy read read-only at C:\Life Ops\School\Thesis\DC-Balancer-charger-custom\references\TI_BQ25798_design_files\BQ25798_SLUSDV2C.pdf): Table 7-17 p.62 reads in full "VINDPM register is reset to 3600mV upon adapter unplugged and it is set to the value based on the VBUS measurement when the adapter plugs in. It is not reset by the REG_RST and the WATCHDOG" - the target's line 567 quotes only the second sentence. Section 7.3.4 step 6 p.26: at each good-source qualification "the ADC reads the ILIM_HIZ pin voltage and VBUS voltage and updates the IINDPM and VINDPM registers accordingly". Section 7.3.4.2 p.27 documents the automatic retry after VBUS_OVP clears, the 7-minute poor-source retry and the host EN_HIZ = 0 retry; whether those retries re-run step 6 is not stated (the dossier's B, S1-B2). Section 7.3.4.4 p.27: default VINDPM = VBUS0 - 1.4 V (VBUS0 >= 7 V), re-measured by FORCE_VINDPM_DET = 1. Section 7.3.4.3 p.27: "the host cannot program the IINDPM register to any values higher than this ILIM clamp after POR, unless the register bit EN_EXTILIM is set to 0"; Table 7-30 p.78: REG14[1] EN_EXTILIM R/W, POR 1, reset by REG_RST only.

Target C:/Life Ops/School/Thesis/DC-Balancer-charger-search/docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md: line 567 (truncated VINDPM fact, no plug-in / re-qualification caveat); lines 577-579 (HR4 "C nominal" with no EN_EXTILIM qualifier); lines 586-589 (principal risks). Grep of the whole target for "S1-B2", "re-qualif", "plug-in", "FORCE_VINDPM", "VBUS0": zero hits for the BQ25798 (the only hit, line 831, is the BQ25690's M8 note). However the EN_EXTILIM defeat IS in the target: line 830 (Table 6.0a, BQ25798 M7 conditional "EN_EXTILIM REG14[1]"), lines 832 and 928 (symmetric M7 grading, "the BQ25798 M7 is itself conditional"), line 1093 (bench gate S1-B3 "EN_EXTILIM = 0 check").

Dossier C:/Life Ops/School/Thesis/DC-Balancer-charger-search/docs/reviews/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10/dossier_BQ25798.md: line 124 (M7 conditional, REG14[1] read-back), line 155 (HR4 row "register-defeatable through EN_EXTILIM (REG14[1]), covered by read-back only"), line 227 (overwrite list, re-qualification not excluded, B: S1-B2), lines 230-232 (auto value 14.5 V at a 15.9 V bus; 5.6-6.6 V at a 7-8 V bus), line 548 (S1-B2 gate extended to the re-qualification paths), lines 586-587 (S3 = 2 B, S4 = 2 B with both items named), lines 603-604 ("survives REG_RST and the watchdog (survival across re-qualification is still B, S1-B2)" - the parenthetical the target dropped), lines 606-610 (the dossier's own weakness list, which the target's principal-risks paragraph reproduces item for item and which likewise names neither EN_EXTILIM nor re-qualification). [SCO] scoring.md line 147 (Table 4 M7 conditional) and lines 299-300 (S3 2 B, S4 2 B). [EVAL] charger_replacement_evaluation_2026-11-05.md lines 488-489 and 495-497 (fault table: VINDPM soft layer vs H1 stuck HIGH; REG14 bit slip covered by read-back each pass; VINDPM reset at plug-in designed around by writing after PG_STAT with P5 LOW); line 905 (S1-B2 definition).
- Quantification: Auto VINDPM after a re-qualification at the nominal bus: VBUS0 - 1.4 V = 15.9 - 1.4 = 14.5 V, which is 1.5 V below the 16.0 V bus (2.23 V below the 16.39 V steady maximum), so the 18.62 V-minimum threshold layer is gone until the host rewrites REG05. In the low-input states: 7.0 - 1.4 = 5.6 V to 8.0 - 1.4 = 6.6 V, below the 7.4-8.4 V pack (dossier line 231, consistent). Loop bound once the layer is lost: ILIM_HIZ is still held LOW by the hardware term (ILIM_REL = GATE OR (CHG_QUAL AND NOT P5)) and /CE is pulled to REGN, so a sustained bus -> charger -> pack current needs a second fault (H1 stuck HIGH, [EVAL] row 488); it is then bounded by the regen ILIM ceiling 1.55-1.61 A (reference-biased) and by F1 <= 6 ms, and the register-image read-back (<= 170 ms) detects the rewritten REG05. Reachability of a mid-Run re-qualification in the always-powered design: an "unplug" needs VCHG-IN < VBUS_PRESENTZ 3.1-3.3 V (full input loss); VBUS_OVP needs >= 25.2 V at the IC pins, above the 22.61-23.63 V eFuse cut-off and the 22.28 V clamp, so it is unreachable without an eFuse fault; a poor-source event needs VBUS to drop below VPOORSRC under a 10 mA pull from a 16 V bus. EN_EXTILIM: a single REG14[1] bit slip removes the clamp but leaves IINDPM at its already-clamped value (1.70 A written, held at the 1.61 A clamp); raising the bus draw needs a second write to REG06 (up to 3.3 A), which in FC mode would exceed the 1.20 A arm drop and reach the 1.4 A OC_FC latch - a double fault covered by the per-pass read-back. Score effect: zero - [SCO] already carries S3 = 2 (B) and S4 = 2 (B) for these two items and Table 6.0a already grades M7 conditional; the 40.3 total and the 10-point margin are unchanged.
- Source: TI SLUSDV2C (primary): Table 7-17 p.62, section 7.3.4 step 6 p.26, sections 7.3.4.2-7.3.4.4 p.27, Table 7-30 p.78. Then [D798] sections 3, 4, 6, 7.2, 15-17; [SCO] Tables 4 and 10; [EVAL] 2.7 fault table and 4.4-4.5.
- Severity rationale: Doc drift and a dropped B hedge, not a changed number or a reachable single-fault loop. The claim at line 567 is true as far as it goes but truncates the Table 7-17 sentence and drops the dossier's "(survival across re-qualification is still B, S1-B2)" qualifier; the HR4 code drops the dossier's "register-defeatable through EN_EXTILIM (REG14[1]), covered by read-back only". Exposure path: loss of the VINDPM layer alone does not create the HR3 loop (two hardware inhibits remain; a loop needs H1 stuck HIGH as a second fault and is then bounded by the 1.61 A ceiling and the <= 6 ms F1 trip); the re-qualification triggers are low-reachability in the always-powered design (VBUS_OVP unreachable below the eFuse cut-off; unplug needs < 3.3 V at VCHG-IN). The EN_EXTILIM defeat needs two register writes and is already covered by the per-pass read-back and is already stated in the target at lines 830, 832, 928 and 1093. No score, must-have grade or shortlist order moves. The finding's half about the principal-risks list is refuted: that paragraph reproduces the dossier's own section 16 weakness list item for item, so section 5.1 is not more favourable than the dossier on that point.
- Minimal fix: Two edits in docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md section 5.1, no change to section 6 scores or to the HR3 condition list (the dossier's HR3 conditions are SYS separate, the parity build and the hard-stop allowance; VINDPM retention is not an HR3 condition in any source, so adding it there would go beyond the record).

(1) Replace line 567 with:
"- VINDPM 3.6-22 V in 100 mV steps, +-2 % at 18.6 V. The IC rewrites it to VBUS0 - 1.4 V (14.5 V at a 15.9 V bus, below the bus) at each plug-in, at FORCE_VINDPM_DET = 1 and at EN_MPPT = 1; it is not reset by REG_RST or the watchdog (DS p.12, section 7.3.4.4 p.27, Table 7-17 p.62). Survival across the re-qualification paths (VBUS_OVP retry, 7-minute poor-source retry, EN_HIZ = 0 retry) is not stated: B, bench gate S1-B2 ([D798] section 6)."
Proof: Table 7-17 p.62 and section 7.3.4.4 p.27 quoted above; 15.9 - 1.4 = 14.5 V; the B mark and gate ID are [D798] lines 227, 548 and 603-604.

(2) In the HR codes sentence (lines 577-579) change "HR4 C nominal (regen ceiling maximum 1.61 A with a 129 us hard-stop allowance; FC bound after a POR 1.15 A, margin 0.05 A to the 1.20 A arm drop)" to "HR4 C nominal (regen ceiling maximum 1.61 A with a 129 us hard-stop allowance; FC bound after a POR 1.15 A, margin 0.05 A to the 1.20 A arm drop; the ILIM clamp is register-defeatable through EN_EXTILIM REG14[1] and is covered by the REG14 read-back each pass, M7 conditional, Table 6.0a)".
Proof: DS section 7.3.4.3 p.27 and Table 7-30 p.78; [D798] line 155; [EVAL] fault-table row at line 495; target Table 6.0a line 830 already carries the same grade.

Fix class: documentation only; no follow-on skill (no test, pinmap, protocol, coefficient or bench action). Optionally add a row "S1-B2 | BQ25798 | VINDPM retention across the re-qualification paths; affects S4 evidence only, not the outcome" to Table 8.2 if the operator wants the dossier's gate list mirrored.
- Adjacent findings: dossier_BQ25798.md line 152 (HR1 row) still says the regen ceiling limits charge at an 8.4 V pack to 2.87-2.91 A, while its own section 15.1 item 2 and the target (lines 572-573, Table 6.0b line 848) adopt the reference-biased ceiling giving 3.10 A; the dossier HR1 row is stale relative to the dossier's adopted baseline.; Target Table 8.2 (lines 1090-1099) lists S1-B4, S1-B3, CCB-1 and CCB-2 for the BQ25798 but not S1-B2, S1-B5 (watchdog read-back) or S1-B6 (ILIM_HIZ re-sampling, the P6 ambiguity) that [D798] section 15 carries; defensible under the table's 'can change the outcome' scope, but S1-B6 decides the HR4 'C nominal' label and arguably belongs there.; [EVAL] fault-table row 497 frames the VINDPM plug-in reset as 'every Run start' (EVM-era, charger powered per Run); under the always-powered CCB design the plug-in occurs once per power-up and the mid-Run case is a re-qualification, which is exactly the B item - the [EVAL] row wording has not been updated for the always-powered design.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): The VINDPM-layer loss alone does not create the HR3 loop (ILIM_HIZ hardware hold and /CE pull-up remain; a loop needs H1 stuck HIGH and is then bounded by the 1.61 A ceiling and <= 6 ms F1), and the re-qualification triggers are low-reachability in the always-powered design (VBUS_OVP unreachable below the eFuse cut-off; unplug needs < 3.3 V at VCHG-IN). The EN_EXTILIM defeat needs two writes and is already stated at four places in the target. My claim about the principal-risks list is refuted: that paragraph reproduces [D798] section 16 item for item. What remains is the truncated Table 7-17 sentence and the dropped B/S1-B2 hedge at :567 and the dropped register-defeat note in the HR codes. Minor; both edits accepted.

### CIS-R1-31 (facts; reviewer minor; final minor; accepted)

- Location: docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:570 (section 5.1), :659 (section 5.3), :667
- Claim: 'Charger watchdog minimum 0.5 s' (BQ25798); 'Charger watchdog 40 s minimum against 0.5 s in the baseline' (BQ25690).
- Problem: Both figures are the shortest register setting, not a timing minimum. [D690] section 5.4 notes that no maximum timeout is published (accuracy is given only at the 160 s setting, 136 s minimum). The worst-case expiry is therefore longer than 40 s and unbounded by the datasheet. 'Minimum' reads as a guaranteed lower bound, which is the wrong direction for a fail-safe backstop.
- Reviewer recommendation: Write 'shortest setting 0.5 s / 40 s (nominal; no maximum published)'.
- Verdict: PARTLY_CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: Target lines 570 and 659/667 state "Charger watchdog minimum 0.5 s" (BQ25798) and "40 s minimum against 0.5 s in the baseline" (BQ25690). The BQ25690 dossier (dossier_BQ25690.md lines 246-251) says "The shortest setting is 40 s (DS Table 6-24 p.50)", "nominal 40 s", and "The data sheet publishes no maximum timeout; it gives only 136 s minimum and 160 s nominal for the 160 s setting (DS section 5.6 pp.12-13)". Dossier line 258 says "nominal 40 s (no maximum published)", and review item 8 (line 910) already corrected an earlier "at most 40 s" wording for this reason. The BQ25798 dossier line 360 gives settings 0.5/1/2/20/40/80/160 s with accuracy only at 160 s (tWDT 136 min / 160 typ), so 0.5 s is likewise the shortest setting with no published tolerance. The finding's premise holds: "minimum" in the target means the shortest register setting, but a reader can take it as a guaranteed lower or upper bound on expiry. The target also drops the dossier's "(no maximum published)" qualifier. Overstated by the finding: the target does not claim a bound on worst-case expiry. Line 981 and 859 use only the 80x slower comparison and the target already treats the charger watchdog as a backstop only (HR5 B for the watchdog; the heartbeat monostables are the fast tier). The recommendation does not rest on the watchdog.
- Quantification: Ratio check: 40 s / 0.5 s = 80, matching the "80 times slower" statement at target line 981. Expiry is unbounded above in the datasheet: the only tolerance data is at the 160 s setting (136 s min / 160 s typ), so no worst-case expiry at the 40 s or 0.5 s settings can be computed. No score or ranking number depends on this.
- Source: Primary datasheet figures as transcribed in dossier_BQ25690.md section 5.4 (DS Table 6-24 p.50, section 5.6 pp.12-13) and dossier_BQ25798.md line 360 (DS section 6.6 p.17, Table 7-26 p.72). Not re-fetched from the TI PDFs.
