- Severity rationale: Wording ambiguity with no computed exposure: the watchdog is already classed B / backstop and the fast hung-MCU tier is the heartbeat hardware, so a longer unbounded expiry does not change any HR5 grade, score or the BQ25798 recommendation. No kill path, number or decision changes.
- Minimal fix: Line 570: "Charger watchdog shortest setting 0.5 s (no tolerance published at that setting; DS Table 7-26 p.72)". Lines 659 and 667: "Charger watchdog shortest setting 40 s (nominal; no maximum published, DS Table 6-24 p.50, section 5.6 pp.12-13) against 0.5 s in the baseline". Same wording in line 855 table cell ("watchdog 40 s minimum" -> "watchdog shortest setting 40 s, no maximum published") and line 881-882 dossier-aligned text. Text-only change, no follow-on skill.
- Adjacent findings: The BQ25690 dossier review-table item 23 (line 925) and dossier line 882 use the same ambiguous phrase '40 s minimum', so the working document has the same wording problem as the target.; Target line 855 repeats 'watchdog 40 s minimum' in the HR5 summary table.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): Both figures are the shortest register setting, not a guaranteed bound, and the datasheet gives tolerance only at 160 s (136 s min), so no worst-case expiry at 40 s or 0.5 s is computable. The watchdog is already classed B / backstop with the heartbeat hardware as the fast tier, so no HR5 grade or score depends on it. Minor; the 'shortest setting ... no maximum published' wording accepted at all four places.

### CIS-R1-32 (decision; reviewer minor; final none; rejected)

- Location: charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:615-618 (BQ25773 adders), :874 (S6 row), :899-903 (S6 score-0 clause applied to LTC4015 A); screening_criteria.md:102
- Claim: "the S6 score-0 anchor also covers 'an adder needed to defeat a register-defeatable pin' ... Adder A3 (the DRVCC stage) exists because VIN_UVCL_SETTING = 0x00 defeats the UVCLFB clamp, so the score-0 clause applies" (LTC4015 design A, S6 1 -> 0).
- Problem: The clause is tested against the LTC4015 only. The BQ25773 build's 2.5 V shunt reference (adder 6) is listed in [D773] section 9.2 as serving "Ceiling accuracy and the VILIM_ENZ guard", i.e. it exists in part because the ILIM_HIZ pin function can be disabled by a pin voltage above 4.1-4.2 V "neglecting EN_EXTILIM bit status", and the same pin is register-defeatable at two addresses. If the clause is read as it was for A3, the BQ25773 S6 moves to 0 and its total to 45.7 (+5.3), the scenario D2 value; if read narrowly, the LTC4015-A grading should state why the BQ25773 reference does not qualify. Effect bounded at 3.3 points; no outcome changes, but the BQ25773 is the only part credited with S6 = 1 and its alternate status is built on that 3.3-point credit.
- Reviewer recommendation: State the reading of the score-0 clause once in [CRIT] 3.1 and apply it to every adder list; record the BQ25773 U_REF disposition explicitly in Table 6.2 prose.
- Verdict: REFUTED, severity none
- Evidence: Clause wording, the only place it is defined: screening_criteria.md:102 "S6 ... 0 = > 8 adder parts, or an adder needed to defeat a register-defeatable pin"; section 3.3 (screening_criteria.md:128-142) adds the counting rule but no further definition of the clause.

How the clause is applied to the LTC4015: dossier_LTC4015.md:458-460 (section 9.4 table) "S6 if A3 counts as 'an adder needed to defeat a register-defeatable pin' ... 0 (A3 exists because VIN_UVCL_SETTING = 0x00 defeats the UVCLFB clamp, [DS] p.46)"; dossier_LTC4015.md:178-184 (section 5.2 "Register defeat of the UVCLFB clamp"): at code 0x00 the grounded UVCLFB sits about 5 mV below the reference, inside the 50 mV taper band, so the pin clamp "does not guarantee zero current" and M5 passes only with the DRVCC stage. The trigger is a REGISTER write that removes the pin interlock's effect; A3 is the adder that restores an inhibit after that register defeat.

What the BQ25773 U_REF exists for: dossier_BQ25773_SMBus_twin_BQ25770G.md:277-295 (section 7.2) separates two hazards. Hazard 1 "Register defeat" (EN_EXTILIM = 0 at REG0x32 b7 / REG0x80 b4; EN_IIN_DPM = 0; IIN_DPM_AUTO_DISABLE) is disposed of with "All four bits go on the never-write list with read-back" - no adder. Hazard 2 "Pin-voltage defeat" (VILIM_ENZ 4.1-4.2 V, "neglecting EN_EXTILIM bit status") arises when "an open bottom resistor pulls the pin to REGN"; the 2.5 V reference is the remedy for that single-fault hardware case ("The pin then cannot exceed 2.5 V with any divider resistor open"), plus ceiling accuracy (section 7.3, dossier:303-313: the +-0.7 % term covers the reference). Table 9.2 row 6 (dossier:434) names exactly those two functions. The reference does nothing against EN_EXTILIM = 0: with EN_EXTILIM = 0 the pin ceiling is removed irrespective of pin voltage (dossier:279-280), and the dossier's S3 row (dossier:741) records the ceiling as "Register-defeatable (EN_EXTILIM, probably EN_IIN_DPM); the CRIT bands carry no defeatability term, as for the BQ25798".

Uniform treatment of the same adder on the baseline: scoring.md:189 counts the BQ25798's own "2.5 V reference and its feed for the regen ceiling" as ordinary adders under the band rule (11, S6 = 0), and dossier_BQ25798.md:276-282 gives its purpose as narrowing the regen band (1.55-1.61 A); the BQ25798 ILIM_HIZ ceiling is likewise register-defeatable through EN_EXTILIM and "covered by read-back only" (dossier_BQ25798.md:155, 268). So the reference is treated identically on both ILIM_HIZ parts: an accuracy/hardware-fault part counted in the band, never a clause trigger.

Target statements checked: charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:615-618 (BQ25773 8 adders, S6 = 1, four contingent items each move S6 to 0), :874 (S6 row), :899-903 (LTC4015 A clause application, 43.3 vs 46.7). scoring.md:373 scenario D2 and :410 "the BQ25773 S6 credit is fragile: any one of its four contingent adders removes 3.3 points" - the fragility is already disclosed, on the contingent-adder axis.

Not re-fetched: SLUSEK7 p.6 / p.20 for the VILIM_ENZ wording. Both the finding and the dossier accept that fact; the verdict does not turn on it.
- Quantification: Arithmetic reproduced with calc/score.py (run with python -I from the scratchpad, miniforge interpreter): base BQ25798 40.33, BQ25690 48.33, BQ25773 49.00, LTC4015-B 48.67, LTC4015-A 43.33, RAA489110 35.67. Scenario F "BQ25773 S6 0": 49.00 - 10 x 1/3 = 49.00 - 3.33 = 45.67 (prints 45.67, margin +5.33) = the document's D2 row 45.7 (+5.3). LTC4015-A with S6 = 1: 43.33 + 3.33 = 46.67, margin +6.33 = the document's 46.7 (+6.3). The finding's numbers (3.3-point bound, 45.7, +5.3) are therefore correct as arithmetic; what fails is the premise that the clause reaches the BQ25773 reference. Under D2 the shortlist order would change (BQ25773 49.0 -> 45.7 falls from first to third among challengers, below LTC4015 B 48.7 and BQ25690 48.3) while the recommendation (no challenger clears the 10-point margin) would not; but D2 is not triggered by this finding.
- Source: Working documents, per the authority order for this task: [CRIT] screening_criteria.md:102 (clause wording), [D773] section 7.2 and Table 9.2 (what U_REF guards: a pin-voltage single fault plus accuracy; register defeat handled by the never-write list, no adder), [D4015] sections 5.2 and 9.4 (the clause's A3 application: an adder whose existence is caused by a register defeat), [D798] section 7.2 / [SCO] Table 6 (the BQ25798's identical reference adder counted under the band rule). Numbers settled by calc/score.py.
- Severity rationale: The substantive claim is refuted: no BQ25773 adder in the recommended build exists to counter a register defeat (the register defeats of ILIM_HIZ are met by the never-write list with read-back, exactly as on the BQ25798), so the clause as applied to LTC4015 A3 does not move the BQ25773 S6, and there is no asymmetric grading. Score and shortlist order are unchanged; no exposure class (wrong part recommendation, kill path, loop, wrong published number, operator decision) is reached. The residual - the target never states the reading of the clause and never records the U_REF disposition - is wording polish (nit level): the information exists in [D773] section 7.2 and the scoring is already consistent across the two ILIM_HIZ parts.
- Minimal fix: Optional nit-level clarification (no score change). In charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md section 6.2, after the sentence ending "...so the score-0 clause applies ([D4015] section 9.4)." (line 901-902), insert: "The clause applies to an adder whose existence is caused by a register write that removes a pin interlock. The BQ25773 2.5 V reference does not meet it: it bounds the ILIM_HIZ pin below VILIM_ENZ against an open divider resistor and narrows the ceiling band ([D773] section 7.2); the ILIM_HIZ register defeats (EN_EXTILIM, EN_IIN_DPM) are met by the never-write list with read-back and add no part, as on the BQ25798. The BQ25773 S6 therefore stays 1." Proof: the replacement wording restates [D773] 7.2 items 1-2 and Table 9.2 row 6, and [SCO] Table 6's identical treatment of the BQ25798 reference; score.py base case is unchanged (BQ25773 49.00). Mirror the same sentence after scoring.md:327. No follow-on skill triggered (documentation only; no test, pinmap, protocol, coefficient or bench change).
- Adjacent findings: The LTC4015 A3 clause application is itself double-motivated: [D4015] 9.4 (dossier_LTC4015.md:450) maps A3 as the replacement for the BQ25798 ILIM_HIZ hold-down (a converter-off stage every candidate's build carries), while :460 says A3 'exists because VIN_UVCL_SETTING = 0x00 defeats the UVCLFB clamp'. If A3 is a functional replacement rather than a defeat guard, design A's S6 would be 1 (46.7, +6.3); the dossier presents both readings and the target picks the stricter one without saying why. Outcome unchanged (design A fails condition 1 either way), nit level.; [D773] section 7.2 item 1: whether EN_IIN_DPM = 0 also removes the ILIM_HIZ pin limit is UNVERIFIED (bench B15, TODO(verify: SLUSEK7 / TI E2E)); the target's line 634-635 'Register-defeat surface ... including EN_IIN_DPM' states it within the never-write list, which is correct, but any reader taking the pin-limit loss as settled would be citing an UNVERIFIED item - the target's wording stays on the right side of this.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (none): Refuted on the premise, and I accept it. The BQ25773 2.5 V reference exists to bound the ILIM_HIZ pin below VILIM_ENZ against an open divider resistor (a pin-level hazard) and to narrow the ceiling band; the ILIM_HIZ register defeats (EN_EXTILIM, EN_IIN_DPM) are met by the never-write list with read-back and add no part, exactly as on the BQ25798. The score-0 clause as applied to LTC4015 A3 (an adder whose existence is caused by a register write that removes a pin interlock) therefore does not reach the BQ25773 reference, and there is no asymmetric grading. My arithmetic (45.7 / +5.3 = scenario D2) was correct but not triggered. The optional clarifying sentence in 6.2 is worthwhile because the document never states the reading of the clause.

### CIS-R1-33 (facts; reviewer minor; final minor; accepted)

- Location: docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:612-613 (section 5.2 HR4), :713 (section 5.4 HR4), :853
- Claim: BQ25773 regen maximum 1.79 A, 'about 112 us; bench pass <= 110 us'; LTC4015 1.649 A, 'about 126 us'.
- Problem: The baseline uses the constant-margin model: allowance = 0.757 V x 0.4 mF / I - 59 us, which gives 129 us and 108 us ([D798] section 7.2). The BQ25773 figure instead uses the smallest I x t product of the EVAL rows (201 A us / 1.79 A = 112 us, [D773] 8.2). The constant-margin model gives 302.8 uC / 1.79 A - 59 = 110.2 us, so the 110 us bench pass has about 0 us margin, not 2 us. The LTC4015 figure is a linear interpolation between rows; the model gives 302.8 / 1.649 - 59 = 124.6 us. The BQ25690 at 1.715 A gives 117.6 us and the RAA489110 at 1.687 A gives 120.5 us; the target does not state the RAA figure.
- Reviewer recommendation: Recompute every finalist's allowance with the single 0.757 V constant-margin model, and state the BQ25773 bench pass as <= 110 us with zero margin. Add the RAA489110 allowance.
- Verdict: CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: The target uses two different allowance models in the same comparison. (1) The baseline uses a constant V-MOT margin. dossier_BQ25798.md:271-273 and :281 derive a 0.757 V margin from the [EVAL] 2.5 rows, then compute 0.757/4.52 - 59 = 108 us and 0.757/4.03 - 59 = 129 us. Target :852 publishes "1.61 A, 129 us". (2) The BQ25773 figure uses a constant I x t product instead. dossier_BQ25773_SMBus_twin_BQ25770G.md:370-373 says "the I x t product of the EVAL rows (201-230 A us) gives about 112 us. The bench pass value is therefore <= 110 us". The target carries this at :612 and :852. (3) The LTC4015 figure is a linear interpolation between table rows. dossier_LTC4015.md:321-323 says "sits between the 1.40 A and 1.72 A rows ... interpolated allowance about 126 us". The target carries this at :713 and :852. (4) The rows themselves are in charger_replacement_evaluation_2026-11-05.md:456-460: 1.25 A gives 184 us, 1.40 A gives 158 us, and 1.72 A gives 117 us at VBUS 15.9 V, on 0.4 mF, with 24 + 15 + 20 = 59 us of deductions (:452). (5) The RAA489110 figure: target :752 and :852 give only "1.687 A (C)" with no allowance. (6) The BQ25690 figure: target :667 gives 1.715 A, and dossier_BQ25690.md:403 says it falls inside the 1.72 A row by 0.3 %. The finding's citation of line 853 is off by one; the table row is at line 852.
- Quantification: The [EVAL] rows are consistent with constant charge, not constant I x t.

Charge per row, Q = (t + 59 us) x I:
- 1.25 A: 243 x 1.25 = 303.75 uC
- 1.40 A: 217 x 1.40 = 303.8 uC
- 1.72 A: 176 x 1.72 = 302.7 uC
- The margin form gives 0.757 V x 0.4 mF = 302.8 uC.

The I x t products of the same rows drift instead: 230, 221.2 and 201.2 A us. They follow Q - 59 x I, which falls as I rises. Carrying the 1.72 A product (201 A us) to a higher current is therefore non-conservative.

Allowance, recomputed as t = Q/I - 59, over the band Q = 302.7 to 303.8 uC:
- BQ25773 at 1.79 A: 110.1 to 110.7 us (302.8/1.79 = 169.2 us; minus 59 gives 110.2 us). The target states about 112 us, which is high by about 1.5-2 us.
- The bench pass value of <= 110 us is still inside the bound, but with only 0.1-0.7 us of margin, not the implied 2 us.
- LTC4015 at 1.649 A: 124.6 to 125.2 us (model value 124.6 us). The target states about 126 us. The linear interpolation 158 - (0.249/0.32) x 41 = 126.1 us overstates it because t(I) is convex.
- BQ25690 at 1.715 A: 302.8/1.715 - 59 = 117.6 us. This is consistent with the target.
- RAA489110 at 1.687 A: 302.8/1.687 - 59 = 120.5 us. The target omits this figure, but it is above the 115 us gate.
- Baseline check: 1.61 A gives 129.1 us and 1.807 A gives 108.6 us, which match the D798 values.

Every finalist's allowance stays at or above its stated bench pass value. The LTC4015, BQ25690 and RAA489110 values (117.6-124.6 us) all exceed the 115 us S1-B3 gate.
- Source: Two sources settle this:
- [EVAL] charger_replacement_evaluation_2026-11-05.md section 2.5 (lines 450-460): the hard-stop rows and the 59 us deductions.
- dossier_BQ25798.md section 7.2 (lines 271-282): the constant-margin model.

The BQ25773 and LTC4015 dossiers (working documents) are the origin of the inconsistent figures. A datasheet is not needed: the claim is pure arithmetic on the [EVAL] rows.
- Severity rationale: Two published numbers are wrong, and the errors run in the non-conservative direction: the BQ25773 value is high by 1.8 us (1.6 %) and the LTC4015 value by 1.4 us (1.1 %). However, no gate or decision changes.
- The BQ25773 bench pass value of <= 110 us remains inside the recomputed 110.1-110.7 us bound. The bench gate would still reject any latency that breaches the allowance, so there is no kill path or back-feed exposure.
- The LTC4015 recomputes to 124.6 us, which is still above the 115 us gate.
- The RAA489110 recomputes to 120.5 us, also above the 115 us gate, so the missing figure hides nothing.
- HR4 codes and scores are unaffected, as are the shortlist order and the recommendation.

The finding is a model inconsistency with a sub-2-us effect, which caps it at minor.
- Minimal fix: Edit the target in five places.

1. Line 612, section 5.2 HR4: replace "maximum 1.79 A, about 112 us; bench pass <= 110 us" with "maximum 1.79 A, 110 us (constant 0.757 V margin: 302.8 uC / 1.79 A - 59 us = 110.2 us); bench pass <= 110 us, margin under 1 us".
2. Line 713, section 5.4 HR4: replace "about 126 us" with "125 us (302.8 uC / 1.649 A - 59 us = 124.6 us)".
3. Line 752, section 5.5 HR4: after "regen maximum 1.687 A", add ", 120 us (302.8 uC / 1.687 A - 59 us = 120.5 us)".
4. Line 852, table row: change the BQ25773 cell to "1.79 A, 110 us; bench pass <= 110 us, margin < 1 us (C)". Change the LTC4015 cell to "1.649 A, 125 us (C)". Change the RAA489110 cell to "1.687 A, 120 us (C)". Optionally add "118 us" to the BQ25690 cell (302.8/1.715 - 59 = 117.6 us).
5. Make the same corrections in the working documents so the scoring stays consistent:
   - dossier_BQ25773 section 8.2 item 2, lines 370-373: replace "the I x t product of the EVAL rows (201-230 A us) gives about 112 us" with "the constant 0.757 V margin of [D798] section 7.2 gives 0.757 V / 4.475 V/ms - 59 us = 110 us".
   - dossier_LTC4015 section 7.2, line 323: replace "interpolated allowance about 126 us" with "allowance 125 us by the constant-margin model".
   - scoring.md line 167: apply the same table-cell changes as item 4.

Proof: Q = 0.757 V x 0.4 mF = 302.8 uC, which reproduces all three [EVAL] 2.5 rows to within 1 uC (303.75 / 303.8 / 302.7). It also reproduces the 129 us and 108 us baseline values.

Fix class: documentation only. No firmware, test or regen-coeffs skill is triggered.
- Adjacent findings: The line-853 citation in the finding is off by one; the HR4 table row is at line 852 of the target, and it duplicates scoring.md:167, which carries the same 112 us and 126 us figures.; dossier_BQ25690.md:382 says the 81-115 us worst case at Qg 15 nC uses the whole 115 us allowance. At the BQ25690 regen maximum of 1.715 A, however, the constant-margin allowance is 117.6 us, so its 115 us gate has only 2.6 us of margin, and the 1.715 A value is itself extrapolated (B). This is not reflected in the target.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): Confirmed: the [EVAL] rows are constant-charge (Q = 302.7-303.8 uC) and the I x t products drift (230 -> 201 A us), so carrying the 1.72 A product upward is non-conservative; the BQ25773 allowance is 110.1-110.7 us (not 'about 112'), leaving the <= 110 us bench pass with under 1 us of margin, the LTC4015 is 124.6 us (not 126), the RAA489110 120.5 us is missing. Every finalist stays at or above its stated pass value and the gates would still reject a breach, so sub-2-us errors cap at minor. The five edits accepted. Note that this verdict is the basis for my R1-07 refinement (96 us at 1.947 A on this model).

### CIS-R1-34 (coverage; reviewer minor; final minor; accepted)

- Location: Table 6.0b row 'HR4 status' (l.854); section 5.3 HR codes (l.667)
- Claim: BQ25690 HR4 'P / C'
- Problem: The governing regen ceiling maximum of 1.715 A is graded B: it is extrapolated above the 1.6 A KILIM test point (dossier_BQ25690.md l.391, l.403, gate T-7). It sits 0.3 % inside the 1.72 A / 117 us hard-stop row. The status code grades the BQ25690 HR4 above the baseline's 'C nominal' while its deciding figure is a bench gate.
- Reviewer recommendation: Grade the BQ25690 HR4 as 'B (regen maximum, T-7) / P-C (FC)'.
- Verdict: PARTLY_CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: Premise checks out against the primary. SLUSFN4A (BQ25690 DS, Oct 2025 rev Dec 2025), section 5.5 Electrical Characteristics: "KILIM Input current limit scale factor (IIN_MAX = KILIM / RILIM), IIN_MAX = 1.6A, 1A, 0.5A: 2890 / 3333 / 3780 A x Ohm" (raw text extract line 798 of the fetched ti.com PDF); section 5.3 ROC: "RILIM_HIZ ILIM_HIZ pulldown resistor 0.0 - 33 k" (line 427). The dossier reads both correctly (dossier_BQ25690.md l.369-371, l.53). The regen maximum 1.715 A is computed from KILIM max 3780 over the RILIM band minimum 2204 Ohm (dossier l.387) and the dossier itself grades it B with gate T-7 (l.391, l.403, l.775; review items 5 and 26 at l.907, l.928). The target's Table 6.0b carries that B in the figure row (charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md l.852: "1.715 A, extrapolated above the 1.6 A KILIM test point (B)") and the section 5.3 HR-code line already says "(regen maximum 1.715 A, extrapolated, B; ...)" (l.666-667), but the status row (l.854) reads "P / C" with no B, mirroring [SCO] Table 5 l.169 and the dossier's own HR table (l.130). So the table is internally inconsistent between rows 852 and 854; section 5.3 is not, contrary to the finding's second location. Where the finding overstates: HR4 as the target defines it (l.113: "bounded by a hardware ceiling plus registers; the ceiling holds after a charger POR") is settled by the FC bound after POR, 0.950 + 0.15 = 1.10 A, which lies inside the KILIM test range (P/C); the regen-maximum-vs-117 us row is the EVAL 2.5 hard-stop (H2/HR3) consequence that [SCO] files under HR4 for every part. The target's own prose nonetheless calls 1.72 A "the HR4 hard-stop row" (l.80, l.548), so the row belongs to HR4 by the document's own usage. Symmetry: the BQ25798's HR4 "C nominal" (l.854) equally omits its B element - "the pin-to-current conversion accuracy stays unspecified (B: S1-B6 calibration)" (dossier_BQ25798.md l.280, l.247-249, no accuracy published at all) - so the convention in use is to code the computed figure and omit the calibration gate; the proposed fix applied to the BQ25690 alone would invert the comparison (the BQ25690 has a published +-13 % band to 1.6 A with a 7 % extrapolation; the BQ25798 has no published pin-to-current accuracy at any current). The BQ25773 status cell already uses a mode split ("C regen; C ... in FC mode"), so the split format exists.
- Quantification: Recomputed (awk, scratchpad): RILIM regen = 4.02 k // 4.99 k = 2226.4 Ohm nominal, 1 % band 2204.1-2248.7 Ohm; IIN_MAX regen = 2890/2248.7 = 1.2852 A min, 3333/2226.4 = 1.4970 A typ, 3780/2204.1 = 1.7150 A max; FC = 0.712 / 0.829 / 0.950 A. Margin to the 1.72 A row: 1.72/1.715 - 1 = 0.29 % (document's 0.3 % confirmed). Extrapolation above the last KILIM test point: 1.715/1.6 - 1 = 7.2 %. EVAL 2.5 implied V-MOT margin: (117 + 59) us x 4.30 V/ms = 0.757 V; allowance at 1.715 A = 0.757 V / (1.715 A / 0.4 mF) - 59 us = 117.5 us. Bound on the extrapolation: the <= 115 us bench pass value stops covering the ceiling when I_in >= 0.757 V x 0.4 mF / 174 us = 1.740 A, i.e. a KILIM max of 3835 A x Ohm (+1.45 % over 3780) at the regen point. If exceeded, EVAL 2.5 l.462-465 bounds the consequence to one bus-to-charger pulse of I_in x t_excess per reversal (t_excess = allowance shortfall, microseconds), not a kill path. Fallback check: removing the extrapolation (max <= 1.6 A) needs RILIM min >= 3780/1.6 = 2362.5 Ohm, i.e. R_RG >= 5.87 kOhm, which gives a regen minimum of 2890/2410 = 1.199 A and HR1 input 1.199 x 18.62 = 22.3 W < 23 W. Score sensitivity: the HR4 status code feeds no S criterion; even an S3 cap to 2 (B) would move the BQ25690 from 48.3 to 48.3 - 8/3 = 45.6, still third and still above the BQ25798 (40.3) by less than the 10-point switching margin.
- Source: Primary datasheet SLUSFN4A section 5.5 (KILIM row) and section 5.3 (RILIM_HIZ ROC), fetched from ti.com/lit/ds/symlink/bq25690.pdf; [EVAL] section 2.5 hard-stop table (l.456-466); dossier_BQ25690.md sections 7.1-7.3 and gate T-7; dossier_BQ25798.md section 7.2; [SCO] Table 5; target Table 6.0b and sections 1, 5.0, 5.3.
- Severity rationale: Doc-grading inconsistency inside one table (row 852 says B, row 854 says P / C) with no number wrong and no score or shortlist consequence: the HR4 status code feeds no S criterion, and even the harshest reading (S3 capped at 2) leaves the BQ25690 third at 45.6, under the switching margin. The exposure if the extrapolation fails is bounded by the computed +1.45 % KILIM headroom before the 115 us pass value stops covering, and beyond that by EVAL 2.5's bounded-pulse criterion, both already bench-gated (T-7, S1-B3). Not a kill path, back-feed loop or wrong published figure.
- Minimal fix: Table 6.0b (l.854), BQ25690 cell: replace "P / C" with "P / C; regen maximum B (T-7)". For symmetry, the BQ25798 cell in the same row: replace "C nominal" with "C nominal; regen accuracy B (S1-B6)" (dossier_BQ25798.md l.280). Mirror both in [SCO] Table 5 l.169. Section 5.3 l.666-667 needs no change: it already reads "(regen maximum 1.715 A, extrapolated, B; FC bound after a POR 1.10 A, margin 0.10 A)". Do not adopt the finding's "B (regen maximum, T-7) / P-C (FC)" as a replacement of the lead code: the HR4 definition at l.113 is settled by the FC bound (1.10 A, inside the KILIM test range), and demoting the lead code for the BQ25690 alone while the BQ25798's less-documented ceiling keeps "C nominal" would invert the comparison. Proof: the added text restates the grade the same table already assigns at l.852 and the dossier assigns at l.391/l.403, so no number changes. Fix class: doc edit only; no follow-on skill (no test, pinmap, protocol, coefficient or bench action).
- Adjacent findings: dossier_BQ25690.md l.403 names "sizing R_RG for a typical ceiling <= 1.6 A" as the T-7 fallback, but the reference design's typical regen ceiling is already 1.497 A, so the fallback as worded is vacuous; the fallback that actually removes the extrapolation (maximum <= 1.6 A) needs R_RG >= 5.87 kOhm, whose regen minimum 1.199 A gives 22.3 W at 18.62 V and fails HR1 (>= 23 W). The B on the regen maximum is therefore structural at the 19.0 V threshold, which the target's l.80 / l.548 argument already implies but the dossier's fallback text contradicts.; Target l.666-667 and Table 6.0b l.852 cite the extrapolation correctly; [SCO] Table 5 l.169 and dossier_BQ25690.md l.130 carry the same "P / C" omission, so the fix must be mirrored in both working documents or the target will drift from its sources again.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): The recomputed band (1.2852 / 1.4970 / 1.7150 A regen; 0.29 % margin to the 1.72 A row; 7.2 % extrapolation above the 1.6 A test point) and the +1.45 % KILIM headroom before the 115 us pass value stops covering bound the exposure to EVAL 2.5's bounded-pulse criterion, already bench-gated (T-7, S1-B3). The HR4 status code feeds no S criterion; even an S3 cap to 2 leaves the BQ25690 third at 45.6. The defect is an intra-table grading inconsistency (row 852 B, row 854 P / C). Minor; the symmetric annotation ('regen maximum B (T-7)' for the BQ25690, 'regen accuracy B (S1-B6)' for the BQ25798) accepted rather than demoting the BQ25690 lead code alone.

### CIS-R1-35 (facts; reviewer minor; final minor; accepted)

- Location: docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:707-708 (section 5.4)
- Claim: 'Hold-state input draw 5-20 mA (p.46) against the <= 10 mA criterion.'
- Problem: [D4015] M8 row says that the hold-state draw is not tabulated. The p.46 figure is the 'minimum current to operate the charger', 'generally approximately in the range 5mA to 20mA'; the dossier's own estimate is about 4 mA IQ plus 10 mA gate drive. The target presents a different quantity as a datasheet hold-state specification: a settled claim where the source marks the value U/B ('at risk').
- Reviewer recommendation: Reword as: 'hold-state draw not tabulated; p.46 gives a 5-20 mA minimum operating current, and the estimate is about 14 mA: M8 at risk (U/B)'.
- Verdict: CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: Target lines 707-708 (section 5.4) state "Hold-state input draw 5-20 mA (p.46) against the <= 10 mA criterion" with no U/B marker. The LTC4015 dossier M8 row (dossier_LTC4015.md:124) says the hold-state draw "is not tabulated". It rates M8 "Pass (P range); U/B draw (at risk)". The dossier uses p.46 only as the datasheet's minimum current to operate the charger. Dossier lines 688 and 734 call 5-20 mA the "operating range" and say it puts the 10 mA criterion "at risk". Review-table row 4 (line 792) records that an earlier "hold-state draw" mis-citation was corrected to "5-20 mA operating range, M8 now U/B at risk". The target therefore re-presents the corrected quantity as a hold-state spec with no hedge. I did not open the datasheet p.46 itself, and I could not find a dossier line giving the "about 4 mA IQ plus 10 mA gate drive = 14 mA" estimate. Line 524 gives only IC quiescent of 4 mA (p.41). The 14 mA figure is the finding's own sum, so the suggested rewording would need the dossier's own estimate to be located first.
- Quantification: The 5-20 mA range has no overlap-free relation to the 10 mA limit. The lower end (5 mA) passes and the upper end (20 mA) fails by 2x. The target's phrasing hides that the criterion outcome is undetermined. Hold-up arithmetic that depends on this range in the dossier: 300 uC / 20 mA = 15 ms and 300 uC / 5 mA = 60 ms, which matches the 15-60 ms in the dossier. Source range 5-20 mA and the 14 mA estimate are consistent with the finding. I did not recompute the 14 mA figure.
- Source: Working document dossier_LTC4015.md (M8 row at line 124, lines 403-404, 688, 734, 792). This ranks above the target's own prose. Primary datasheet p.46 not re-fetched.
- Severity rationale: The target misstates a U/B quantity as a settled spec. However, LTC4015 B scores 48.7 and is not the recommendation, and the recommendation (BQ25798) is unaffected. The at-risk status of M8 is stated in the dossier and presumably elsewhere in the target. A reader of section 5.4 alone could be misled about the margin, but no kill path, circular loop or wrong-part exposure is shown, so the severity is capped at minor.
- Minimal fix: In docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md lines 707-708, replace "Hold-state input draw 5-20 mA (p.46) against the <= 10 mA criterion." with "Hold-state input draw is not tabulated; p.46 gives a 5-20 mA minimum operating current, so M8 is U/B (at risk) against the <= 10 mA criterion." Quote the 14 mA estimate only if it is traced to a dossier line, since I did not find one. Doc-only change, no follow-on skill.
- Adjacent findings: Dossier line 524 lists 4 mA IC quiescent from p.41. No dossier line was found for the finding's 'about 14 mA' (4 mA + 10 mA gate drive) estimate, so that figure may not appear in any working document.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): Confirmed: the 5-20 mA p.46 figure is the minimum operating current, not a tabulated hold-state draw, and it straddles the 10 mA limit (5 passes, 20 fails by 2x), so the criterion outcome is undetermined and the target's phrasing hides that. LTC4015 B is not the recommendation. Minor; the replacement sentence accepted, with the 14 mA estimate quoted only if traced to a dossier line.

### CIS-R1-S01 (sweep; reviewer major; final major; accepted)

- Location: docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:77-80 (reason 4), 123-127, 530-555 (Table 5.1 and 5.0), 977-982 (6.4 statement 5), 1032-1033 (ICHG 2.50 A cap closed), 1086 (Pack limit ruling); [CRIT] screening_criteria.md:68 (M4)
- Claim: "Two challengers do not reach 3.1 A at an 8.4 V pack at their low tolerance corner" ... "On this record the BQ25690 and the BQ25773 fail the brief's 3.1 A figure"; "The ICHG 2.50 A cap of critic item C2 is closed on the CCB for every part"; M4 sources its 3.1 A figure to "HR1 (regen capture; >= 23 W input at the threshold)".
- Problem: The 3.1 A figure is not a requirement of the vehicle; it is the BQ25798 regen-image ICHG setting of [WD] (r1-10, H.7), which [WD] 1388-1389 and 2001 hold at 2.50 A "until T-E-11/T-B-19 pass" ("charge current 3.10 A accepted by the pack", [WD] 63), and which [EVAL] 8.1 item 6 and 8.2 leave open ("Sets the ICHG ceiling (2.50 A today, 2.83 A for HR1 at 23 W)"; "or whether HR1 is restated at about 20 W"). [D798] 403 and 532 carry this forward ("3.1 A needs the pack's maximum charge current"). The target then (a) promotes the setting to a pass/fail figure, (b) applies it at an 8.4 V pack, where 3.1 A is 26.0 W out and 28.3 W in at eta 0.92, which is 23 % above the HR1 pass condition (23 W; 2.52 A at 8.4 V) and 50 % above the 18.9 W peak of the defining 3 -> 1 m/s, 12 A brake, and (c) declares the 2.50 A pack-pending cap "closed on the CCB" while listing "Pack limit" as an open ruling in 8.1. The BQ25690 the target marks "Not met (-0.48 A)" delivers 23.9 W input (its own Table 5.1: HR1 PASS, +3.9 %), 22.0 W into the pack (16 % above the brake peak) and 2.97 A at the 7.4 V pack where the 2.83 A figure was derived. The CCB changes nothing about the pack's charge-current acceptance, so the C2 row of Table 9 is a category error (an EVM-era register image was never the binding limit). Exposure: a decision the operator would make differently; reason 4 of the recommendation and the succession-rule reason "it delivers 2.62 A, not 3.1 A" rest on a figure that the operator has not yet admitted and that exceeds the hard requirement's own definition; the BQ25690's regen HR1 input margin is understated as a failure.
- Reviewer recommendation: Restate the delivered-current test against the HR1 pass condition as defined in Table 1.1 (>= 23 W input at the threshold, i.e. 18.9 W brake peak with margin), or against 2.83 A at a 7.4 V pack, and present 3.1 A at 8.4 V as the BQ25798 design setting it is, conditional on the pack ruling. Delete reason 4 or re-derive it; correct the Table 9 / 6.6 "closed on the CCB" entry to "open: pack datasheet ([EVAL] 8.1 item 6)"; remove "delivers 2.62 A, not 3.1 A" from the succession-rule reasoning or qualify it as a setting margin.
- Verdict: CONFIRMED, severity major
- Evidence: Origin of the 3.1 A figure. No upstream requirement source states 3.1 A as a vehicle requirement. [EVAL] docs/charger_replacement_evaluation_2026-11-05.md:281 defines HR1 as "input >= 23 W at the threshold for a 3 -> 1 m/s, 12 A brake (peak 18.9 W)"; [EVAL]:592-595 derives the ICHG needed as ">= 2.83 A (23 x 0.91 / 7.4), which waits on the pack datasheet's maximum charge current (section 8.1 item 6)... Alternatively, the operator restates HR1 at about 20 W"; [EVAL]:1024-1026 and :1035 keep this open ("Sets the ICHG ceiling (2.50 A today, 2.83 A for HR1 at 23 W)"). The 3.10 A appears first as a design setting in [WD] docs/charger_bq25798_wiring_design_2026-11-05.md:123 (r1-10 "Regen image ICHG 3.10 A (0x0136)"), :2156-2157 (H.7: at pack 7.40 V, eta_max 0.93, ICHG -6.5 %: "ICHG needed 2.51 A for 18.9 W; 3.05 A for the HR1 23 W condition; 3.10 A chosen"), :1388-1389 and :2001 ("3.10 A (2.50 A = 0x00FA until T-E-11/T-B-19 pass)"), :63 ("T-B-19 / T-E-11 (charge current 3.10 A accepted by the pack...)"), :2315 (T-E-11 fallback "ICHG 2.50 A"). [D798] dossier_BQ25798.md:403 and :532 carry it forward as open ("3.1 A needs the pack's maximum charge current ([EVAL] 8.1 item 6)"). [CRIT] screening_criteria.md:68 sources M4's 3.1 A to "HR1 (regen capture; >= 23 W input at the threshold)" and :187 calls 3.10 A the "image"; [CRIT] section 1 (lines 1-54) carries no 3.1 A at all, although the target:123 cites "[CRIT] sections 1, 4" for "The regen path needs a charge current of 3.1 A or more".

Target statements. Target:77-80 (reason 4) "Two challengers do not reach 3.1 A at an 8.4 V pack"; :535-536 Table 5.1 bold "Not met (-0.48 A)" / "Not met (-0.06 A)" while the same rows show HR1 input 23.9 W (+3.9 %) and 23.7 W (+3.0 %); :553 "fail the brief's 3.1 A figure"; :982 and :1067-1068 "delivers 2.62 A, not 3.1 A ... which the operator must accept explicitly"; :1032-1033 "The ICHG 2.50 A cap of critic item C2 is closed on the CCB for every part ([SCO] Table 9)" against :1086 "Pack limit | Maximum pack charge current for a 3.1 A or higher ICHG setting ([EVAL] section 8.1 item 6) | Applies to every part". [SCO] scoring.md:278 is the Table 9 row; the C2 residual it claims to close is [EVAL]:234 "New residual: ICHG 2.50 A caps input at 20.3 W at a 7.4 V pack", which is pack-gated, not CCB-gated (the BQ25798 ICHG range 0.05-5 A was the same on the EVM, [D798]:121). [D690] dossier_BQ25690.md:587-596 gives the BQ25690 figures the target condenses: 23.9 W input, 2.97 A at 7.4 V, 2.62 A at 8.4 V, "The 18.9 W peak of the defining 3 -> 1 m/s brake is covered in every row".
- Quantification: Recomputed (scratch script, .venv_hil python -I): HR1 ICHG at 7.4 V, eta 0.91: 23 x 0.91 / 7.4 = 2.828 A ([EVAL] 2.83 A reproduced). HR1 pass condition expressed as charge current at 8.4 V, eta 0.92: 23 x 0.92 / 8.4 = 2.519 A. 3.1 A at 8.4 V = 26.04 W out, 26.04 / 0.92 = 28.30 W in = +23.1 % over 23 W and +49.8 % over the 18.9 W brake peak. BQ25690 at the ceiling minimum: 1.285 A x 18.62 V = 23.93 W in (+4.0 % vs 23 W), 23.93 x 0.92 = 22.01 W into the pack (+16.5 % vs 18.9 W), 22.01 / 8.4 = 2.62 A, 22.01 / 7.4 = 2.97 A (target and [D690] figures reproduced). Target's structural claim also reproduces: ceiling minimum for 3.1 A at 8.4 V = 3.1 x 8.4 / (0.92 x 18.62) = 1.520 A, x 1.715/1.285 = 2.03 A maximum > 1.72 A HR4 row; this arithmetic is correct but is a bound on a figure that is not the requirement. WD H.7 cross-check: 23 x 0.93 / 7.4 / 0.935 = 3.09 A (WD states 3.05 A; same order, 3.10 A chosen). BQ25773 at 3.04 A, 8.4 V = 25.5 W out vs the HR1 output-equivalent 23 x 0.92 = 21.2 W. Every "Not met" verdict in Table 5.1 sits 16-20 % above the HR1 pass condition in power.
- Source: [EVAL] section 1.1 (HR1 definition, line 281) and 3.3 (lines 592-595), [EVAL] 8.1 item 6 / 8.2 (open pack ruling); [WD] r1-10, H.7 and the register image (3.10 A as a conditional setting, 2.50 A until T-E-11/T-B-19); [D798] 11.3 and 15.1 item 3; [D690] 11.3; [CRIT] M4 and section 4.2; [SCO] Tables 5 and 9. No primary datasheet is needed: the finding is about the provenance of a requirement figure, and the design documents settle it.
- Severity rationale: A published pass/fail verdict ("Not met", bold, Table 5.1; reason 4 of the recommendation; section 7 "which the operator must accept explicitly") is derived from a design setting that the authoritative requirement sources ([EVAL] 1.1, 3.3, 8.1) hold open and at a pack voltage (8.4 V) 13.5 % above the one where the figure was derived (7.4 V). Computed bound: the test is 23 % stricter in input power than HR1's own pass condition, and both parts marked "Not met" pass HR1 by +3.0 to +4.0 % on the target's own numbers. Exposure path: the rule of succession (target:1058-1068) tells the operator the BQ25690 lead "carries its 2.62 A delivered-current shortfall ... which the operator must accept explicitly" - a decision the operator would weigh differently knowing the part exceeds the hard requirement and the brake peak by 16 %. Second exposure: a claim the sources mark open (pack maximum charge current, [EVAL] 8.1 item 6, [WD] T-E-11) is stated as settled ("closed on the CCB", target:1032; [SCO]:278) while the same document lists it as an open ruling (:1086). Not critical: no kill path, back-feed or loop; the scored totals and the ranking are unchanged (target:554-555 "no criterion scores delivered current").
- Minimal fix: (1) Target:77-80, replace reason 4 with: "4. **Every finalist meets the HR1 input condition at the threshold floor** (>= 23 W; Table 5.1). The 3.1 A regen setting of the BQ25798 image ([WD] H.7) is a design choice conditional on the pack ruling; at an 8.4 V pack the BQ25690 (2.62 A, 23.9 W) and the BQ25773 (3.04 A, 23.7 W) deliver less than that setting but 16 % or more above the 18.9 W brake peak." (2) Target:123-127, replace "The regen path needs a charge current of 3.1 A or more" with "The regen path needs >= 23 W input at the threshold (HR1); the BQ25798 image sets ICHG 3.10 A for this at a 7.4 V pack ([WD] H.7: 23 x 0.93 / 7.4 with the -6.5 % ICHG tolerance), conditional on the pack's maximum charge current ([EVAL] section 8.1 item 6)." (3) Table 5.1 (:532-538): rename the column "Against 3.1 A" to "Against the 3.10 A BQ25798 image setting" and replace "**Not met**" with "Below the setting"; keep the HR1 column as the pass/fail column. (4) Target:553, replace "fail the brief's 3.1 A figure" with "deliver less than the BQ25798 3.10 A image setting at the low tolerance corner; all five meet the HR1 input condition". (5) Target:1032-1033 and [SCO]:278 (and the 6.6 reference): replace "closed on the CCB for every part" with "open for every part on the pack ruling ([EVAL] section 8.1 item 6; [WD] T-E-11); every part's ICHG range covers 3.1 A, so the CCB adds no limit". (6) Target:982 and :1067-1068, qualify "delivers 2.62 A, not 3.1 A" as "delivers 2.62 A at an 8.4 V pack, below the BQ25798 3.10 A image setting but 4.0 % above the HR1 input condition". Proof: each replacement number is the recomputation above (2.519 A HR1-equivalent at 8.4 V; 23.93 W / 22.01 W / 2.62 A / 2.97 A for the BQ25690; 28.30 W for 3.1 A at 8.4 V). Follow-on skill: none of test/pinmap-audit/protocol-bump/regen-coeffs/bench-incident; this is a documentation correction that also touches [SCO] Table 9 and section 8.5 (same wording at scoring.md:278 and :429).
- Adjacent findings: Target:534 and :848 mark the BQ25798 'Met at the floor' with 3.10 A only under the reference-biased ceiling, which is itself the open ruling R-CEIL798 (target:1085; REGN divider gives 2.91 A per [D798]:398): the baseline's own 'Met' is conditional and should carry the same qualifier as the challengers' rows.; Target:123 cites '[CRIT] sections 1, 4' for the 3.1 A requirement; [CRIT] section 1 (screening_criteria.md:1-54) contains no charge-current figure and section 4.2 (:187) calls 3.10 A the 'image' with WD H.11 as source - the citation does not support a requirement reading.; Target:535 and [SCO]:162 state the BQ25690 HR1 margin as +3.9 %; 23.93 / 23 = +4.0 % ([D690]:402 says 4.0 %). Rounding drift only.; [WD]:2157 states the ICHG needed for the HR1 23 W condition as 3.05 A at 7.4 V, eta 0.93, ICHG -6.5 %; the stated inputs give 23 x 0.93 / 7.4 / 0.935 = 3.09 A. The chosen 3.10 A still covers it; the intermediate figure is low by about 0.04 A.
- Counter-review: DEFEND (major): Major confirmed and I hold it. The 3.1 A figure is the BQ25798 regen-image ICHG setting ([WD] H.7, derived at a 7.4 V pack: 23 x 0.93 / 7.4 / 0.935 = 3.09 A), held at 2.50 A in [WD] until T-E-11/T-B-19 pass and left open by [EVAL] 8.1 item 6; the target promotes it to a bold pass/fail column and applies it at 8.4 V, where 3.1 A is 28.3 W input at eta 0.92, 23 % above the HR1 pass condition (23 W; 2.52 A at 8.4 V) and 50 % above the 18.9 W brake peak. Both parts marked 'Not met' pass HR1 by +3.0 to +4.0 % on the target's own numbers, and the BQ25690 puts 22.0 W into the pack, 16 % above the brake peak. The rule of succession then tells the operator to 'accept explicitly' a shortfall against a figure the operator has not admitted, while 'closed on the CCB' (:1032) sits beside the same item listed as an open ruling (:1086). That is a decision input the operator would weigh differently and a claim marked open by the authoritative sources stated as settled. Not critical: no kill path, scores unchanged. The six edits are accepted, and they are the frame into which R1-04, R1-07, R1-14 and R1-24 must be fitted.

### CIS-R1-S02 (sweep; reviewer major; final none; rejected)

- Location: docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:882 (Table 6.1 S14 row), 1016 (Table 6.6 finding 1), 588-589; [CRIT] screening_criteria.md:110, 152; [SCO] scoring.md:242-246; [D798] dossier_BQ25798.md:501, 634
- Claim: S14 BQ25798 = 2 (C), BQ25690 = 2, BQ25773 = 2, RAA489110 = 1 ("Reopened" finding 8); Table 6.6 finding 1 for the BQ25798: "Kept, more serious: a CELL write lifts the VREG bound to 18.8 V until POR"; [CRIT] 3.4: a part "reopens a finding if it makes the mechanism worse (more never-write registers, ...)".
- Problem: S14 is defined relative to the BQ25798's own round-6 ledger, so the baseline scores "Net 0" = 2 by construction and holds a guaranteed 5 x 2/3 = 3.33 points that no fact about the BQ25798 can remove under the scoring as applied. The ledger itself records a worsening for the BQ25798: [D798] 501 grades finding 1 "Kept, and more serious than the 2026-11-05 wording implies" (host-writable CELL field, VREG bound 5-9.99 V only while CELL reads 2s, survives REG_RST and watchdog, DS section 7.3.2 pp.25-26) and adds CELL to the read-back / never-write image; review_loop.md contains no CELL or 18.8 V entry, so this is new relative to R6 finding 1. By the [CRIT] 3.4 rule that the document applies to the RAA489110 (S14 = 1 for making finding 8 worse) and in scenario D1 to the TI challengers (a longer never-write list reopens finding 8), the BQ25798 reopens finding 1 (a major finding) and should score S14 = 1. Recomputed: BQ25798 40.31 - 1.67 = 38.6; margins BQ25773 +10.4, LTC4015 +10.1, BQ25690 +9.7. The BQ25773 then meets condition 2 as well as the conditions 1, 3 and 4 the document already grades "Met" (Table 6.4), so the document's own decision rule would recommend the BQ25773 instead of recording it as the alternate. Exposure: a wrong part recommendation by the document's own rule, and a scoring construction that cannot penalise the incumbent on a 5-point criterion.
- Reviewer recommendation: Either score S14 for the BQ25798 by the same reopening rule as the challengers (S14 = 1 on the CELL escalation) and restate Tables 6.1, 6.2, 6.4 and 6.5 and the recommendation accordingly, or redefine S14 on an absolute basis (count of major IC-dependent findings each part carries, with the BQ25798 at 3 kept including finding 1 "more serious") so the baseline is not the zero point of its own criterion. State explicitly in 6.5 that the baseline holds a 3.33-point structural floor on S14 under the present definition.
- Verdict: REFUTED, severity none
- Evidence: 1. [CRIT] screening_criteria.md:110 defines S14 as a net change ("Net reopens >= 2 / 1 / Net 0 / Net closes >= 1"). [CRIT]:152 defines "reopens" as a property of a candidate measured against the BQ25798 design: "makes the mechanism worse (more never-write registers, a slower ADC, a mode change that needs more writes)". The same row defines closure against "the BQ25798 image". The BQ25798 is therefore the reference point of the criterion. It is not a candidate that can move away from itself.
2. The CELL fact ([D798] dossier_BQ25798.md:634, DS SLUSDV2C section 7.3.2, pp.25-26) belongs to the same IC that the round-6 design already used. The CCB does not introduce it. The CCB only changes how R6 finding 1 is worded. R6 finding 1 is keyed "vreg-corruption-no-fixed-pack-ovp" (review_loop.md:391). Its disposition is a fixed hardware backstop that does not depend on VREG: BQ29200 OUT, then T22/U123, then OVN into ARM_OK' (U122), plus the FR-46 read-back. [D798]:634 confirms that "the OVN term from the BQ29200 OUT stays the only fixed pack-OV backstop" on the CCB. A CELL write that lifts VREG to 18.8 V is still a VREG corruption, and the same OVN term catches it at the BQ29200 threshold. The mechanism of R6 finding 1 (no fixed pack OVP) is not made worse. The read-back image only gains CELL, which is the same remedy class as FR-46.
3. The finding's consistency argument fails. The challengers that are "Kept" on finding 1 also have register paths with equal or larger escalations: BQ25773 SYSOVP_MAX raises SYSOVP to 27 V, and RAA489110 MaxSystemVoltage is writable to 18.304 V ([SCO] scoring.md:242, Table 8 row 1). Both still score 2 or are penalised only on finding 8. Penalising the incumbent alone for a newly characterised escalation, while the BQ25773's 27 V path stays at 2, would be the asymmetric reading.
4. The "structural floor" does not bias the margins. A baseline-relative criterion has the baseline as its zero point by definition. A constant offset that every part shares cancels in the condition-2 margin. Challengers can score above the baseline: the LTC4015 B scores S14 = 3 by closing findings 1, 8 and 12 (target line 882; [SCO] Table 8).
5. The target already discloses the CELL escalation as "Kept, more serious" (lines 588-589, 1016; [SCO]:242). It also reports the S14 reading's sensitivity of 1.7 points (line 67, line 999, scenario D1 at line 953). No number is hidden.
- Quantification: I re-ran calc/score.py from a scratch copy with python -I (miniforge). Base totals: BQ25798 40.33, BQ25690 48.33 (+8.00), BQ25773 49.00 (+8.67), LTC4015-B 48.67 (+8.33), RAA489110 35.67 (-4.67). The incumbent's S14 contributes 5 x 2/3 = 3.33 points. With the finding's alternative of BQ25798 S14 = 1, the incumbent scores 40.33 - 1.67 = 38.67. The margins become BQ25773 +10.33, LTC4015-B +10.00 and BQ25690 +9.67. The finding's 38.6 / +10.4 / +10.1 / +9.7 differ only by rounding (it used 40.31). The arithmetic of the counterfactual is correct. The counterfactual is invalid because its premise misreads the [CRIT] 3.4 definition, which is a candidate-versus-BQ25798 delta. The OVN backstop is independent of VREG, so it bounds every VREG value, 18.8 V included.
- Source: I settled this against the working documents' own definitions and against R6 itself. [CRIT] screening_criteria.md:110 and :152 give the relative definition and the reopen rule. review_loop.md:391 gives the R6 finding 1 key and the OVN disposition. [D798] dossier_BQ25798.md:634 (DS SLUSDV2C section 7.3.2, pp.25-26) shows the CELL path is intrinsic to the same IC and that the OVN backstop is retained on the CCB. [SCO] scoring.md:242-246 gives Table 8, where the challengers' finding-1 escalations are kept at 27 V and 18.304 V. calc/score.py supplied the recomputed totals.
- Severity rationale: The claimed exposure, a wrong part recommendation, depends on reading S14 as an absolute severity count that applies to the incumbent alone. [CRIT] 3.4 defines S14 as a delta against the BQ25798 design, so under the rule as written the incumbent scores 2. The CELL escalation is the same IC property that R6 finding 1's fixed OVN backstop already covers. The mechanism (no fixed pack OVP) is unchanged, and the target discloses the escalation in three places. A symmetric absolute reading would also downgrade the BQ25773 for its 27 V SYSOVP_MAX path, so the incumbent-only penalty does not hold. The challengers can exceed the reference (LTC4015 B = 3), so the zero point cancels in the margin. I found no wrong published number and no hidden decision input. At most this is an optional wording clarification.
- Minimal fix: No scoring change is needed. One optional clarification would make the zero point explicit in section 6.5 of the target, or in a note under Table 6.1. Append: "S14 is scored as a change against the BQ25798 round-6 design ([CRIT] section 3.4), so the BQ25798 scores 2 by definition. The CELL escalation of R6 finding 1 is a property of that same reference design. It is not scored as a reopening because the fixed OVN backstop from the BQ29200 OUT bounds the pack voltage for any VREG value ([D798] section 14 row 1). The challengers' own finding-1 register paths (BQ25773 SYSOVP_MAX to 27 V, RAA489110 MaxSystemVoltage to 18.304 V) are treated the same way." This is a documentation-only change and triggers no follow-on skill.
- Adjacent findings: Table 8 row 1 ([SCO]:244) grades the BQ25690 as 'Kept, mitigated: VREG clamped by strap'. That reads as a partial improvement over the incumbent's CELL path, but it scores the same 2. It is evidence status U (T-14), so the [CRIT] 3.2 cap of 1 would block any credit anyway. Worth one sentence if R-S14 is ruled, but no score change.; The finding uses 40.31 for the BQ25798 baseline. score.py gives 40.33, and the target rounds it to 40.3. This is consistent, with no action.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (none): Refuted on the premise, and I accept it. [CRIT] 3.4 defines S14 as a candidate-versus-BQ25798 delta, so the incumbent scores 2 by construction and the zero point cancels in every margin (the LTC4015 B = 3 shows challengers can exceed the reference). The CELL escalation of R6 finding 1 is a property of the same reference design, bounded by the fixed OVN backstop independent of VREG, and a symmetric absolute reading would also downgrade the BQ25773 for its 27 V SYSOVP_MAX path, so no incumbent-only penalty follows. My counterfactual arithmetic (38.67; +10.33/+10.00/+9.67) was correct but invalid. The optional note making the zero point explicit under Table 6.1 is worth adding because the construction is otherwise opaque to a reader.

### CIS-R1-S03 (sweep; reviewer minor; final minor; accepted)

- Location: docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:993-1000 (6.5 reason 1); [CRIT] screening_criteria.md:159-161; [SCO] scoring.md:455-458
- Claim: "Switching cost. A switch discards the BQ25798 CCB layout work in progress, six review rounds, the 112-entry test registry and the firmware register image with its poll schedule ... It needs a new driver, a new never-write list and readback image, a new test registry and at least one review cycle of the same depth."
- Problem: The first clause is sunk cost (review rounds already spent, registry already written) and is not a forward cost of switching. Of the forward costs listed, the firmware driver is symmetric: teensy_controller.ino contains no BQ25798 code (grep count 0 for BQ25798 in teensy_controller/teensy_controller.ino of this worktree; the firmware drives the Ag105 only), so a driver, never-write list and readback image must be written for whichever part is chosen; the BQ25773 and BQ25690 dossiers already carry their never-write lists ([D773] sections 5.1, 7.2; [D690] section 16). The only asymmetric forward cost is the CCB layout in progress plus one review cycle, whose magnitude the document does not estimate. The 10-point value is therefore not derived from any cost quantity, and the one reason that is not already challenged (CIS-R1-01 on reason 3, CIS-R1-03 on reason 2) rests on sunk cost. Exposure: a decision the operator would make differently; a thesis committee would read reason 1 as incumbent protection rather than a cost argument.
- Reviewer recommendation: Separate sunk from forward costs in 6.5; state the forward asymmetric cost (layout hours or weeks in progress, one review round) and either derive the margin from it in score points or state that the 10-point figure is a judgement. Remove the driver, never-write list and readback image from the switching-cost list, or note that they are owed for the BQ25798 too.
- Verdict: CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: Target 6.5 reason 1: C:/Life Ops/School/Thesis/DC-Balancer-charger-search/docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:995-997 ("A switch discards the BQ25798 CCB layout work in progress, six review rounds, the 112-entry test registry and the firmware register image with its poll schedule ... It needs a new driver, a new never-write list and readback image, a new test registry and at least one review cycle"). Same wording in [SCO] C:/Life Ops/School/Thesis/DC-Balancer-charger-search/docs/reviews/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10/scoring.md:455-458 and [CRIT] screening_criteria.md:159-161 (condition 2: "The margin covers the switching cost" with no derivation).

(1) Firmware driver is symmetric. `grep -c BQ25798` on C:/Life Ops/School/Thesis/DC-Balancer-charger-search/teensy_controller/teensy_controller.ino = 0 (the case-insensitive BQ257 hits at lines 807 and 828 are BQ25690 removal notes in the changelog; the readback/never-write hits at 775, 2568, 3835, 12178, 15954-15991 are the Ag105 MPPT ratchet). [EVAL] C:/Life Ops/School/Thesis/DC-Balancer-charger-search/docs/charger_replacement_evaluation_2026-11-05.md:834 lists "New driver | BQ25798 at 0x6B; static + mode images; masked verify; never-write list; WD kick policy; POR/WD re-image; non-blocking mode manager" as FUTURE firmware work, and :854-859 lists the ~60 charger tests to rewrite and the never-write/masked-verify tests still to be written. So the driver, never-write list and readback image are owed for the BQ25798 too; they are not a cost a switch adds.

(2) Never-write lists already exist on paper for the two TI challengers: BQ25773 dossier_BQ25773_SMBus_twin_BQ25770G.md:197-200 (seven fields, section 5.1; :646 confirms "seven fields at eight register positions"); BQ25690 dossier_BQ25690.md:734-742 (table of never-write bits) and :749-754 (S14 comparison against the baseline list) — this is section 14, not section 16 as the finding cites. The BQ25798 list is itself a paper list at [EVAL]:740.

(3) The "firmware register image with its poll schedule" ([EVAL] 4.5, :738-765) is a 17-row paper table plus a poll-schedule paragraph; it is not implemented (grep 0). Writing an equivalent table for a challenger is a small forward asymmetric cost; the dossiers hold partial register content but no full image.

(4) The 112-entry test registry is real (112 unique `| T-` rows in docs/charger_bq25798_wiring_design_2026-11-05.md section I at :2222; groups T-A 12, T-B 26, T-C 17, T-D 15, T-E 14, T-F 19, T-G 7, T-W 2). Of these, 40 rows name BQ25798 registers/I2C behaviour (IC-specific), 28 name EVM/EIB/carrier/J101/JP geometry (obsolete for the BQ25798 CCB as well: target :1027 "Findings 13-16 (EVM-carrier geometry) close for every part"; :149 "Lead-length limits no longer apply"), and 55 name neither (eFuse, comparator, motherboard, watchdog tests that carry over to any IC). "Discards the 112-entry test registry" therefore overstates: at most 40-63 entries are IC-specific, and the registry already needs rework for the baseline CCB.

(5) "Six review rounds" are spent and reviewed the EVM-carrier design (target :38 "[R6] ... review round 6 of the BQ25798 carrier design"); their IC-independent findings (2, 4, 5, 6, 7, 9, 11) apply to every candidate per [CRIT]:151. Sunk, and partly transferable.

(6) Layout work in progress is unquantified anywhere: `git log main..charger-bq25798-custom-board` is empty (branch at 330344c = main) and `git ls-tree` of that branch contains no layout/kicad/CCB file; the target gives no hours/weeks estimate. (The sibling worktree itself was not inspected, per the brief.)

(7) The 10-point figure is derived from no cost quantity: [CRIT]:159-161 asserts it; score.py (calc/score.py:1-55) has no threshold constant; [SCO] reason 3 (:465-467) is the only arithmetic anchor (two S1 levels).
- Quantification: Scores recomputed from calc/score.py rows (weight x level / 3): BQ25798 = (15+10+16+16+7+0+10+6+0+7+3+10+2+10+9+0)/3 = 121/3 = 40.33; BQ25773 = (15+20+24+16+7+10+10+6+8+0+3+10+2+10+6+0)/3 = 147/3 = 49.00; margin 8.67, shortfall to the threshold 10 - 8.67 = 1.33 points. S1 weight 15 -> one S1 level = 15/3 = 5.0 points, two levels = 10.0 points: reason 3's equivalence is arithmetically correct and is the only quantitative anchor of the threshold. Firmware: 0 occurrences of "BQ25798" in teensy_controller.ino (the 10 case-insensitive BQ257 hits are all BQ25690 changelog text). Test registry: 112 unique T- rows; 40 register/I2C-specific, 28 EVM-geometry (obsolete for the baseline CCB too), 55 IC-independent (sets overlap). Decision sensitivity: the BQ25773 meets conditions 1, 3 and 4 (target :926-934) and fails only condition 2 by 1.33 points, so any cost-derived threshold at or below 8.67 would flip it from alternate to recommended; no document bounds the forward asymmetric cost either side of that line.
- Source: Repo firmware (teensy_controller.ino grep) and git history for the symmetric-driver and unquantified-layout premises; [EVAL] sections 4.5 and 5.2 for the register image and the planned BQ25798 driver; working documents dossier_BQ25773 section 5.1 and dossier_BQ25690 section 14 for the challengers' never-write lists; charger_bq25798_wiring_design_2026-11-05.md section I for the registry count and classification; [CRIT] 3.5 and calc/score.py for the threshold's (absent) derivation and the S1 arithmetic.
- Severity rationale: Doc-argument defect, not a wrong number: reason 1 states sunk costs (six rounds, the written registry) and symmetric forward costs (driver, never-write list, readback image, which [EVAL] 5.2 lists as new work for the BQ25798 itself) as if they were asymmetric switching costs, and overstates the registry loss (at most 40-63 of 112 entries are IC-specific; 28 are EVM-geometry rows the baseline CCB must also redo). Exposure path: the BQ25773 fails only condition 2, by 1.33 points, so the threshold's basis is decision-determining for the alternate. Bounded away from major because the threshold has two independent supports that do not rest on reason 1: reason 3 is arithmetically exact (two S1 levels = 2 x 15/3 = 10.0 points) and reason 2's evidence band (single-part movements of 2.3-4.0 points against 8.0-8.7 margins) independently justifies a margin at or above the base spread. Removing or correcting reason 1 leaves the 10-point figure standing and the recommendation (keep the BQ25798; BQ25773 alternate) unchanged. No hardware, protocol or test exposure.
- Minimal fix: Replace target 6.5 item 1 (charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:995-997) with:

"1. **Switching cost.** Only forward work that a challenger adds over the baseline enters the margin. That work is the CCB layout in progress (extent not recorded in the repository), one register image and poll schedule of the kind in [EVAL] section 4.5, the IC-specific part of the test registry (about 40 of the 112 entries name BQ25798 registers or I2C behaviour; the eFuse, comparator, motherboard and watchdog entries carry over to any IC), and at least one review cycle of the same depth. The firmware driver, never-write list and readback image are owed for every part, including the BQ25798 (`teensy_controller.ino` contains no BQ25798 code; [EVAL] section 5.2 lists the driver as new work), and the BQ25773 and BQ25690 dossiers already give their never-write lists ([D773] section 5.1; [D690] section 14). The six review rounds and the registry entries already written are spent whichever part is chosen and do not enter the margin. The 10-point figure is a judgement; its quantitative anchor is reason 3."

Apply the same substance to [SCO] scoring.md:455-458 and shorten [CRIT] screening_criteria.md:159-161 condition 2 to: "Its weighted score exceeds the BQ25798 score by at least 10 points. The margin is a judgement that covers the forward cost of a switch (the CCB layout in progress, a new register image, the IC-specific test entries and one review cycle); section 9 of scoring.md gives its basis."

Proof: every number in the replacement is recomputed above (grep 0; 112 rows; 40/28/55 classification; two S1 levels = 10.0); the never-write citations are dossier_BQ25773 :197-200 and dossier_BQ25690 :734-754; the "owed for every part" claim is [EVAL]:834 and :854-859. The fix changes no score, margin or ranking. Follow-on skill: none of test / pinmap-audit / protocol-bump / regen-coeffs / bench-incident applies; doc-only edit.
- Adjacent findings: Target :996 and [SCO] :456-457 call [EVAL] 4.5 a 'firmware register image'; it is a paper table ([EVAL]:738-765) and no BQ25798 firmware exists (grep 0) - the phrase reads as implemented code and should say 'register image table'.; 'Discards the 112-entry test registry' is overstated for the baseline too: 28 of the 112 rows are EVM/EIB/carrier geometry tests that the BQ25798 CCB itself retires (target :1027, :149), so the registry is already partly obsolete whichever IC is chosen.; The BQ25773 fails only decision-rule condition 2 and by exactly 1.33 points (49.00 - 40.33 = 8.67 vs 10); section 6.5 does not state that the threshold is decision-determining for the alternate, which a reader needs to weigh the judgement.; The finding cites the BQ25690 never-write list as '[D690] section 16'; it is in section 14 (dossier_BQ25690.md:725-754); section 16 is the verdict/comparison (:821).; The 'parallel session is laying out a custom BQ25798 board' ([CRIT]:160-161) has no committed trace: branch charger-bq25798-custom-board is at 330344c = main with no layout files, so the layout-in-progress cost is unverifiable from the repository.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): Confirmed minor. Reason 1 mixes sunk costs (six rounds, the written registry) and symmetric forward costs (driver, never-write list, readback image: 0 occurrences of BQ25798 in teensy_controller.ino; [EVAL] 5.2 lists the driver as new work for the BQ25798 itself) with the one asymmetric forward cost (CCB layout in progress plus one review cycle), and overstates the registry loss (40 IC-specific of 112; 28 EVM-geometry rows the baseline CCB must also redo). The BQ25773 fails only condition 2, by 1.33 points, so the threshold's basis is decision-determining for the alternate, but reason 3 (two S1 levels = 10.0 exactly) and reason 2 (evidence band) support the 10-point figure independently of reason 1, so removing it leaves the recommendation standing. The replacement text and the [CRIT] condition-2 shortening accepted.

### CIS-R1-S04 (sweep; reviewer minor; final minor; accepted)

- Location: docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:75-76 (reason 3), 581, 615, 669, 716, 755, 1041; [SCO] scoring.md:189-196, 505

The claim is defensible only on total CCB part count (power stage plus adders: BQ25798 38-39, BQ25690 42, BQ25773 65), which the sentence does not say. Exposure: a recommendation reason stated as fact that the reader can falsify from Table 6 / section 5, which weakens the document's credibility on the reasons that do hold; a committee would strike it.

Total CCB parts (stage plus adders): BQ25798 27-28 + 11 = 38-39; BQ25690 30 + 3 + 9 = 42; BQ25773 57 + 8 = 65. The finding's numbers reproduce from [SCO] Tables 6 and 7. ### CIS-R1-S05 (sweep; reviewer minor; final minor; accepted)

- Location: docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:81-82 (reason 5), 1070-1071 (P11 parallel measurement), 1088-1099 (8.2 bench gates), 1054-1056 (S1-B4 condition)
- Claim: "No challenger resolves the decisive unknown ... Only a bench measurement resolves it"; "The 2026-11-05 recommendation to measure two inhibit mechanisms in parallel ([EVAL] section 0.3, P11) remains valid for the CCB, with the BQ25773 as the second mechanism. This document orders nothing."; Table 8.2 lists S1-B4, S1-B3, CCB-1, CCB-2 (BQ25798), T-5.HIZ, T-3.VRECHG, T-8a (BQ25690), B4, B13 (BQ25773) as gates that change the outcome.
- Problem: The document names the bench gates that decide the recommendation (S1-B4 decides M9 for the baseline; T-5.HIZ decides condition 1 for the BQ25690; B4 decides the BQ25773 burst-entry hazard) but gives the operator no vehicle, cost or sequence for any of them. On the CCB framing the 2026-11-05 P11 vehicle (two EVMs) is no longer the design under test: S1-B4 on the BQ25798 needs the reference-biased ceiling and REGN bias of the CCB; B4 for the BQ25773 needs the D-BT-SQ drained-VCHG topology (a stock BQ25773EVM carries the 167.6 uF input bank the re-screen voided as a form-factor defect, Table 4.1) and the recommended build is a 57-part, 7-FET, 590 mm2 controller board (5.2). "Measure two mechanisms in parallel" on the CCB therefore means two custom boards, or an EVM measurement whose transfer to the CCB the document does not argue. The operator cannot decide whether to fund the alternate's gates from this document, and the recommendation's own condition S1-B4 has no date or prerequisite. Exposure: a decision the operator cannot schedule; the "alternate" status carries no actionable next step.
- Reviewer recommendation: Add a short gate plan to section 8.2: for each decisive gate, the test vehicle (BQ25798 CCB prototype, BQ25773EVM with input bank reduced, or a BQ25773 CCB), what transfers from an EVM measurement to the CCB (warm response and /CE or ILIM_HIZ exit latency transfer; burst entry behind D-BT-SQ does not), the order (S1-B4 first, since its failure changes the question), and an order-of-magnitude cost. State whether P11 now means two custom boards.
- Verdict: PARTLY_CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: CONFIRMED PART (gap exists). Target C:/Life Ops/School/Thesis/DC-Balancer-charger-search/docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:1088-1099 (Table 8.2) has three columns: Gate / Part / Effect on the decision. No vehicle, prerequisite, order or cost column. Lines 1070-1071 ("measure two inhibit mechanisms in parallel ([EVAL] section 0.3, P11) remains valid for the CCB ... This document orders nothing") are a verbatim carry of scoring.md:519-521. The P11 vehicle that this sentence points at is EVM-based: charger_replacement_evaluation_2026-11-05.md:220 ("Adopted: section 7 buys 2 x BQ25798EVM + 1 x BQ25773EVM"), :973-974 (BQ25798EVM USD 156.45 x2, BQ25773EVM USD 199.00 x1), :990 ("EVMs USD 511.90"), :999 ("Track 1 S1 ... BQ25773 in parallel, 4-6 bench days, week 3"). The target's own framing (lines 5-14) retires the EVM as a screening criterion and names the BQ25798 CCB as the design under test, but never states whether the P11 EVM purchase is still the measurement vehicle or whether S1-B4 now waits for a CCB prototype. Condition S1-B4 at lines 1054-1056 and 88-89 carries no prerequisite or date. So the operator cannot read from this document what to buy, in what order, or when the recommendation's own condition can close. REFUTED PARTS (finding's mechanisms). (a) "S1-B4 on the BQ25798 needs the reference-biased ceiling and REGN bias of the CCB": dossier_BQ25798.md:550 marks S1-B4/B4r "Unchanged"; the CCB-specific gates are S1-B3 (:549, recomputed latency with the reference-biased ceiling), S1-B6 (:552, calibrates the CCB divider) and CCB-1 (:556, REGN-ordering closure). The warm-response tau is a VINDPM-loop property measured with ILIM_HIZ released; the divider sets the IINDPM level, not tau. The one vehicle prerequisite the sources do state is EVAL:907 ("real RT1987 in the D-BT-SQ position, VCHG below VBT at release", panel P2 at EVAL:211), which an EVM satisfies. (b) "B4 needs the D-BT-SQ drained-VCHG topology; a stock BQ25773EVM carries the 167.6 uF input bank": the drained node in dossier_BQ25773:379-397 is VSYS/VCHG (the battery-side 9 x 10 uF bank of its section 12.3), not the VBUS input bank; the 167.6 uF figure (rescreen:142) is the EVM INPUT capacitance and is irrelevant to the drain. The dominant drain load is IQ_HIZ 1.00-1.15 mA (DS EC p.13 via dossier:387), IC-intrinsic; remedies 1 and 2 (BATFETOFF_HIZ = 1, EN_LDO = 0; dossier:402-405) are register writes and transfer from any vehicle; only remedy 3 (VBT keep-alive, 3 adders, :406-407) and the divider-only 1 V/s slow-drain case are CCB-specific. B13 (ILIM_HIZ held LOW through power-up, dossier:670) and T-5.HIZ (dossier_BQ25690:772, pin level and strap re-read) are pin/register behaviours, also IC-intrinsic. Therefore "P11 now means two custom boards" does not follow from the sources; one BQ25773EVM with an RT1987 in the D-BT-SQ position covers B2, B4 (remedies 1-2) and B13.
- Quantification: Drain-time transfer check for B4 (t = C x dV / I; dossier_BQ25773:389-392 inputs): CCB, divider only: 90e-6 F x 1.10 V / 88e-6 A = 1.125 s; CCB, divider + IQ_HIZ max: 90e-6 x 0.75 / (1.15e-3 + 88e-6) = 0.0545 s (reproduces the dossier's 0.06-1.1 s). Any vehicle without the CCB divider, IQ_HIZ only: 90e-6 x 0.75 / 1.15e-3 = 0.059 s to 90e-6 x 1.10 / 1.00e-3 = 0.099 s. The B4 sub-case "burst after more than 1 s of cruise HIZ" (dossier:409, :662) reaches the VBAT_PRECHG threshold on either vehicle by a factor of >= 10 in time, so the pre-charge entry and the 5 ms tTRANS_CHG (DS section 6.6 p.21) are observable on an EVM; only the 1 V/s divider-only corner is CCB-specific. Cost figures the sources carry: BQ25773EVM USD 199.00 (EVAL:974), BQ25798EVM USD 156.45 (EVAL:973; dossier_BQ25798:491, 161 in stock 2026-11-10), P11 set USD 511.90 (EVAL:990), S1 bench stage 4-6 bench days (EVAL:999). No BQ25690 vehicle price exists in the sources (BQ25690EVM existence UNVERIFIED, dossier_BQ25690:45).
- Source: Working documents and [EVAL] (authority tiers 3-4 of the brief): dossier_BQ25798.md section 15.2 (gate changes on the CCB), dossier_BQ25773_SMBus_twin_BQ25770G.md sections 8.3 and 15.1 (drain mechanism, B4/B13 definitions), dossier_BQ25690.md line 45 and 772, rescreen_2026-11-05_candidates.md:142, charger_replacement_evaluation_2026-11-05.md sections 0.3 (P2, P11), 6.2 (S1-B4) and 7 (vehicle, cost, schedule). No primary datasheet fetch was needed: the finding is about what the target states, not about a datasheet number.
- Severity rationale: No wrong published number, no hazard path, no circular loop, and the recommendation (keep BQ25798) and shortlist order are unchanged by the gap. The exposure is documentation completeness: the operator cannot schedule or price the gates that section 7 makes the recommendation conditional on, and the P11 pointer resolves to an EVM plan under a framing that retired EVMs without saying the plan still stands. The finding's stronger claim (two custom boards needed, S1-B4 requires CCB-specific bias) is refuted by the working documents, so no wasted-board-spin exposure can be constructed. Caps at minor per the scale (doc drift / missing hedge).
- Minimal fix: Two edits to docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md. (1) Replace lines 1070-1071 with: "The 2026-11-05 recommendation to measure two inhibit mechanisms in parallel ([EVAL] section 0.3, P11) remains valid for the CCB, with the BQ25773 as the second mechanism. The P11 vehicle is unchanged: two BQ25798EVM and one BQ25773EVM (USD 511.90, [EVAL] section 7), each with a real RT1987 in the D-BT-SQ position ([EVAL] section 0.3, P2). The warm-response, inhibit-exit and register-remedy gates (S1-B4, S1-B4r, B2, B4 remedies 1 and 2, B13, T-5.HIZ) measure IC properties and transfer from an EVM to the CCB; the gates that measure the CCB divider or bias (S1-B3, S1-B6, CCB-1, CCB-2, B4 remedy 3) run on the CCB prototype. S1-B4 runs first, because its failure changes the question (condition 2). No vehicle for T-5.HIZ is confirmed (BQ25690EVM existence UNVERIFIED, [D690] section 1). This document orders nothing." (2) Add a column "Vehicle; order" to Table 8.2 with the rows: S1-B4/S1-B4r "BQ25798EVM + RT1987 D-BT-SQ ([EVAL] 6.2); first"; S1-B3, CCB-1, CCB-2 "BQ25798 CCB prototype"; T-5.HIZ, T-3.VRECHG, T-8a "BQ25690 vehicle UNVERIFIED"; B4, B13 "BQ25773EVM + RT1987 D-BT-SQ (remedies 1-2, B13); CCB for remedy 3"; L-2, L-3 "only if the TI warm response fails". Proof: every figure in the wording is cited above (EVAL:907, :973-974, :990; D798:549-557; D773:402-407, :670; D690:45); the transfer statements follow from the drain arithmetic in QUANTIFICATION. Follow-on skill class: none (doc-only); no test, pinmap, protocol or coefficient change.
- Adjacent findings: Rule of succession (target 1062-1063) promotes the BQ25690 to lead if T-5.HIZ passes, but the evidence base has no confirmed test vehicle for T-5.HIZ: dossier_BQ25690.md:45 marks the BQ25690EVM as UNVERIFIED and 'not relevant to the CCB', and no BQ25690 CCB design exists; the succession order therefore rests on a gate that cannot currently be run.; Target Table 8.2 omits the one vehicle prerequisite the sources do state for S1-B4: 'real RT1987 in the D-BT-SQ position, VCHG below VBT at release' (EVAL:907, panel P2 at EVAL:211); dossier_BQ25798.md:550 carries S1-B4 as 'Unchanged', so the prerequisite is inherited silently.; Finding CIS-R1-S05 misattributes the BQ25773 drain to the 167.6 uF EVM input bank; dossier_BQ25773:379-392 drains the VSYS/VCHG (battery-side) node, and the re-screen's 167.6 uF figure is input capacitance (rescreen:142). If the reviewer's text is adopted anywhere, this attribution must not be copied.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): My stronger claim (two custom boards needed; S1-B4 requires CCB-specific bias) is refuted by the drain arithmetic: the B4 sub-case 'burst after > 1 s of cruise HIZ' reaches VBAT_PRECHG on either vehicle by a factor >= 10 in time (0.0545-1.125 s CCB; 0.059-0.099 s EVM), so the IC-property gates transfer from EVMs and only the divider/bias gates (S1-B3, S1-B6, CCB-1, CCB-2, B4 remedy 3) need the CCB prototype.
