### CIS-R1-S06 (sweep; reviewer minor; final minor; accepted)

- Location: docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md:685-686, 849 (Table 6.0b contrary fact), 977-980 (6.4 statement 5), 1063, 1097; [CRIT] screening_criteria.md:63-78 (must-have set), 154-166 (decision rule)
- Claim: BQ25690: "A /CE edge starts a cycle only with SRN below VRECHG (section 6.3.8.2.2 p.28), so capture at a pack above about 8.0-8.15 V is not established (T-3.VRECHG)"; succession rule: the BQ25690 leads after T-5.HIZ because "its HR1 hazard has a firmware-side test (T-3.VRECHG), whereas the BQ25773 burst-entry hazard needs a hardware remedy"; Table 6.0b lists this as an HR1 "documented contrary fact".
- Problem: The must-have set (M1-M13) has no criterion for charge start at any pack voltage in the 7.4-8.4 V window, and decision-rule condition 1 tests must-haves only, so a part with a documented contrary fact against hard requirement HR1 (Table 1.1) passes condition 1 and can lead under the rule of succession. On the datasheet fact as recorded, the BQ25690 does not start regen capture whenever the pack sits above 8.02-8.15 V, which is the upper 25-35 % of the operating voltage window and the state after any full charge; this is a documented HR1 failure at those SoCs, not a bench unknown. The succession rule then prefers the BQ25690 over the BQ25773 partly because its hazard "has a firmware-side test": T-3.VRECHG is a bench gate (Table 8.2), and no firmware action can lower an IC-internal SRN comparator threshold, so the characterisation is unsupported. Exposure: a wrong part recommendation (lead under the succession rule) for a part whose datasheet already denies regen harvest at high SoC; the document's own 1.3 rule ("A candidate that fails a must-have on a documented contrary fact is rejected") has no must-have through which this fact can act.
- Reviewer recommendation: Either add to the must-have set a capture-start condition ("regen charging starts at any pack voltage from 7.4 V to the CV setpoint without a recharge-threshold gate, or the gate is register-settable to >= 8.4 V") and grade the BQ25690 against it, or carry the VRECHG fact as an HR1 F-at-high-SoC entry in Tables 5.1 and 6.0b with the SoC fraction affected. Replace "firmware-side test" with "bench gate T-3.VRECHG" and remove it as a reason in the succession rule unless a datasheet mechanism (for example a VRECHG register setting above the CV point) is cited from SLUSFN4A.
- Verdict: PARTLY_CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: CONFIRMED parts. (1) "Firmware-side test" is unsupported: T-3.VRECHG is a bench gate everywhere else in the record - target Table 8.2 line 1097 ("T-3.VRECHG, T-8a | BQ25690 | Regen capture above about 8.0 V"), target line 1063 ("gates T-8a, T-3.VRECHG"), [D690] section 15.1 line 768 (pass condition: "/CE falling edge with SRN at 8.1 V and at 8.35 V ... Records whether a charge cycle starts above VRECHG"). The only firmware-reachable parameter is VRECHG (REG0x10 bits 5:4, SLUSFN4A Table 6-23 p.49), whose maximum is 97 % x VREG = 8.148 V; no setting reaches the 8.4 V CV point, so firmware cannot remove the gate. The phrase appears at target lines 979-980 and is copied from [SCO] lines 426-427. (2) Terminology inconsistency: Table 6.0b (target line 846) codes the BQ25690 HR1 status "U (B)" while line 849 files the same fact under a row headed "HR1 documented contrary fact"; "documented contrary fact" is the defined trigger for a must-have failure (target line 163; [CRIT] line 61). The 6.1 prose at line 857-858 calls the same entries "a documented HR1 hazard", which is the consistent term. (3) No must-have covers a capture start across 7.4-8.4 V: [CRIT] lines 63-77 (M1-M13). The target routes the fact into M9 as a conditional pass (line 831: "M9 (/CE delay typical, VRECHG start condition)"), but M9's pass condition ([CRIT] line 73) names only deglitch/POR/soft-start >= 10 ms, so a bench no-start would not fail M9 as written. REFUTED parts. (4) The finding's premise that the datasheet establishes a no-start above VRECHG ("a documented HR1 failure at those SoCs, not a bench unknown") is wrong. SLUSFN4A p.28 section 6.3.8.2.2 lists "VBAT < VRECHG" among the new-cycle conditions AND states "After the charging terminates, toggling either CE pin or EN_CHG bit initiates a new charging cycle"; p.30 section 6.3.8.2.4 defines termination as occurring with battery voltage above the recharge threshold. The datasheet therefore documents a /CE-edge start above VRECHG from the terminated state, and the U/B coding with a bench gate ([D690] lines 457-461, 797-798) is the correct reading. Datasheet copy: scratch download of https://www.ti.com/lit/ds/symlink/bq25690.pdf, MD5 95895e694b73cf1e851af891d5c99e4e = the MD5 recorded in [D690] line 898. (5) The succession order does not depend on the "firmware-side" reason: reasons 1 and 2 at target lines 978-979 (1.10 A vs 1.39 A FC bound after POR; about 30 vs 57 parts) stand on their own, and line 1063 already conditions the BQ25690 lead on T-3.VRECHG.
- Quantification: VRECHG POR 10b: 0.955 x 8.400 = 8.022 V; maximum 11b: 0.970 x 8.400 = 8.148 V (Table 6-23 p.49); the dossier's 8.02 / 8.15 V reproduce. Fraction of the 7.4-8.4 V voltage window above the gate: (8.400 - 8.022)/1.000 = 37.8 % at POR, (8.400 - 8.148)/1.000 = 25.2 % at the 97 % setting - the finding's "25-35 %" understates the POR case and is a voltage-window fraction, not an SoC fraction (about the top 10-25 % SoC at 4.01-4.07 V/cell OCV). No VRECHG code >= 8.4 V exists (gap to CV 0.252 V at the maximum setting), so no register write lifts the gate; the only register route (VREG >= 8.66 V so that 0.97 x VREG >= 8.4 V) violates M3 and the R6 finding-1 never-write discipline. Effect on the published scores: none (no criterion scores the start condition; the 40.3 / 48.3 / 49.0 / 48.7 / 35.7 totals are unaffected). Effect on the succession order: none (two of three reasons remain and the lead is already gated on T-3.VRECHG).
- Source: Primary datasheet TI SLUSFN4A (BQ25690, Rev. A, Oct 2025 rev. Dec 2025): p.28 section 6.3.8.2.2 (start conditions; /CE or EN_CHG toggle after termination), p.30 section 6.3.8.2.4 (termination requires VBAT above the recharge threshold), p.49 Table 6-23 (VRECHG 5:4, reset 0x2, codes 92.7/94.1/95.5/97 % x VREG). Working documents: [CRIT] section 2 lines 57-77 and section 3.5 lines 154-166; [D690] sections 8.3 (lines 453-461), 11.3 (lines 591-593), 15.1 (line 768), 15.2 item 9 (lines 797-798), verification log item 3 (line 905); [SCO] section 8.5 lines 421-431. Target: lines 163, 685-686, 831, 846-849, 857-858, 977-980, 1063, 1097.
- Severity rationale: Doc drift and an unsupported characterisation, with no exposure path to a wrong part recommendation: the headline recommendation (keep the BQ25798) does not involve the BQ25690 at all; the succession rule applies only under strict M2 or an S1-B4 failure, only after T-5.HIZ passes, and its BQ25690 lead is already gated on T-3.VRECHG (line 1063), so re-labelling the test as a bench gate changes no ordering. The datasheet does not establish a no-start above VRECHG (the /CE-toggle-after-termination sentence on the same page documents a start above it), so the "documented HR1 failure" exposure the finding asserts does not exist on the record; the fact is correctly U/B. The residual real defect is that a defined term ("documented contrary fact", the must-have fail trigger) is applied in Table 6.0b to conditional HR1 hazards, and that no must-have would convert a failed T-3.VRECHG into a rejection - a rule-set gap that only matters after a bench failure, which the document already names as a gate. No computed bound, kill path or published number is wrong.
- Minimal fix: Three text edits, no score or order change. (a) Target lines 979-980 (section 6.4 statement 5), and the same sentence at [SCO] lines 426-427: replace "its HR1 hazard has a firmware-side test (T-3.VRECHG)" with "its HR1 hazard is decided by one bench gate (T-3.VRECHG) with no hardware change, whereas the BQ25773 burst-entry hazard needs a hardware remedy that is not yet proven". Proof: T-3.VRECHG is defined as a bench measurement ([D690] 15.1 line 768; target Table 8.2 line 1097), and VRECHG's maximum code is 97 % x VREG = 8.148 V < 8.4 V (Table 6-23 p.49), so no firmware setting resolves it. (b) Target line 849 row header: rename "HR1 documented contrary fact" to "HR1 documented hazard (conditional; bench gate)" so the row no longer uses the 1.3 must-have fail term for entries coded U (B) in line 846; this matches the 6.1 prose at lines 857-858 ("a documented HR1 hazard"). (c) Target line 686 and line 848 BQ25690 cell: append the affected band, "about 25-38 % of the 7.4-8.4 V window (8.022 V at the POR setting, 8.148 V at the 97 % maximum; SLUSFN4A p.28 also states that a /CE toggle after termination starts a new cycle, so the status is B)". Optional, if the operator wants the rule-set gap closed: add to [CRIT] section 2 a note under M9 that a bench no-start on inhibit exit at any pack voltage within 7.4-8.4 V counts as a documented restart failure for M9 (bench-gate class), so that a failed T-3.VRECHG has a must-have through which it rejects the part. Follow-on skill class: none beyond doc edit (no test, pinmap, protocol, coefficient or bench-incident action); the bench gate T-3.VRECHG itself is unchanged.
- Adjacent findings: Table 6.0b row 'HR1 documented contrary fact' (target line 849) applies the section 1.3 must-have fail term to all four challengers while line 846 codes three of them U (B); the same drift exists in [SCO] Table 5 line 164. Only the BQ25773 tTRANS_CHG 5 ms > 4 ms entry is a datasheet number exceeding a stated budget; the LTC4015 and RAA489110 entries are also conditional hazards.; [D690] section 15.1 gate T-3.VRECHG ('CE toggled after a previous burst') conflates two datasheet states: a /CE toggle after TERMINATION is documented to start a new cycle (SLUSFN4A p.28), whereas the regen case is a /CE HIGH interruption of an unterminated cycle, which the datasheet does not address. The gate should record both start states separately or the pass result from the terminated state will be over-read.; The affected band is stated as 'about 8.0-8.15 V' (target line 686) but the quantity that matters is the pack voltage seen at SRN behind D-BT-SQ at the /CE edge; the target never states whether SRN sits at pack OCV or at a decayed VCHG level between bursts (the dossier's keep-alive question, [D690] lines 462-467), which decides whether the VRECHG gate or the trickle/pre-charge phases apply.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): The verifier is right that SLUSFN4A p.28 also documents a /CE toggle after termination starting a new cycle, so the datasheet does not establish a no-start above VRECHG; the fact is correctly U/B and my 'documented HR1 failure' exposure does not exist on the record. My affected-band figure also understated the POR case (37.8 % of the voltage window at 8.022 V; 25.2 % at 8.148 V; a voltage-window fraction, not SoC). No VRECHG code >= 8.4 V exists, so 'firmware-side test' is an unsupported characterisation (T-3.VRECHG is a bench gate), and the BQ25690 lead is already gated on it, so no ordering changes. Minor; the three edits accepted; the optional [CRIT] M9 note closing the rule-set gap (a bench no-start on inhibit exit at any pack voltage in 7.4-8.4 V counts as a documented restart failure) is worth adopting.

### CIS-R1-S07 (sweep; reviewer major; final minor; accepted)

- Location: charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md Table 6.0b HR2 row (line 850); section 5.2 lines 628-630; Table 8.1 R-FC773 (line 1084)
- Claim: HR2: BQ25798 'P / sys; FC charge 1.24-1.75 A at 8.4 V' (no H5 annotation); BQ25690 '(H5 F at typical ...)'; BQ25773 '1.35-1.77 A ... (H5 F at the low corner)'. R-FC773: 'BQ25773 FC ceiling position: no setting meets both H5 at the low corner and the 1.20 A arm drop at the high corner ... Needed only if the BQ25773 is adopted'.
- Problem: H5 requires >= 1.5 A at 8.4 V ([EVAL] line 290). [D798] section 11.4 gives the BQ25798 1.24 A at its 0.71 A ceiling minimum, a miss of 0.26 A. That is a larger miss than the BQ25773's 1.35 A (-0.15 A), which the document marks 'H5 F at the low corner'. The BQ25798's nominal figure is 1.50 A, exactly at the limit ([EVAL] line 605: 'H5's 1.5 A holds at nominal only'). The BQ25690's typical 1.46 A is marked F. [D798] line 161 also grades the BQ25798 H5 as P although its CV error of +0.65 % exceeds the H5 limit of +-0.5 %. The same trade-off applies to the baseline. Meeting H5 at the low corner needs a ceiling minimum of 1.5 x 8.4 / (15.6 x 0.94) = 0.859 A. With the BQ25798 band ratio of 1.00 / 0.71 = 1.41, the ceiling maximum is then 1.21 A. Adding the 0.15 A aux load gives a bus draw of 1.36 A, above the 1.20 A arm drop and the 1.247 A P_MAX. So no BQ25798 ceiling position meets both conditions either, and R-FC773 applies to the baseline. Exposure: the operator would decide differently. The ruling is filed as challenger-only, and a hazard shared by both parts counts against the BQ25773 in the rule-of-succession reasons.
- Reviewer recommendation: Grade H5 on one basis for every part (low corner, nominal or typical) and add the BQ25798 H5 result to Table 6.0b. Recast R-FC773 as a general FC-ceiling ruling that applies to the BQ25798, or state why the baseline is exempt. Correct the BQ25798 H5 'P' for the +0.65 % CV error, or relax the H5 CV wording for every part.
- Verdict: PARTLY_CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: Confirmed parts. (1) Asymmetric H5 annotation: target line 850 (Table 6.0b HR2) gives the BQ25798 "P / sys; FC charge 1.24-1.75 A at 8.4 V" with no H5 code, while the BQ25690 carries "(H5 F at typical)" and the BQ25773 "(H5 F at the low corner)"; the same row is copied from [SCO] scoring.md:165. H5 is ">= 1.5 A at 8.4 V ... CV 8.400 V +-0.5 % or better" ([EVAL] charger_replacement_evaluation_2026-11-05.md:290), kept unchanged for the CCB ([CRIT] screening_criteria.md:49). (2) The BQ25798 low-corner figure 1.24 A comes from [D798] dossier_BQ25798.md:405-413 (FC ceiling 0.71 A minimum, 15.6 V, 94 %); the 0.71-1.00 A band is the REGN-divider FC leg of [D798] 7.2 (lines 253-260) and is reproduced by the repo script calc/bq798_ccb.py ("FC ceiling A: 0.709-1.003 nominal 0.855"). The BQ25773 low corner is 1.35 A ([D773] dossier_BQ25773_SMBus_twin_BQ25770G.md:550). So the baseline's worst-case H5 miss (0.26 A) exceeds the challenger's (0.15 A) that the table flags. (3) [D798]:161 grades H5 "P (CV note)" while [D773]:155 grades H5 "C typical / F worst case" on the same kind of band analysis; [EVAL]:605 and :160 already state for the baseline that "H5's 1.5 A holds at nominal only". (4) The ceiling-position trade-off of [D773] 11.4 (lines 563-569) applies to the baseline's REGN-divider FC leg: recomputed below, no position meets H5 at the low corner and the 1.20 A arm drop at the high corner. [D798] 7.2 item 1 (lines 264-269) leaves the FC leg's construction open ("Keep the [EVAL] FC band, or bias both legs from the reference option of item 3") and never computes the trade-off; R-FC773 is filed "Needed only if the BQ25773 is adopted" (target line 1084; [SCO]:534). The same window test also fails for the BQ25690 band ([D690] dossier_BQ25690.md:600-616, 0.747-0.997 A with R_FC 3.83 kOhm). Refuted parts. (a) The CV sub-claim is not an asymmetry: the BQ25773 CV is -0.5/+0.6 % ([D773]:124) and its H5 row (:155) likewise does not fail it on CV; the scoring applies the +-0.5 % anchor symmetrically, S8 = 1 for both the BQ25798 ([D798]:591, target line 876) and the BQ25773 ([D773]:746), S8 = 2 for the BQ25690 whose CV is +-0.5 % ([D690]:136). (b) The finding's exposure claim that "a hazard shared by both parts counts against the BQ25773 in the rule-of-succession reasons" is not what the target says: target lines 69-71 (reason 2) and 977-980 (rule of succession) cite the BQ25773's 1.39 A bus draw after an IIN_HOST loss, which is a real differentiator at the chosen nominals (BQ25798 1.15 A, BQ25690 1.10 A, target line 853) and follows from the BQ25773's wider +-0.24 A silicon band ([D773]:565) versus the divider-tolerance bands of the other two (0.29 A, 0.25 A). Only [SCO]:424-425 appends the shared "no ceiling position meets both H5 and the arm drop" clause to that reason; the target does not. (c) No scored criterion depends on the delivered FC current (S8 scores step size, CV, termination, published accuracy: [CRIT]:104), so the scores and order are unaffected.
- Quantification: H5 ceiling minimum for the BQ25798 basis: 1.5 x 8.4 / (15.6 x 0.94) = 12.6 / 14.664 = 0.859 A. Arm-drop ceiling maximum: 1.20 - 0.15 = 1.05 A. Window 0.191 A. BQ25798 REGN-divider FC band reproduced from [D798] 7.2 inputs (REGN 4.8-5.2 V, 1 % resistors, 1.5 uA leakage x 18.5 kOhm Thevenin): 0.709-1.003 A, width 0.29 A > 0.191 A. Repositioning the nominal so the low corner is 0.859 A (pin scale 1.0751, nominal 1.014 A) gives a high corner of 1.170 A and a bus draw of 1.32 A, above the 1.20 A arm drop and the 1.247 A P_MAX (the finding's ratio method, 0.859 x 1.00/0.71 = 1.21 A, bus 1.36 A, reaches the same conclusion). Charges at 8.4 V: 0.71 A -> 1.239 A, 0.86 A -> 1.501 A, 1.00 A -> 1.746 A (matches the table). BQ25773 low corner: 0.76 x 15.6 x 0.955 / 8.4 = 1.348 A (miss 0.15 A); BQ25798 miss 0.26 A. BQ25690 (R_FC 3.83 kOhm): needed 12.6 / 0.949 / 15.57 = 0.853 A, window to 1.05 A = 0.197 A, band width 0.997 - 0.747 = 0.25 A, so no position there either. A reference-biased FC leg (the [D798] 7.2 item 3 construction, about +-0.8 % plus 7.5 mV, band width about 0.05 A) would nominally admit a position, but only on ideal Eq.1 with the pin-to-current accuracy unspecified (B, S1-B6); if an IINDPM-class -12 % error applied the low corner at a 0.95 A nominal falls to 0.84 A and 1.46 A, below H5 again. Not computed in any working document. CV: BQ25798 +0.65 % and BQ25773 +0.6 % both exceed +-0.5 %; both already scored S8 = 1. Score impact of the finding: 0 points; order unchanged.
- Source: [EVAL] section 1.1 (H5 definition, line 290), 3.4 (lines 604-606, arm drop and FC band basis); [D798] sections 7.2 and 11.4 and calc/bq798_ccb.py (band reproduced); [D773] section 11.4; [D690] section 11.4; [CRIT] S8 definition; [SCO] Tables 5 and 10. No datasheet fetch needed: every disputed figure is a derived quantity from inputs the working documents already cite, and the dispute is about grading symmetry, not a datasheet value.
- Severity rationale: No published number is wrong (1.24-1.75 A reproduces); the defect is an inconsistent grading label and a ruling filed with too narrow a scope. Bound: the baseline's worst-case FC charge at the ceiling minimum is 1.24 A, 17 % below H5, with no hardware exposure (its arm-drop margin 0.05 A is unchanged, no kill path, no loop). Reachability of a decision change: none in the scores (S8 already symmetric; delivered FC current is unscored), none in the rule of succession (reason 1 rests on the BQ25773's 1.39 A, which is genuinely part-specific at the chosen nominals). The operator exposure is an unlisted ruling for the recommended part (FC-leg position or reference bias), which [D798] 7.2 item 1 leaves open and [EVAL] 605 already discloses as "holds at nominal only"; that is document drift, not a decision the evidence would reverse.
- Minimal fix: Three edits to charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md. (1) Table 6.0b HR2 row, BQ25798 cell (line 850): replace "P / sys; FC charge 1.24-1.75 A at 8.4 V" with "P / sys; FC charge 1.24-1.75 A at 8.4 V, nominal 1.50 A (H5 F at the low corner with the REGN-divider FC leg; a reference-biased FC leg is not computed in [D798])". Proof: 0.71 x 15.6 x 0.94 / 8.4 = 1.239 A < 1.5 A ([D798] 11.4). (2) Section 5.2 bullet (lines 628-630): after "No ceiling position meets both H5 at the low corner and the arm drop at the high corner" add "; the same holds for the BQ25798 REGN-divider FC leg (band 0.29 A against a 0.19 A window) and the BQ25690 (0.25 A against 0.20 A); the BQ25773 differs in the width of its band (0.48 A, a pin accuracy specification) and therefore in its bus draw at the chosen nominal (1.39 A against 1.15 A and 1.10 A)". Proof: window 1.05 - 0.859 = 0.191 A; bands from [D798] 7.2, [D690] 7.2, [D773] 7.3. (3) Table 8.1 (line 1084): rename R-FC773 to R-FCCEIL and reword: "FC ceiling position for every TI finalist: no setting of a divider-tolerance or pin-accuracy band meets both H5 at the low corner (ceiling minimum 0.85-0.86 A) and the 1.20 A arm drop at the high corner (ceiling maximum 1.05 A); for the BQ25798 the choice is the REGN-divider FC leg (H5 F at 1.24 A worst case) or a reference-biased FC leg (band not computed, pin accuracy B until S1-B6). Needed for the adopted part." Mirror the same three changes in [SCO] scoring.md lines 165, 424-425 and 534. Leave [D798] line 161 and the CV wording unchanged: the CV clause is applied the same way to the BQ25798 and the BQ25773 in both the H5 rows and S8. Fix class: documentation (no test, pinmap, protocol, coefficient or bench follow-on); it adds one line to the operator rulings list.
- Adjacent findings: [D798] 7.2 item 1 leaves the scored build's FC leg undefined ('Keep the [EVAL] FC band, or bias both legs'): Table 6.0b uses the REGN-divider FC band (1.15 A bus draw, 1.24-1.75 A charge) while the same build takes the reference-biased REGEN ceiling; the FC-leg construction should be fixed in [SCO] Table 4 so the HR4 FC row and the HR2 row rest on one network.; [SCO] scoring.md:424-425 attaches the shared 'no ceiling position meets both H5 and the arm drop' clause to the BQ25690-over-BQ25773 reason, while the target (line 978) drops it; the two documents disagree on the wording of rule-of-succession reason 1.; The three TI FC-charge figures use three different efficiency and bus-voltage bases (BQ25798 15.6 V / 0.94; BQ25690 15.57 V / 0.949; BQ25773 15.6 V / 0.955), each a figure reading; a one-basis comparison would move the figures by up to about 1.6 %, below the H5 miss but worth a footnote in Table 6.0b.; [D798] 7.2 item 1 states a 0.047 A margin to the arm drop but Table 6.0b line 853 rounds it to 0.05 A and labels the bound 'C nominal' with the pin-to-current accuracy unspecified; an IINDPM-class error of the magnitude the dossier itself entertains (-12 % at 1 A) would be a 0.12 A shift, larger than the margin in either direction. Already flagged in the dossier as S1-B6, not restated in the target.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): The published numbers reproduce (1.24-1.75 A; H5 window 0.859-1.05 A = 0.191 A against a 0.29 A REGN-divider band, 0.25 A for the BQ25690, 0.48 A for the BQ25773) and the verifier's repositioning check (high corner 1.170 A, bus 1.32 A) reaches my conclusion that no BQ25798 REGN-divider position meets both H5 and the arm drop either. But the defect is a grading label ('H5 F' marked on two parts, not the third) and a ruling filed too narrowly; delivered FC current is unscored, S8 is already symmetric on the CV clause, and succession reason 1 rests on the BQ25773's 1.39 A bus draw at its chosen nominal, which is genuinely part-specific. The reference-biased FC leg is uncomputed in any working document and B until S1-B6. Minor; the three edits accepted, R-FC773 renamed R-FCCEIL for every TI finalist.

### CIS-R1-S08 (sweep; reviewer minor; final major; accepted)

- Location: Table 6.1 S4 row (line 872), Table 6.2 LTC4015 row (line 893), Table 6.5 LTC4015 column and statement 3 (line 966)
- Claim: LTC4015 B: S4 threshold quality 3 (P), 8.00 points; Table 6.5 B1: LTC4015 49.8 (+10.7); statement 3: 'With the weights set to 0 (B1) the BQ25690 and the LTC4015 pass 10 points'.
- Problem: [D4015] line 151 grades H4 as 'P range / B warm, taper, hold draw' and adds 'full-scale accuracy only; taper 1.2 -> 1.15 V typical only'. [D4015] line 282 gives the regen zero-current point as 18.13 V nominal and 17.92 V worst low against an 18.92 V setting. That is -4.2 % from the setpoint and below the 18.5 V bottom of the M8/S4 band, so the zero-current threshold does not meet the S4 anchor-2 accuracy (<= +-2 % at 18-19 V) on P evidence. The taper is B evidence, and section 1.4 caps B at 2. With S4 = 2 the LTC4015 B total is 46.0 (+5.7, not +8.3). B1 becomes (46.333 - 2.667 - 2.333) x 100/93 = 44.4 against 39.1, a margin of +5.4 rather than +10.7, which makes statement 3 false for the LTC4015. A1, A2 and A4 fall from +13.3 to +10.7. The ranking does not change, because the LTC4015 already fails condition 1, but the published totals and the sensitivity statements are wrong.
- Reviewer recommendation: Cap LTC4015 S4 at 2 (B) unless a primary publishes the taper accuracy. Alternatively, state in [SCO] why the full-current point and not the zero-current point is the scored threshold, and apply that rule to every part. Recompute Tables 6.2 and 6.5 and statements 2-3 of section 6.4.
- Verdict: PARTLY_CONFIRMED, severity major
- Evidence: The premise holds. The arithmetic for one number is wrong.

1. The criteria define the threshold as the zero-current point. [CRIT] screening_criteria.md:72 (M8, the H4 parent) reads: "The loop regulates input current to zero below the threshold". [CRIT]:100 S4 anchor 2 requires accuracy <= +-2 % at 18-19 V, and anchor 3 adds "As 2". [CRIT]:117-123 caps B evidence at 2.

2. The LTC4015 publishes accuracy only for the full-current point. dossier_LTC4015.md:278 gives 1188/1200/1212 mV at code 0xFF ([DS] p.4). The zero-current point is 1.15 V "typical only" (dossier:280, [DS] p.13 pin text: "at 1.15V the charge current is zero"). dossier:151 grades H4 as "P range / B warm, taper, hold draw" and adds "full-scale accuracy only; taper 1.2 -> 1.15 V typical only". dossier:282 gives the zero-current point as 18.13 V nominal and 17.92 V worst low, and labels it (C). dossier:682 lists the taper width as "not in [DS]".

3. The LTC4015 scores were not adjusted for this. dossier:757 scores S4 = 3 (P) with no basis text, and [SCO] scoring.md:300 and the target at line 872 copy it.

4. The BQ25798 baseline uses an integrating VINDPM loop (dossier_BQ25798.md:587: "100 mV, +-2 % at 18.6 V"), so its regulation point and its zero-current point are the same. The LTC4015 UVCL is a proportional loop with a band of about 4 %.

5. The finding's B1 figure is wrong. It computes (46.333 - 2.667 - 2.333) x 100/93 = 44.4. The correct S12 + S13 terms are 1.67 + 0.67. score.py, re-run with S4 = 2, gives 46.95 against 39.07, a margin of +7.89, not +5.4. Statement 3 is still false for the LTC4015 (+7.9 < 10).

6. The finding's A1, A2 and A4 values (+13.3 -> +10.7) are correct. Statement 2 stays true in substance, but its number changes.
- Quantification: Taper (DS p.13, typical) at k = 15.766:
- Zero-current point: 1.15 x 15.766 = 18.13 V.
- Full-current point: 1.2 x 15.766 = 18.92 V.
- Offset: 18.13 / 18.92 = 0.958, which is -4.2 % (nominal).
- Worst low: 18.13 x (1 - 0.012) = 17.92 V. This is a C extrapolation, because the taper has no published tolerance.
- 17.92 V and 18.13 V both lie below the 18.5 V M8 regen floor.

Re-run: score.py was copied to scratchpad/advrev/score_s08.py with S4 = 3 -> 2 for LTC4015-A and LTC4015-B, and run with .venv_hil python -I.

| Scenario | Published | Recomputed |

| A3 | 53.67 (+8.33) | 51.00 (+5.67) |
| A4 | 53.67 (+13.33) | 51.00 (+10.67) |

| D1, D2, D3, E1 | 48.67 (+8.33) | 46.00 (+5.67) |

The finding's own B1 (44.4, +5.4) is an arithmetic error; the correct value is 46.95 (+7.89). Rank order is unchanged: LTC4015 B stays fourth, below BQ25773 49.0 and BQ25690 48.3.
- Source: Sources, highest authority first:
- Primary datasheet LTC4015 (4015fb) p.4 (UVCL accuracy, full-current point only) and p.13 (1.15 V zero-current point, typical, no limits), as quoted in dossier_LTC4015.md:278-282.
- [CRIT] screening_criteria.md:72 (M8 threshold definition), :100 (S4 anchors) and :117-123 (evidence cap).
- calc/score.py, re-run with the one changed score.
- Severity rationale: This is the exposure class "silently wrong published numbers".

The LTC4015 B total moves from 48.7 to 46.0 (-2.67 points), and the published margin moves from +8.3 to +5.7. These figures appear in the summary table (line 60), Table 6.2, Table 6.3 and every LTC4015 cell of Table 6.5.

One published sensitivity statement becomes false. Statement 3 says the LTC4015 passes 10 points in B1, but the recomputed margin is +7.9.

The finding does not reach the other exposure classes. Ranking is unchanged: the LTC4015 was already fourth and already fails decision-rule condition 1 (M3 F, M5 c). The recommendation (keep the BQ25798) and the shortlist order are therefore unaffected. No kill path or loop is involved.

The severity is major, not critical, because the decision does not change.

Proof: the score.py re-run in scratchpad/advrev/s08.txt produces every value listed above.

The alternative (scoring the full-current point) is not supported. [CRIT] M8 defines the threshold as the point below which input current is zero.

No firmware follow-on skill applies; this is a documentation and scoring regeneration only.
- Adjacent findings: The LTC4015 M8 regen band is not met at the chosen divider. The UVCL register cannot raise the threshold above code 0xFF (dossier:286), and at k = 15.766 the zero-current point is 18.13 V nominal, below the 18.5 V M8 floor. A divider with k >= 16.09 (1.15 x 16.09 = 18.5 V) would meet the floor, and at 22.28 V the pin would sit at 1.385 V, still under the 2 V clamp-engage point. The dossier records M8 as conditional and does not state this point.; The FC-mode zero-current point at code 189 is unpublished. The 14.04 V figure is the full-current point, and the taper below 0xFF is typical-only (dossier:682). The FC threshold therefore sits about 0.6 V lower if the 4 % taper scales with the code. This is unverified (TODO(verify: 4015fb)).; The finding's own B1 recomputation is wrong. It gives 44.4 (+5.4); the correct value is 46.95 (+7.9). Its arithmetic subtracts 2.667 + 2.333 instead of the S12 + S13 terms 1.67 + 0.67.
- Counter-review: REFINE (major): I accept the upgrade to major and the correction of my own B1 arithmetic (46.95 / +7.89, not 44.4 / +5.4). The zero-current point 1.15 x 15.766 = 18.13 V (worst low 17.92 V, C extrapolation) is -4.2 % from the setpoint and below the 18.5 V M8 floor, the taper is typical-only (B), and [CRIT] M8 defines the threshold as the zero-input-current point, so S4 = 3 (P) is not supportable; the alternative of scoring the full-current point is not. The scratch re-run moves LTC4015 B from 48.7 (+8.3) to 46.0 (+5.7) in the summary table, Tables 6.2, 6.3 and every Table 6.5 cell, drops A1/A2/A4 from +13.3 to +10.7, and makes statement 3 false (B1 +7.9, not above 10). Silently wrong published numbers in the headline table are the major class; not critical because the LTC4015 is fourth and fails condition 1 regardless, so the recommendation and order are unchanged. The ten edits plus [SCO] Tables 10/11/13, dossier_LTC4015 16.1 and the score.py LTC4015-A/B rows accepted; this is the second finding requiring a score.py re-run.

### CIS-R1-S09 (sweep; reviewer minor; final minor; accepted)

- Location: section 2.5 line 305 (and [CRIT] section 4.5 row 'Cruise decay of VCHG-IN')
- Claim: 'the cruise decay of VCHG-IN below the RT1987 UVLO in 0.17-0.78 s'
- Problem: The cited source, [EVAL] lines 92-93, says that 'In cruise, VCHG decays below the RT1987 UVLO in 0.17-0.78 s'. VCHG is the charger output and the input of D-BT-SQ ([EVAL] P1: 2.5 V is below the RT1987 VIN UVLO, so every burst could pay tD_ON 8 ms). It is not VCHG-IN. Mislabelling the node does two things. It lists as a low-input state, where the buck 'hardware no-charge floor' argument applies, a decay that happens on the output side. It also contradicts section 5.6, which holds VCHG-IN at bus level through Run. The real cruise effect is the D-BT-SQ re-turn-on of 8 ms per burst, against the 4 ms warm budget, and it applies to every part. The document hides that by placing the decay on the input node.
- Reviewer recommendation: Rename the state to the VCHG (D-BT-SQ input) decay. Move it out of the buck-floor argument and cross-reference P1, the keep-alive and T-B-09 as an HR1 burst-entry cost shared by every part.
- Verdict: PARTLY_CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: The node mislabel is real. [EVAL] lines 92-93 (charger_replacement_evaluation_2026-11-05.md) say "VCHG decays below the RT1987 UVLO in 0.17-0.78 s" in regen-armed cruise. The wiring design (charger_bq25798_wiring_design_2026-11-05.md lines 55-56) says the same, and it ties the decay to the BQ25798 battery-short threshold. That threshold is a battery-pin quantity, so the node is VCHG, the D-BT-SQ input, as the finding states. dossier_BQ25798.md line 312-313 states "The D-BT-SQ UVLO drop-out between bursts ... VCHG decays below the RT1987 UVLO in 0.17-0.78 s", with the next burst paying tD_ON 8 ms + tON about 1.9 ms. The target nonetheless writes "VCHG-IN" at line 305 of charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md, and [CRIT] section 4.5 (screening_criteria.md line 230) repeats it as "Cruise decay of VCHG-IN ... Stops below about 10.9 V", citing "EVAL 0.1a". [EVAL] 0.1a is the round-3 wiring-design heading; the sentence itself is at [EVAL] lines 92-93 under the section 0.x summary. The contradiction with target section 5.6 is real. Target lines 678-679 and 781 state that D-BC-RG holds VCHG-IN at bus level through Run, so no part pays a cold re-entry per purge in Run.

Two parts of the finding do not hold. First, "the document hides the cost": the P1 / D-BT-SQ burst-entry cost is not hidden. scoring.md line 271 has a per-part row "P1: BAT held near 2.5 V with /CE HIGH behind D-BT-SQ". The target carries H7 D-BT-SQ and S1-B7 (the 2.5 V hold bench test), and keep-alive terms appear in S6 and in the RAA489110 build. Second, the buck-floor argument is not invalidated. The target's section 2.5 conclusion that every low-input state forbids charging still holds for its other four states. The mislabelled row only inflates the list; the "buck stops below about 10.9 V" cell is vacuous for a 2.5 V output-side node and is the only substantive wrong cell. One ambiguity is not resolved by the sources: VCHG-IN itself also decays when REGEN is open in non-regen cruise. Target line 734 puts the 70 uF VCHG-IN fall at 40-70 V/s, giving roughly 0.16-0.28 s from 16 V to about 5 V. That is a different node and mechanism from the 0.17-0.78 s figure, which no source ties to VCHG-IN.
- Quantification: Timing figure unchanged: 0.17-0.78 s (quoted verbatim in EVAL 92-93, wiring design 56, dossier 313; not recomputed because no working document carries the derivation). Per-burst cost on the correct node: tD_ON 8 ms + tON about 1.9 ms against the HR1 warm budget of 4 ms. This is typical-only per RT1987 DS-00 p.7. The cost is identical for every part and already lies outside the 4 ms budget. Cross-check of the VCHG-IN alternative: 70 uF at 40-70 V/s from 16 V to about 5 V is 11 V / 70 V/s = 0.16 s to 11 V / 40 V/s = 0.28 s, so it does not reproduce the 0.78 s upper bound.
- Source: Working documents and [EVAL], which rank above the target's prose in the authority order: EVAL lines 92-93, charger_bq25798_wiring_design_2026-11-05.md lines 55-56, dossier_BQ25798.md lines 312-314, scoring.md line 271. The RT1987 UVLO and tD_ON figures were not re-fetched from DS-00.
- Severity rationale: This is a node-label error in prose and in one [CRIT] table row. It has no computed bound or exposure path to a kill, a loop or a wrong score. The D-BT-SQ burst cost is scored per part in scoring.md line 271 and does not depend on the mislabelled row, so the 40.3 vs 49.0/48.3/48.7 ranking is unaffected.
- Minimal fix: Edit charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md line 305. Delete "the cruise decay of VCHG-IN below the RT1987 UVLO in 0.17-0.78 s," from the list. Add a separate sentence after line 307: "In regen-armed cruise, VCHG (the D-BT-SQ input, not VCHG-IN) decays below the RT1987 UVLO in 0.17-0.78 s ([EVAL] section 0.x); the next burst pays tD_ON 8 ms + tON about 1.9 ms against the 4 ms HR1 warm budget for every part (P1, S1-B7, T-B-09). The buck-floor argument does not apply to this node." Make the same change in screening_criteria.md section 4.5: rename the row to "Cruise decay of VCHG (D-BT-SQ input)", change the VCHG-IN column text, and replace the Buck and Buck-boost cells with "not an input-side state; see P1". Change the citation "EVAL 0.1a" to "EVAL 0.x (lines 92-93)". No follow-on skill is required; the edit is documentation only.
- Adjacent findings: The citation label 'EVAL 0.1a' in screening_criteria.md line 230 points at the round-3 wiring-design subsection heading, not at the summary bullet at EVAL lines 92-93 where the sentence lives.; VCHG-IN decay in non-regen cruise (REGEN open, 70 uF at 40-70 V/s per target line 734) is a separate low-input case that section 2.5 does not list; it would give roughly 0.16-0.28 s to a 5 V floor by my arithmetic, which is not the figure quoted.; The 0.17-0.78 s range has no derivation in any working document or calc script that I found; it is traced only to 'H.9' of the wiring design, which was not re-derived here.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): Node-label error confirmed: [EVAL] 92-93 places the 0.17-0.78 s decay on VCHG (the D-BT-SQ input), not VCHG-IN, and the VCHG-IN alternative (70 uF at 40-70 V/s: 0.16-0.28 s) does not reproduce the 0.78 s bound. The real per-burst cost is tD_ON 8 ms + tON about 1.9 ms for every part, scored at scoring.md :271 independently of the mislabelled row. Minor; the :305/:307 edit and the [CRIT] 4.5 row rename accepted.

### CIS-R1-S10 (sweep; reviewer minor; final minor; accepted)

- Location: Table 2.4 BQ25690 row and the paragraph after it (lines 344, 349)
- Claim: BQ25690 'Q1 + Q4 = 74 mOhm typical (37 + 37 mOhm; 134 mOhm at the maximum), plus the external BATFET (3.7 mOhm class), the shunts and DCR'; 'Every finalist with a stated resistance stays inside the 100 mOhm assumption at the typical values.'
- Problem: The BQ25690 buck path also carries RAC_SNS of 10 mOhm and RBAT_SNS of 10 mOhm ([D690] lines 79, 548, 666). At typical values the sum is 37 + 37 + 3.7 + 10 + 10 = 97.7 mOhm before the inductor DCR. Any DCR above 2.3 mOhm, which a 10 uH inductor such as the SRP5050FA-100M of the efficiency figure ([D690] section 11.2) exceeds, takes the path past 100 mOhm. At the maximum RDS(on), Q1 + Q4 alone is 134 mOhm. The sentence is therefore false for the BQ25690. There is no feasibility consequence, because the part is a buck-boost, but the published check is wrong.
- Reviewer recommendation: Add the two shunts and an inductor DCR value to the BQ25690 row. State that the BQ25690 exceeds the 100 mOhm assumption, and that this does not matter for a buck-boost.
- Verdict: CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: Target line 344 (Table 2.4, BQ25690 row) lists "Q1 + Q4 = 74 mOhm typical (37 + 37 mOhm; 134 mOhm at the maximum), plus the external BATFET (3.7 mOhm class), the shunts and DCR". Line 349 then states "Every finalist with a stated resistance stays inside the 100 mOhm assumption at the typical values." The BQ25690 dossier (docs/reviews/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10/dossier_BQ25690.md) gives the series elements. Line 79: RAC_SNS 10 mOhm sits in the input path (ACP -> ACN -> VIN). Line 84 and line 667: RBAT_SNS 10 mOhm sits on SRP -> SRN. Line 665: the BATFET is a TPN3R704PL, 3.7 mOhm. Lines 579-580: RDS(on) is 37/77/77/37 mOhm typical and 67/140 mOhm maximum, with Q4 continuously on in buck mode. Line 566: the efficiency figure uses a 10 uH SRP5050FA-100M. The CRIT definition (screening_criteria.md lines 196-197) counts the high-side FET, the inductor DCR, the sense resistor and any series BATFET in R_internal. The target applies the same accounting to the BQ25798, whose 60 mOhm figure includes the internal input sense RSNS of 8 mOhm (target lines 264-266; dossier_BQ25798.md lines 53-59). Excluding RAC_SNS for the BQ25690 would therefore be inconsistent with the document's own BQ25798 row. Even if RAC_SNS is excluded, the claim still fails once a realistic DCR is added.
- Quantification: Typical, with both shunts: 37 + 37 + 3.7 + 10 + 10 = 97.7 mOhm before DCR. Margin to 100 mOhm = 2.3 mOhm of DCR. With the 10 mOhm DCR the document assumes for the BQ25798, the sum is 107.7 mOhm, above 100. With RAC_SNS excluded (it carries input current, not charge current), the sum is 87.7 + 10 = 97.7 mOhm. That exceeds 100 mOhm for any DCR above 12.3 mOhm. A 10 uH SRP5050FA-class part is in the tens of mOhm (value TODO(verify: Bourns SRP5050FA datasheet)). At maximum RDS(on), Q1 + Q4 = 67 + 67 = 134 mOhm, already above 100 mOhm without any other element. Consequence check (worst case): R = 134 + 3.7 + 20 + 74 (assumed DCR) = about 232 mOhm. Required VIN = (8.484 + 3.21 x 0.232) / 0.95 = 9.71 V, below the 10.88 V buck feasibility floor. The BQ25690 is also a buck-boost, so buck headroom does not constrain it. The ranking and the recommendation are unchanged.
- Source: Working documents: dossier_BQ25690.md lines 79, 84, 566, 579-580, 665-667; screening_criteria.md section 4.3 (R_internal definition); dossier_BQ25798.md lines 53-59 (the RSNS-inclusive precedent). The primary datasheet (SLUSFN4A) was not re-fetched; the dossier cites DS section 5.5 p.10 for RDS(on) and Table 7-2 p.64 for the BATFET.
- Severity rationale: This is a wrong published check statement. Exposure path: a reader who relies on line 349 for a buck-only use of the BQ25690 would under-count about 0.03-0.4 V of drop. However, the computed worst-case headroom (9.71 V) stays below the 10.88 V floor. The part is a buck-boost, so no feasibility result, score, ranking or recommendation changes. It is not a silently wrong number that drives a decision, so it caps at minor.
- Minimal fix: Section 2.5, Table 2.4, BQ25690 row, buck-path cell: replace it with "Q1 + Q4 = 74 mOhm typical (134 mOhm maximum) + BATFET 3.7 mOhm + RBAT_SNS 10 mOhm + RAC_SNS 10 mOhm = 97.7 mOhm typical before DCR; 107.7 mOhm with an assumed 10 mOhm DCR; above 100 mOhm. Not limiting: buck-boost; (8.484 + 3.21 x 0.232) / 0.95 = 9.71 V at the maximum values with 74 mOhm DCR". Line 349: replace "Every finalist with a stated resistance stays inside the 100 mOhm assumption at the typical values." with "The BQ25798 and the LTC4015 stay inside the 100 mOhm assumption at the typical values. The BQ25690 exceeds it (97.7 mOhm before DCR) but is a buck-boost, and its worst-case required input of 9.71 V stays below the 10.88 V floor." Proof: the arithmetic in QUANTIFICATION, from the cited dossier lines. Follow-on skill: none (doc-only); the SRP5050FA DCR needs a TODO(verify: Bourns datasheet).
- Adjacent findings: The R_internal accounting is inconsistent across rows. The BQ25798 includes the input-side RSNS, while the LTC4015 row includes RSNSI 20 mOhm (input) and INFET. The document never says whether input-side elements should be weighted by I_in (about D x I_chg) or by I_chg. State one convention in section 2.3.; The BQ25773 row ('RAC 10 mOhm', 'about 10 mOhm' BATFET+RSR) gives no external FET RDS(on) or DCR. Its inclusion in the 'stated resistance' sentence is undefined, so the sentence cannot be checked for that part either.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): Confirmed: 37 + 37 + 3.7 + 10 + 10 = 97.7 mOhm before DCR (107.7 mOhm with the 10 mOhm DCR the document assumes for the BQ25798; 134 mOhm from Q1 + Q4 alone at maximum), so the 'inside 100 mOhm at typical values' sentence is false for the BQ25690. Worst-case required input 9.71 V stays below the 10.88 V floor and the part is a buck-boost, so no feasibility result changes. Minor; the Table 2.4 cell and :349 replacement accepted, with the SRP5050FA DCR as TODO(verify).

### CIS-R1-S11 (sweep; reviewer minor; final minor; accepted)

- Location: section 3.3 lines 386-388; section 6.6 finding-3 bullet (lines 1028-1029)
- Claim: (a) 'Most rejections rest on a primary-read fact ... Three entries do not: the BQ25822 and LT8705/LT8705A ... and the BQ25820'. (b) 'Finding 3 ... reopens for every part as a CCB copper-sizing task, on a basis current of 3.21 A (BQ25798), 3.36 A (BQ25773) or 3.30 A (LTC4015).'
- Problem: (a) Table 3.1 also has a 'Not scorable' row (MP2769, SY20776/SY20779) whose basis is 'Section 8.3', that is, no primary read. [SCR] row 25 gives SY20776 as 'M2 likely F ... UNVERIFIED'. So five entries lack a primary basis, not three. (b) The bullet says the finding reopens for every part but gives basis currents for three of the five finalists. The BQ25690 and RAA489110 copper-sizing currents are missing, so the task cannot be sized from the document for those two parts.
- Reviewer recommendation: (a) Count the 'Not scorable' row among the entries without a primary basis. (b) Add the BQ25690 and RAA489110 basis currents from their dossiers, or mark them TODO.
- Verdict: PARTLY_CONFIRMED, severity minor
- Evidence: (a) Target lines 386-389 say 'Three entries do not' rest on a primary read: BQ25822, LT8705/LT8705A and BQ25820. Table 3.1 line 406 (the 'Not scorable' row) gives MP2769 (primary behind an MPS login) and SY20776/SY20779 (Silergy pages fail) with the basis 'Section 8.3', which is no primary read. [SCR] screening.md line 86 (row 25) gives SY20776/79 as 'M2 likely F (UNVERIFIED); all other items U; Not scorable'. These rows are disclosed in the same table, but the sentence's count of three is literally incomplete: five entries, or three-plus-two, lack a primary basis. A mitigating point: the sentence is about 'rejections', and Not-scorable parts are not rejected; the table title still says 'Full-screen rejections'. (b) Target lines 1025-1026 say finding 3 reopens 'for every part' and give 3.21 A (BQ25798), 3.36 A (BQ25773) and 3.30 A (LTC4015). scoring.md lines 255-257 carry the same three numbers and no others. dossier_BQ25690.md lines 720-721 only say 'reopens as a copper-sizing task for every CCB' with no current. The RAA489110 dossier has no copper basis current. Its line 494 gives 3.30 A, but that is the charge-side input-ceiling limit (1.589 A x 18.53 V x 0.94 / 8.4 V), not a copper basis. So the BQ25690 and RAA489110 basis currents do not exist in any working document, and the target does not invent them.
- Quantification: Count (a): the sentence names 3 entries (BQ25822, LT8705/LT8705A, BQ25820); Table 3.1 adds 2 entries (MP2769, SY20776/SY20779) with no primary basis, so the count is 5 if Not-scorable rows are included. Count (b): the bullet gives 3 of 5 finalists. For the BQ25690 the ICHG setting is not fixed in the sources; its basis would be the chosen ICHG plus tolerance, at least 3.10 A. The RAA489110 gives no source figure. A basis of at least the 3.21 A image-plus-tolerance floor ([CRIT] line 187) would conservatively cover both, but that is my inference and not a source value.
- Source: Target lines 386-406 and 1025-1026; screening.md line 86; scoring.md lines 255-257; dossier_BQ25690.md lines 720-721; dossier_RAA489110 line 494; screening_criteria.md line 187. Authority order: working documents and the target's prose only. No datasheet is involved.
- Severity rationale: Neither point changes a score, the BQ25798 recommendation or the shortlist order. The Not-scorable parts are disclosed in Table 3.1 and are not finalists. The omitted basis currents affect only a later copper-sizing task, and the reopen applies equally to all parts, so no part is favoured. There is no kill path, no loop and no published number that is wrong. The count sentence is imprecise wording and the bullet is an incompleteness that leaves two finalists' sizing without a number.
- Minimal fix: (a) Section 3.3 lines 386-389: replace 'Three entries do not: the BQ25822 and LT8705/LT8705A rejections are UNVERIFIED (primary not read or blocked), and the BQ25820 is held at M9 U because its decisive figure is illegible.' with 'Three rejected or held entries do not: the BQ25822 and LT8705/LT8705A rejections are UNVERIFIED (primary not read or blocked), and the BQ25820 is held at M9 U because its decisive figure is illegible. The two Not-scorable entries (MP2769, SY20776/SY20779) also have no primary read and are not rejected.' (b) Section 6.6 lines 1025-1026: append 'The BQ25690 and RAA489110 basis currents are not recorded in [SCO] or the dossiers; TODO(verify: set from the chosen ICHG plus tolerance at design time; 3.21 A is the conservative floor).' Docs-only change; no follow-on skill is triggered.
- Adjacent findings: The RAA489110 dossier line 494 figure of 3.30 A is a charge-side input-ceiling limit and is numerically identical to the LTC4015 copper-basis 3.30 A. Readers could confuse them; label them separately.; Table 3.1's title says 'Full-screen rejections' but includes the 'Not scorable' and 'M9 U, held in reserve' rows, which are not rejections.
- Counter-review: CONCEDE (minor): Counts confirmed (5 entries without a primary basis if the two Not-scorable rows are included; 3 of 5 finalists given a copper-sizing basis). The BQ25690 ICHG is not fixed in the sources and the RAA489110 has no source figure, so the fix can only mark them TODO(verify) with the 3.21 A floor as a conservative placeholder. No score or order moves. Minor; both edits accepted.

## 6. Status

The round is review only. No fix has been applied to the target or to any working document. Approved findings proceed as a documentation revision of `docs/charger_ic_search_2026-11-10.md`, `scoring.md` and `calc/score.py`; no firmware is involved.

## 7. Fix application (2026-11-10)

The operator approved every accepted finding. The fixes were applied in the order of the ranked adjustment list (section 4), in twelve sequential editing groups, because all groups edit the same documents. The refuted findings CIS-R1-32 and CIS-R1-S02 were not applied. Thirteen read-only checkers (one per group plus one consistency critic that re-ran `calc/score.py`) raised 32 defects in the applied text: stale copies of replaced values, the LTC4015 margin range after the S4 cap (5.7-8.7 points; Table 6.5 span +2.0 to +13.7), succession-acceptance wording, table captions and cross-references, and sentence length. A repair pass applied 31 of them and partly applied CC-14 (sentence length; the longest sentences were split). The CIS-R1-04 fix item 6 was applied afterwards: `calc/bq798_ccb.py` now reproduces the BQ25798 low-corner charge (2.899 A at a 3.10 A setting; 3.104 A at a 3.32 A setting; input limit 3.178 A) and the FC bound after an IINDPM loss in the sampled-once branch (1.70-1.76 A reference-biased; 1.57-1.96 A REGN divider).

Post-fix totals from `calc/score.py`: BQ25798 40.33, BQ25690 48.33 (+8.00), BQ25773 49.00 (+8.67), LTC4015 design B 46.00 (+5.67), LTC4015 design A 40.67 (+0.33), RAA489110 35.67 (-4.67). The recommendation (retain the BQ25798) and the shortlist order are unchanged.
